# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING — Frozen External Research Report

- **Analytical label:** `EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING`
- **Freeze date / access date:** 2026-08-11
- **Research scope:** independent external history, technical reconstruction, criticism, property extraction and audit-intake preparation.
- **Exclusions:** no repository inspection, target-system mapping, adoption decision, issue proposal or implementation prescription.
- **Ontology warning:** the label is a synthesis construct. The evidence does not establish one historical methodology formally named “Evolved Statistical Engineering.”

## Frozen conclusion

The evidence supports **a discipline of decision-discriminating evidence**, not a demand to “use more statistics” and not a synonym for Six Sigma. Its strongest surviving form begins by fixing the decision and the property to be measured; establishes whether the measurement/evaluator can discriminate the states that matter; preserves sampling, provenance, dependence and missingness; distinguishes process stability, capability and specifications; and chooses the smallest experiment or monitoring design that can separate live alternatives with stated error, stopping, drift and consequence boundaries. Repeated observations count only to the extent that they add independent information rather than repeat a shared bias, unit, seed, batch, evaluator, benchmark or selection mechanism. [S001–S003, S005–S007, S015, S023, S032–S038, S051–S064, S098, S110]

The mature construct is **anti-ceremonial and assumption-explicit**. Control charts, acceptance plans, factorial designs, response surfaces, capability indices, accelerated tests, sequential procedures, gauge studies and branded improvement cycles are conditional instruments. They do not trigger merely because data exist. A direct authoritative or exhaustive deterministic discriminator takes precedence when it already settles the decision; additional measurement must have positive expected decision value. Statistical evidence remains essential where variation, sampling, uncertainty, interaction, time, transfer or rare events prevent deterministic settlement. [S008–S014, S021, S030–S031, S043–S050, S068–S078, S079–S087, S098]

Programme-level evidence is materially weaker than the formal and measurement-science evidence for many component mechanisms. Six Sigma has documented organisational architectures and observational performance associations, but selection, leadership, project choice, resources and publication/measurement choices complicate causal attribution. The frozen synthesis therefore retains transferable properties while treating DMAIC/DMADV names, belts, tollgates and universal sigma rhetoric as optional or ceremonial rather than statistical laws. [S082–S087]

## Research method, adversarial rule and saturation

The research reconstructed multiple lineages rather than forcing a single genealogy: Bell Labs statistical quality control and acceptance sampling; Fisherian experimental design; Neyman–Pearson and Wald decision/sequential theory; physical metrology and measurement assurance; reliability and robust-design practice; management-programme translations; and modern convergent work on adaptive evaluation, benchmark reuse and dataset shift. The NIST/SEMATECH handbook and its detailed source map were used as a present-practice spine, not as authority for every synthesis claim. [S101, S110]

Candidate properties were admitted only after identifying the decision changed, measurement preconditions, sampling/design assumptions, false-positive and false-negative costs, drift exposure, cheaper path, known misuse and contrary evidence. A named method was demoted when the evidence supported only a conditional tool, domain-specific technique, superseded form or programme ceremony. “No general property” was allowed and is represented in P058–P065.

The saturation stop rule was met when every explicit family in the research contract had: (1) historical or technical provenance; (2) a current-practice or standards disposition where applicable; (3) at least one criticism/misuse route; (4) an evolved, narrowed, rejected or unresolved disposition; (5) source-linked measurement and assumption boundaries; and (6) a place in the frozen 65-property denominator. Later searches produced corroboration or narrower locators rather than a new material property family. Twenty scholarly/transfer questions remain explicit without leaving any property undispositioned.

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_TIMELINE

| Period | Development | Lineage disposition | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1919–1924 | Bell Labs industrial quality problems motivate statistical process views; Shewhart’s 16 May 1924 memorandum contains the control-chart sketch. | DIRECT_LINEAGE | S001, S002, S004 |
| 1926 | Shewhart publishes “Quality Control Charts”; Fisher publishes “The Arrangement of Field Experiments.” | PARALLEL_DIRECT_LINEAGES | S001, S005 |
| 1931 | Shewhart’s Economic Control integrates variation discrimination with economic action. | DIRECT_LINEAGE | S002 |
| 1933 | Neyman–Pearson formalise efficient tests with specified alternatives and error probabilities. | SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY | S007 |
| 1935 | Fisher’s Design of Experiments consolidates randomisation, replication, blocking and factorial design. | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE | S006 |
| 1940s | Dodge–Romig acceptance-sampling plans and wartime/industrial standards operationalise producer/consumer lot risk. | DIRECT_LINEAGE | S008, S068 |
| 1946 | Plackett–Burman economical multifactor screening formalises deliberate information/alias trade-offs. | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE | S010 |
| 1947–1948 | Wald and Wald–Wolfowitz establish planned sequential analysis and SPRT optimality under specified conditions. | SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY | S011, S012 |
| 1950 | Deming’s Japanese lectures transmit substantial SPC content; later institutional history must not be reduced to a single-hero story. | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME_TRANSLATION | S088, S092, S093 |
| 1951 | Box–Wilson introduce response-surface sequential optimisation; Juran’s handbook consolidates a broader quality-planning/control tradition. | PARALLEL_LINEAGES | S009, S090 |
| 1954–1956 | Page’s CUSUM and Duncan’s economic chart design sharpen small-shift detection and explicit monitoring-cost trade-offs. | DIRECT_LINEAGE | S013, S014 |
| 1956 | Western Electric handbook popularises supplementary run rules, later exposing sensitivity/false-alarm trade-offs. | TEXTBOOK_AND_PRACTICE_TRANSLATION | S065, S066 |
| 1963 | Eisenhart treats calibration as a measurement process requiring both statistical control and accuracy. | MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE | S015 |
| 1964 | Huber robust estimation and Box–Cox transformation/model diagnostics expand treatment of misspecification. | REACTION_OR_CORRECTION | S016, S017 |
| 1974–1979 | Potential-outcomes causal reasoning, missing-data mechanisms, and Goodhart/Campbell indicator corruption broaden validity and proxy concerns. | SHARED_AND_CONVERGENT | S018, S019, S020, S022 |
| 1980s | Industrial robust design/Taguchi practice spreads; Six Sigma emerges as an organisational programme; Deming/Juran managerial translations become highly visible. | RELIABILITY_AND_MANAGEMENT_TRANSLATION | S089, S090, S094 |
| 1984–1988 | Pseudoreplication, agreement-vs-correlation, autocorrelated SPC and Taguchi critiques expose dependence, measurement and recipe limits. | REACTION_OR_CORRECTION | S023, S024, S025, S026 |
| 1990s | GUM/VIM-style uncertainty and traceability mature; FDR and equivalence/absence-of-evidence corrections refine inferential practice. | MEASUREMENT_AND_STATISTICAL_EVOLUTION | S028, S029, S051, S052 |
| 1990s–2000s | Six Sigma/DM(A)IC and belt systems institutionalise role/project structures; evidence later distinguishes those artefacts from inherited statistical tools. | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME_TRANSLATION | S082, S083, S084 |
| 1999–2001 | SPC literature explicitly documents controversies, autocorrelation and measurement-error effects on chart performance. | REACTION_OR_CORRECTION | S030, S031, S032 |
| 2005–2017 | Steiner–MacKay and Hoerl–Snee use “statistical engineering” at different scopes; ISEA develops the broader complex-problem discipline. | QUALITY_ENGINEERING_GENERALISATION | S079, S080, S081 |
| 2008–2012 | Operations-management studies formalise Six Sigma’s organisational theory and report observational programme-performance associations. | EMPIRICAL_PROGRAMME_EVIDENCE | S082, S085, S086 |
| 2009–2020 | Critical/systematic reviews find heterogeneous and often weak branded-programme evidence in some domains and catalogue recurrent Six Sigma criticisms. | CRITICAL_PROGRAMME_EVIDENCE | S084, S087 |
| 2009–2019 | Dataset-shift science, reusable holdouts and refreshed benchmark studies translate measurement/selection concerns into adaptive digital evaluation. | MODERN_DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | S038, S039, S099 |
| 2011–2021 | Analyst-flexibility evidence and ASA statements narrow p-value claims toward uncertainty, transparency, magnitude, multiplicity and context. | REACTION_OR_CORRECTION | S033, S034, S035, S040, S042 |
| 2021–2022 | Confidence sequences and always-valid online inference supply formal modern options for planned continuous monitoring. | MODERN_EVOLVED_FORM | S036, S037 |
| 2023–2026 | Current ISO/NIST practice continues measurement competence, proficiency, time-dependent capability and revised acceptance-sampling standards. | CURRENT_STANDARDISATION | S064, S069, S105, S110 |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_GENEALOGY

The genealogy is plural. Shared mathematics, later borrowing and retrospective similarity do not by themselves establish direct transmission.

| School / lineage | Documented sequence | Lineage class | Property range | Boundary | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Bell Labs statistical quality control | Shewhart control/economic control → control charts → CUSUM/EWMA/time-series/change-point variants | DIRECT_LINEAGE | P015–P024 | Not equivalent to every dashboard or to specification compliance. | S001–S004, S013–S014, S026, S030–S032 |
| Bell Labs acceptance sampling | Dodge–Romig lot risk → military/industrial plans → ISO 2859 | DIRECT_LINEAGE | P026–P027 | Lot disposition, not process capability or improvement. | S008, S068–S070 |
| Fisherian experimental design | Randomisation/blocking/replication/factorials → industrial DOE/RSM | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE | P029–P035 | Does not automatically solve measurement, interference, transport or stopping. | S005–S006, S009–S010, S043–S050 |
| Neyman–Pearson/Wald decision and sequential theory | Specified alternatives/error control → power/sample planning → sequential/group/anytime-valid inference | SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY | P028, P036–P038 | Model- and loss-dependent; not universal thresholding. | S007, S011–S012, S028, S036–S037 |
| Measurement science/metrology | Calibration/accuracy/uncertainty/traceability → measurement assurance, GR&R, decision rules, proficiency | MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE | P001–P013, P052–P053 | Traceability/repeatability never alone establish validity or fitness. | S015, S051–S060, S100, S103, S105 |
| Reliability and robust design | Life testing/censoring/ALT/growth + parameter/noise-factor design | RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE | P043–P050 | Transfer is strongest when failure/exposure/stress mechanisms are coherent. | S025, S071–S078, S094–S096, S102, S104 |
| Deming/Juran/Japanese quality institutions | Statistical content plus management/planning translations and institutional uptake | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME_TRANSLATION | P015, P020, P056–P057 | Documented influence without single-hero or direct causal attribution. | S088–S093 |
| Six Sigma | Inherited statistical/quality tools + distinctive roles, projects, metrics and structured procedures | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME_TRANSLATION | P056, P060–P062 | Not synonymous with statistical engineering; branded causal evidence is heterogeneous. | S082–S087 |
| Broader statistical engineering | Steiner–MacKay manufacturing algorithm and later Hoerl–Snee/ISEA complex-problem integration | QUALITY_ENGINEERING_GENERALISATION | P056–P057 | Emerging discipline; component evidence is stronger than a unique programme effect. | S079–S081 |
| Adaptive digital/ML evaluation | Reusable holdouts, refreshed benchmarks, dataset shift and always-valid A/B methods | MODERN_DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | P009–P012, P037–P039, P049–P054 | Direct statistical ancestry/convergence, not proof of historical industrial lineage. | S036–S040, S099 |
| Proxy corruption/audit effects | Goodhart, Campbell and Strathern | CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING | P051–P052 | Mechanism is compelling under incentives but not a universal theorem for every metric. | S019, S022, S097 |

# STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_VS_SIX_SIGMA_VS_TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

| Construct | What the evidence supports | What it is not | Disposition | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Statistical engineering | System-level integration of statistical and domain methods to solve complex, often unstructured problems; methods are selected around the decision system. | A single fixed method, certification or universal tool sequence. | QUALITY_ENGINEERING_GENERALISATION / modern professional formulation | S079–S081 |
| Six Sigma | A management-programme architecture combining project selection, roles, training, problem-solving stages and established statistical/quality tools. | The historical origin of control charts, DOE, metrology, reliability or every property in this report. | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME_TRANSLATION; effects context-dependent | S082–S087 |
| Statistical toolkit caricature | A menu of charts, tests, capability indices, normality checks and templates applied because data exist. | Decision framing, construct/measurement validation, dependence control, causal design or value-of-information discipline. | CEREMONIAL_IMPLEMENTATION | S030–S035, S042–S044, S110 |
| EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING | The frozen synthesis: decision-linked measurement integrity, variation/dependence modelling, efficient causal/design discrimination, valid stopping, drift/evaluator governance, reliability/tail humility and evidence proportionality. | A claim of one historical branded school or a mandate for formal statistics where deterministic evidence suffices. | RESEARCH CONSTRUCT, externally adjudicable | P001–P057 |

## Caricature corrections

| Caricature | Corrected property-level account | Properties | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| “Statistics means run a significance test.” | Inference is one conditional layer after construct, measurement, sampling/design and decision framing. | P001–P013, P029, P036–P040 | S033–S035, S042 |
| “SPC means put everything on a control chart.” | SPC’s surviving property is calibrated variation discrimination with an actionable response under a suitable process model. | P015–P024, P059 | S001–S003, S030–S032 |
| “Six Sigma means 3.4 DPMO.” | Six Sigma is a management/project architecture using inherited tools; 3.4 DPMO is conditional rhetoric, not a universal property. | P056, P060–P062 | S082–S087 |
| “More samples mean more truth.” | Only valid, sufficiently independent and representative information reduces the relevant uncertainty. | P025, P028, P032, P038, P063 | S023, S028, S038 |
| “Repeated green tests establish the property.” | Repeated exposure can share bias, dependence, selection and optional stopping; deterministic authority or fresh confirmation may dominate. | P010–P012, P032, P038, P054, P063 | S032, S038–S040 |
| “Precise means accurate.” | Precision, bias/trueness, uncertainty and construct validity are separate. | P001–P008 | S015, S024, S052, S057 |
| “Inside specification means controlled.” | Specifications are requirements; control limits describe process behaviour; capability additionally requires stability. | P017–P019 | S053, S062–S064 |
| “Significant means engineering-important.” | Magnitude, uncertainty, equivalence margin and consequence determine action. | P036, P058 | S029, S033–S035, S041 |
| “Randomisation and replication solve validity.” | They address assignment and independent variation, not invalid measurement, spillover, carryover, missingness or transport. | P030–P032, P039 | S018, S020, S023, S047–S048 |
| “A held-out evaluator is objective forever.” | Adaptive reuse, contamination, shift and evaluator drift can erode its evidential value. | P009–P012, P049, P052–P054 | S038–S039, S099 |

The denominator is frozen at **65/65**. It includes retained, evolved, precondition, experimental-design, context-dependent, assumption-sensitive, domain-specific, superseded, ceremonial and rejected candidates. No failed candidate was silently omitted.

| P041 | Choose experiments and measurements by expected information or decision value relative to cost | EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING | CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | S014, S098, S009 | S021, S080 |

| P046 | Treat reliability growth as intervention-conditioned evidence, not an automatically improving trend | ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS | RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE | DOMAIN_SPECIFIC | S078, S071, S077 | S040, S097 |
| P047 | Translate zero observed failures into an upper risk bound, never “perfect reliability” | ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS | RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | S073, S075, S071 | S045, S078 |

| P049 | Test distribution shift and stress contexts; do not equate held-out performance with deployment validity | ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS | MODERN_DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | S099, S039, S038 | S027, S097 |
| P050 | Keep mean performance, variability, tail behaviour, reliability over time and model robustness as separate claims | ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS | QUALITY_ENGINEERING_GENERALISATION | STRONGLY_RETAINED | S062, S071, S074 | S039, S084 |
| P051 | Treat optimised metrics as vulnerable proxies and monitor for gaming/surrogation | PROXY_EVALUATOR_AND_SYSTEM_GOVERNANCE | CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING | USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED | S019, S022, S097 | S038, S039, S040 |

### P001 — Define the decision-relevant measurand or construct before quantification

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P001`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Define the decision-relevant measurand or construct before quantification
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Metrology’s measurand discipline, experimental operationalisation, and later construct-validity/invariance work.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** State what quantity or property is intended, under which conditions, before assigning a number.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A precise, reproducible number can answer the wrong question or represent an unstable proxy.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Construct substitution, category drift, metric availability being mistaken for decision relevance.
- **MECHANISM:** Bind each metric to a stated measurand/construct, unit of analysis, operating conditions, intended use, and disconfirming observations.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Any measurement used to accept, reject, optimise, compare, or monitor an engineering state.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When an authoritative deterministic fact directly answers the decision, record that fact rather than inventing a surrogate score.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Domain definition, decision owner, scope, and an explicit mapping from observations to the property of interest.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** The operational definition must preserve the distinctions the decision actually requires; an evaluator must not collapse relevant states.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The construct is sufficiently coherent for the intended decision and its operationalisation does not change materially across samples.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents expensive optimisation of a convenient but irrelevant number and makes later uncertainty interpretable.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Design authority, test owner, regulator, operator, or other consumer of the decision.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-specifying the construct can delay cheap obvious decisions or disguise normative choices as technical definitions.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Under-definition permits proxy capture, post-hoc relabelling, and false agreement.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Ambiguous labels; changing definitions between baseline and candidate; composite scores whose weights hide trade-offs.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Metrology can define a measurand precisely while still leaving broader validity contested; formal latent constructs add model dependence.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from physical quantity definition toward explicit construct, context and decision-use validation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** No metric governs until its intended property, scope, consumer, and admissible interpretation are fixed enough to be challenged.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A template or ontology is optional; the property is the explicit decision-linked definition.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S052, S055, S056
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S027, S097
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S039, S099
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Construct-validity evidence is domain-dependent; no universal statistical test establishes that a metric means what stakeholders intend.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should construct adequacy be stress-tested when the desired property is partly normative or changes with deployment context?

| --- | --- |

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What exact property is the target system claiming to measure?
- Would two materially different states receive the same score?
- Is an authoritative deterministic discriminator already available?

### P002 — Establish measurement-system fitness before metric governance

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P002`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Establish measurement-system fitness before metric governance
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Eisenhart/NBS calibration-system evaluation, measurement assurance, gauge R&R, GUM and laboratory-quality systems.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Characterise the measurement process before treating its outputs as facts about the production process.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Observed variation may be dominated by instrument, operator, environment, fixture, algorithm, or evaluator variation.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Governing a candidate or process with a measurement system unable to discriminate decision-relevant states.
- **MECHANISM:** Estimate bias, repeatability, reproducibility, resolution, stability and uncertainty relative to the decision margin; block use when discrimination is inadequate.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Metrics near thresholds, comparisons whose expected effect is small, multi-operator/evaluator systems, or costly decisions.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a deterministic binary check with an authoritative oracle and negligible ambiguity, verify the oracle and use it directly rather than perform full MSA.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined measurand, representative items/conditions, reference or comparison basis, and a stated decision margin.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** The study must span the real operating range and relevant operators, instruments, environments and preprocessing paths.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Measurement error is reasonably estimable over the intended range and the study itself has not selected unusually easy samples.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Separates candidate/process variation from evaluator variation and reduces false corrective action.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Anyone relying on a metric, threshold, trend or experiment outcome.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-formal MSA can cost more than the decision and arbitrary percentage rules can reject a perfectly adequate binary discriminator.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Skipping MSA can make improvements, regressions and capability estimates indistinguishable from measurement noise.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Narrow part range; nonrepresentative operators; stable repeatability but unknown bias; passing a generic GR&R percentage unrelated to the decision.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Gauge R&R is only one design; destructive, automated, subjective and probabilistic evaluators require other designs.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from instrument calibration and variance decomposition to decision-specific evaluator capability and versioned validation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Demonstrate enough discrimination for the intended decision, not abstract “good measurement,” and recheck after material evaluator change.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** The form, software package, fixed 10×3×3 design or percentage cutoff is ceremony; decision-linked discrimination is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S015, S055, S058
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S032, S053
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S105, S024
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** No single GR&R threshold is universally optimal; measurement investment depends on decision loss and achievable resolution.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should measurement fitness be quantified for adaptive, generative or partly subjective evaluators?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Decide whether observed process/candidate differences exceed measurement variation.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** True state plus instrument/operator/environment/evaluator error, possibly heteroscedastic or drifting.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Representative range, repeated measures, reference comparisons and version control.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Variance/bias/uncertainty small enough relative to the decision margin.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Unnecessary redesign or rejection caused by measurement noise.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** A real defect hidden by poor resolution or attenuation.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Calibration/evaluator drift can invalidate earlier decomposition.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Changing the candidate to chase evaluator noise; changing the evaluator to erase a candidate failure.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct authoritative check or coarse decision with a very wide margin.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Decision-specific measurement-capability gate with ongoing checks.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Can the target measurement system discriminate the states that change the decision?
- How much observed variation is evaluator rather than candidate variation?
- Would the conclusion survive another competent operator, fixture or evaluator version?

### P003 — Calibration and metrological traceability where decision-relevant

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P003`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Calibration and metrological traceability where decision-relevant
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Physical metrology, calibration hierarchies, VIM/GUM and ISO/IEC 17025.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Relate measurement results to references through a documented calibration chain with stated uncertainty.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A stable instrument can be consistently offset, incomparable across sites, or anchored to an obsolete reference.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False comparability, hidden bias, ungrounded units and calibration labels treated as universal validity.
- **MECHANISM:** Use suitable standards/reference methods, record calibration state and uncertainty contributions, and maintain traceability at the result level.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Physical/chemical measurements, regulated tolerances, interlaboratory exchange, or any decision requiring comparability to a standard.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For purely ordinal/local decisions with a verified internal reference and no cross-context claim, full national/international traceability may add no value.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Valid calibration method, competent execution, suitable reference, environmental controls, and an uncertainty chain.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Calibration points and standards must cover the range, matrix and conditions of use.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The reference hierarchy and calibration model remain applicable; no unmodelled transport or nonlinear effects dominate.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Makes results comparable, exposes bias/uncertainty and supports defensible conformance decisions.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Laboratory, manufacturer, customer, regulator and downstream analysts.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Calibration theatre can consume resources while routine use, sampling or construct definition remains defective.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Absent or stale calibration can produce confident but systematically wrong decisions.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** “NIST-traceable” asserted for an organisation; calibration certificate without uncertainty; traceability outside the working range.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** NIST explicitly rejects treating traceability as a guarantee of fitness for purpose or absence of mistakes.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from periodic instrument adjustment to result-specific traceability plus ongoing measurement assurance.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Require the level of traceability needed for comparability and decision risk, alongside—not instead of—fitness validation.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A certificate, logo or fixed interval is not the property; the evidence chain and decision fitness are.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S052, S054, S056
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S060, S103
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S105
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Some digital/subjective measurements lack a meaningful SI chain; analogous provenance does not equal metrological traceability.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What is the minimum defensible reference chain for nonphysical evaluators and rapidly changing benchmarks?

| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |

- Does the result—not merely the instrument—have a documented reference chain and uncertainty?
- Is cross-site or cross-version comparability actually required for this decision?
- Could a local deterministic reference provide a cheaper adequate path?

### P004 — Keep bias/accuracy distinct from precision

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P004`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Keep bias/accuracy distinct from precision
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Classical measurement error, NBS measurement evaluation, ISO 5725 and VIM.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Separate systematic displacement from random dispersion and estimate each against a reference/use case.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Highly repeatable measurements can be systematically wrong; averaging reduces random error but not shared bias.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False confidence from tight distributions, many decimals, or repeated agreement around the wrong value.
- **MECHANISM:** Compare to suitable references/alternatives for bias while estimating dispersion under repeatability and reproducibility conditions.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Whenever magnitude or direction relative to truth/standard matters, especially near limits.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** If the decision is invariant to a known constant offset and only ranks within one stable system, bias correction may be unnecessary—but must be explicit.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Reference value or defensible triangulation, representative operating range and stable calibration/evaluator state.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Enough resolution and coverage to detect both offset and dispersion; uncertainty in the reference included.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Bias is reasonably stable or modelled over range/context; reference is more informative than the system under test.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents repeated measurement from converting shared bias into narrow but false confidence.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Measurement owner and decision consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-correcting an uncertain estimated bias can add variance or create discontinuities.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring bias can leave every repeated test green while the underlying property remains wrong.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Using correlation as accuracy; reporting only standard deviation; reference standard shares the same bias.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Using correlation as accuracy; reporting only standard deviation; reference standard shares the same bias. Contrary evidence/limit: For complex construct measures, “true value” may be unavailable; bias must then be bounded through convergent/criterion evidence rather than asserted.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern practice reports trueness/bias, precision and uncertainty rather than one undifferentiated “accuracy” score.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Decision-specific decomposition of systematic and random error, with correction only when validated.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Extra decimal places and repeated readings are ceremony when the dominant error is systematic.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S015, S052, S057
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S024, S032
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S055
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** For complex construct measures, “true value” may be unavailable; bias must then be bounded through convergent/criterion evidence rather than asserted.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How can shared-method bias be detected when all available reference systems inherit common training data or assumptions?

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |

- What independent reference or triangulation checks bias?
- Would more repetitions reduce the dominant uncertainty component?

### P005 — Distinguish repeatability, reproducibility and validity

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P005`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Distinguish repeatability, reproducibility and validity
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Interlaboratory measurement science, ISO 5725, gauge R&R, rater reliability and reproducibility debates.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Estimate same-condition variation separately from operator/site/instrument/context variation.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A result may repeat under one setup but fail across competent operators or contexts; both may repeat an invalid construct.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Treating identical reruns as independent corroboration or interpreting consistency as truth.
- **MECHANISM:** Design crossed/nested comparisons over relevant operators, instruments, sites, times and methods; assess validity separately.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Multi-operator, multi-site, automated evaluator, stochastic test or claimed general result.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For one-use, one-instrument local control with no transfer claim and a wide margin, a limited stability check may suffice.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Representative factors and levels, randomisation/order control, clear agreement target and adequate item range.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement outputs are comparable and evaluator versions are fixed/documented during the study.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Factor effects and interactions can be estimated; repeated conditions approximate intended routine use.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents pseudo-replication and hidden operator/evaluator sensitivity.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Measurement owner, experimenter and anyone claiming portability.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Demanding universal reproducibility can reject legitimately context-specific measurements.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Testing only repeatability misses site/operator/context failures and shared invalidity.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Confusing reproducibility with exact equality; narrow sample range; no validity criterion; treating reruns as new evidence.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Confusing reproducibility with exact equality; narrow sample range; no validity criterion; treating reruns as new evidence. Contrary evidence/limit: Definitions of “reproducibility” vary across metrology and science; the factors changed must always be stated.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from instrument precision to interlaboratory, computational and evaluator-version reproducibility, while retaining validity as a separate burden.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Claim only the level of stability actually tested, and never infer validity from repeatability alone.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A standard GR&R worksheet is optional; the factor-appropriate variance/agreement design is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S057, S058, S059
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S024, S027
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S105, S038
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Definitions of “reproducibility” vary across metrology and science; the factors changed must always be stated.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should reproducibility be defined for non-deterministic systems whose output distribution, not individual output, is the object?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Determine whether measurement conclusions survive relevant changes of operator/instrument/site/context.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Measured item under a specified factor combination.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Repeated items or clusters must be modelled; repeated readings on one item are not independent items.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise order where drift/learning/carryover is possible.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block by item/batch/context when nuisance variation is large.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Replication means new independent items or justified repeated-measures modelling, not duplicated records.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Operator×item and context×method interactions can dominate.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Pre-specify precision/agreement targets and analysis before inspecting favourable subsets.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Quantifies which variation source threatens the decision.
- **COST:** Additional operators/sites/items and possibly destructive samples.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** A maximal study may be wasteful for a narrow local use.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Factor-matched reproducibility study plus separate validity evidence.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Are repeated results same-condition repeatability or genuinely cross-condition reproducibility?
- Which operator, site, seed, environment or evaluator factors were varied?
- What evidence addresses validity rather than consistency?

### P006 — Match resolution, sensitivity, specificity and threshold discrimination to the decision

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P006`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Match resolution, sensitivity, specificity and threshold discrimination to the decision
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Instrument resolution/metrology, diagnostic classification, ROC analysis and conformity-assessment decision rules.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Ensure the measurement/classifier can separate states on opposite sides of the action boundary.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A coarse or poorly discriminating evaluator makes candidate and measurement variation observationally inseparable.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False passes/fails hidden by a precise-looking continuous score or an arbitrary threshold.
- **MECHANISM:** Characterise resolution and class-conditional error across the operating range; choose thresholds using consequence-weighted false-positive/false-negative risks.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Threshold decisions, defect detection, rare-event screens, small expected improvements, or changing class prevalence.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When a deterministic authoritative predicate directly distinguishes states, use it instead of estimating sensitivity/specificity.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable labels/reference adjudication, representative prevalence/spectrum, stated loss asymmetry and threshold version.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Reference labels are sufficiently accurate and the validation set represents intended use; resolution is not inflated by preprocessing leakage.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Future cases are drawn from a relevant population or shift is monitored; class definitions remain stable.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Reduces threshold gaming and makes error trade-offs visible.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Decision owner, safety/quality gate, monitoring operator.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Optimising sensitivity can create alert floods; optimising specificity can hide rare costly defects.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** A low-power detector can report no change while meaningful failures accumulate.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Selecting threshold on the same test set; ignoring prevalence; changing expected answers after failures; resolution below tolerance width.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Selecting threshold on the same test set; ignoring prevalence; changing expected answers after failures; resolution below tolerance width. Contrary evidence/limit: ROC-like summaries may obscure calibration, prevalence shift and heterogeneous costs; no single threshold is universally optimal.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from instrument least count and fixed cutoffs to calibrated decision rules, guard bands, ROC/utility analysis and drift checks.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use the cheapest discriminator whose error profile is adequate for the decision and freeze/version its threshold unless independent recalibration evidence exists.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A universal “90% accuracy” or fixed sigma cutoff is ceremony; the decision-specific error surface is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S052, S053, S103
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S024, S039
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S100
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** ROC-like summaries may obscure calibration, prevalence shift and heterogeneous costs; no single threshold is universally optimal.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should threshold uncertainty be propagated when both labels and prevalence drift?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Choose and maintain an action threshold under measurement error and changing prevalence.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Latent state observed through a noisy score/classifier with class-conditional errors.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Representative reference labels, uncertainty and threshold versioning.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Stable separation/calibration sufficient for consequence-weighted decision.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Alert burden, needless rejection or process tampering.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Missed defect or false assurance.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Prevalence/concept drift changes predictive values and optimal threshold.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Post-failure threshold movement can reward-hack the metric.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Authoritative deterministic predicate with negligible ambiguity.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Guard-banded/versioned threshold with periodic independent validation.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What false-pass and false-fail rates matter at the actual threshold?
- Is the evaluator’s resolution small enough relative to the decision margin?
- Can a threshold change be justified on evidence independent of the failed candidate?

### P007 — Carry measurement uncertainty into engineering decisions

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P007`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Carry measurement uncertainty into engineering decisions
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** GUM uncertainty budgets, calibration science and conformity-assessment decision rules.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Identify material uncertainty components, combine them appropriately and report their relation to the decision boundary.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Point estimates and binary labels can conceal that the decision is not actually discriminated.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False precision, unsupported conformance, and treating reference or model uncertainty as zero.
- **MECHANISM:** Build an uncertainty model/budget, propagate components and use intervals, guard bands or explicit loss-based rules.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Measurements close to specifications, comparisons with small effects, extrapolations, and regulated claims.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When the result is far from every relevant boundary and a conservative worst-case bound suffices, a detailed budget may be unnecessary.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined measurand/model, traceable components where applicable, covariance handling, and a stated coverage/decision rule.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Inputs and correlations are characterised over the intended regime; software/preprocessing uncertainty included when material.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The propagation model is adequate; uncertainty is not silently conditional on fixed but uncertain calibration/model choices.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents binary overclaim and directs effort toward dominant uncertainty components.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Conformance authority, designer, customer or risk owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Excessively conservative stacking can reject useful systems and encourage uninformative complexity.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring uncertainty makes near-boundary decisions appear decisive and weakens reproducibility.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Double-counted components; omitted covariance; confidence interval mislabeled as tolerance interval; uncertainty reported without decision use.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Double-counted components; omitted covariance; confidence interval mislabeled as tolerance interval; uncertainty reported without decision use. Contrary evidence/limit: GUM-style propagation can be strained by nonlinearity, asymmetric tails, model uncertainty and adaptive algorithms; simulation/Bayesian or interval methods may be needed.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from error margins to structured budgets and decision rules linking uncertainty to false accept/reject risks.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use proportionate uncertainty analysis sufficient to show whether the next action is discriminated.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A long budget is ceremony if a dominant bound already resolves the decision; conversely, “±” without a model is decorative.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S051, S053, S100
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S054, S103
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S015, S055
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** GUM-style propagation can be strained by nonlinearity, asymmetric tails, model uncertainty and adaptive algorithms; simulation/Bayesian or interval methods may be needed.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should epistemic model uncertainty and evaluator retraining uncertainty be integrated without false numerical precision?

| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Would plausible measurement uncertainty cross the action boundary?
- Which uncertainty component dominates, and can it be reduced economically?
- Is the interval about a parameter, future observation, or population content?

### P008 — Evaluate inter-rater/evaluator agreement with a design matched to use

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P008`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Evaluate inter-rater/evaluator agreement with a design matched to use
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Interlaboratory precision, ICC families, method-comparison and modern evaluator studies.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Quantify agreement, not merely correlation, under the exact rater/evaluator sampling and decision target.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Evaluators can rank cases similarly while disagreeing materially in level or pass/fail outcome.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Stable-looking scores that vary by evaluator, rubric interpretation, operator or model version.
- **MECHANISM:** Use paired/crossed items, blinded/randomised order, agreement-appropriate ICC/kappa/difference analyses, disagreement adjudication and subgroup checks.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Subjective ratings, manual inspection, model judging, multi-laboratory tests, or operator-dependent measurements.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** A deterministic reference rule with auditable outputs may need spot verification rather than a full agreement study.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Representative difficult/easy items, sufficient rater diversity, stable rubric, and a declared agreement target.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Raters/evaluators are independent enough to expose variation rather than copied or trained on identical answers.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The selected agreement statistic matches fixed/random raters, absolute agreement/consistency and scale properties.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Reveals evaluator variance and cases where no stable decision exists.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Test owner, adjudicator, quality gate and downstream experimenter.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Forcing consensus can erase legitimate ambiguity or minority expertise.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring disagreement converts evaluator identity into hidden treatment.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Using Pearson correlation; prevalence-sensitive kappa without context; shared rubric bias; adjudicating after seeing candidate identity.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Using Pearson correlation; prevalence-sensitive kappa without context; shared rubric bias; adjudicating after seeing candidate identity. Contrary evidence/limit: Agreement can be high because cases are homogeneous or decisions trivial; validity and representativeness remain separate.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from precision/reproducibility studies to versioned evaluator ensembles and explicit adjudication.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Report disagreement structure, not only one coefficient; block governance where disagreement crosses decision boundaries.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A fashionable reliability coefficient or arbitrary cutoff is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S024, S059, S057
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S027, S097
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S105
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Agreement can be high because cases are homogeneous or decisions trivial; validity and representativeness remain separate.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How much independent evaluator diversity is needed before agreement adds evidence rather than multiplicity theatre?

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Determine whether evaluator identity changes the engineering conclusion.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Item×evaluator judgement, with item as the generalisation unit.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Multiple ratings of one item are clustered; evaluators may share training/rubric dependence.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise/blind order and candidate identity where possible.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Stratify by item difficulty/context and evaluator type.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent items and independently formed judgements, not duplicated outputs.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Evaluator×item interactions identify ambiguous regions.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Predefine agreement target and adjudication protocol.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Separates stable decisions from evaluator-sensitive ones.
- **COST:** Rater time, adjudication and representative case construction.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Excessive panels can create consensus bureaucracy and common-mode bias.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Decision-linked agreement study plus construct-validity checks.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Do evaluators agree on the actual action, not just rank ordering?
- Were difficult and boundary cases represented?
- Could shared training data or rubric bias make agreement non-independent?

### P009 — Test measurement invariance and transport across contexts

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P009`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Test measurement invariance and transport across contexts
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Interlaboratory comparison, psychometric invariance, dataset-shift analysis and robust measurement practice.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Check whether the same score has the same interpretation across sites, populations, environments, versions or time.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A metric may be stable locally yet change meaning under population mix, language, hardware, operator or deployment context.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Invalid cross-context comparisons, hidden subgroup bias and false trend attribution.
- **MECHANISM:** Specify transport claim, compare calibration/error/construct structure across contexts, and restrict claims or recalibrate when invariance fails.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Cross-site benchmarking, longitudinal monitoring, heterogeneous users, distribution shift or environment changes.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** No invariance study is needed for an explicitly local, one-time decision that makes no comparison beyond its validated context.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Comparable reference outcomes, sufficient overlap/coverage, versioned context metadata and a plausible transport model.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** The measurement process and sampling frame are observable enough to distinguish construct shift from composition shift.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Observed contexts span the intended deployment; unobserved effect modifiers are bounded or acknowledged.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents scores from being treated as universal when they are context-indexed.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Benchmark owner, product/system decision maker, regulator or cross-site analyst.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-demanding invariance may suppress legitimate context-specific adaptation.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** No check permits silent construct drift and unfair/unsafe threshold transfer.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Aggregate invariance hiding subgroup failure; recalibration on contaminated outcomes; using identical preprocessing as proof of meaning.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Aggregate invariance hiding subgroup failure; recalibration on contaminated outcomes; using identical preprocessing as proof of meaning. Contrary evidence/limit: No finite study guarantees future invariance under open-ended distribution shift; monitoring and bounded claims are required.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from interlaboratory reproducibility to formal invariance, calibration transport and shift monitoring.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Claim equivalence only over tested contexts; otherwise maintain context-specific calibration or explicit uncertainty.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** One global score or normalisation recipe is ceremony when meanings differ.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S027, S057, S099
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S039, S038
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S105, S104
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** No finite study guarantees future invariance under open-ended distribution shift; monitoring and bounded claims are required.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Which context dimensions must be covered before a metric can support a general engineering claim?

| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Decide whether a change in score reflects a changed target state or changed measurement context.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Context-indexed latent property observed through context-sensitive measurement and sampling.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Comparable anchors/reference outcomes and context metadata.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Stable calibration/construct relation or an identified context effect.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** False process intervention due to composition/evaluator shift.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Failure to detect real subgroup/context degradation.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** High: deployment populations and instruments evolve.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Global recalibration can erase local failures; local tuning can destroy comparability.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Restrict the claim to the validated context.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Versioned context-specific calibration with transport tests and drift alarms.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Has the score retained calibration and meaning across the contexts being compared?
- Could a changed population mix explain the apparent process change?
- Is a context-specific metric safer than forced global invariance?

### P010 — Monitor evaluator/instrument drift and require independent recalibration evidence

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P010`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Monitor evaluator/instrument drift and require independent recalibration evidence
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Check standards and measurement assurance, control of calibration systems, longitudinal rater/benchmark stability.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Treat the measurement system as a process that can drift, not a one-time certified constant.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Apparent candidate/process change may be caused by aging sensors, changed operators, rubric edits, data refreshes or threshold movement.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Attributing measurement change to the object; changing the evaluator after failure until a pass appears.
- **MECHANISM:** Use stable check/reference samples, bridge studies, version control, drift limits and independent change justification before recalibration.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Long-lived controls, automated scoring, model-based evaluators, changing laboratories or any metric with material maintenance.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a one-shot destructive test with contemporaneous references, ongoing drift monitoring may be impossible; preserve calibration evidence and uncertainty instead.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Reference/check material stable enough to detect drift, bridge overlap between versions, and governance independent of candidate outcome.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Check samples represent important ranges and are protected from optimisation/contamination.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Reference drift is slower or independently detectable; repeated checks are not all affected by the same environmental cause.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Preserves comparability and prevents evaluator mutation from masquerading as improvement.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Measurement owner, release/acceptance authority and audit consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-sensitive drift rules create churn; frozen obsolete evaluators can also become invalid.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** No drift detection can systematically misclassify every subsequent candidate or hide concept change.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Rebaselining after alarms; editing expected answers after seeing failures; check-set overfitting; no version bridge.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Rebaselining after alarms; editing expected answers after seeing failures; check-set overfitting; no version bridge. Contrary evidence/limit: Reference materials and holdouts can themselves age, leak or become unrepresentative; drift detection has no absolute anchor without external refresh.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from physical check standards to benchmark holdouts, blind audits, bridge datasets and change-control evidence.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Recalibrate when independent evidence shows measurement defect or context change; preserve old/new bridge and re-evaluate prior conclusions where material.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A calendar recalibration interval or automatic threshold refresh is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S015, S060, S056
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S038, S039, S097
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S105, S032
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Reference materials and holdouts can themselves age, leak or become unrepresentative; drift detection has no absolute anchor without external refresh.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should old conclusions be revisited after a material evaluator recalibration?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Detect whether the measurement process, rather than the object, has changed.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Stable/slowly varying references observed through a potentially drifting evaluator.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Protected check samples, version metadata and bridge overlap.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Reference/control deviation beyond a consequence-weighted drift boundary.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Unnecessary recalibration and broken comparability.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Persistent biased governance or hidden evaluator degradation.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Reference populations and constructs may drift too.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Rebaselining to current outputs can erase evidence.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Manual reference comparison before a high-stakes decision.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Independent bridge study, versioned change control and bounded revalidation.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What evidence distinguishes evaluator drift from candidate/process change?
- Was recalibration justified using evidence independent of the failed candidate?
- Is there a bridge allowing old and new evaluator versions to be compared?

### P011 — Use representative reference standards and challenge shared-reference bias

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P011`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use representative reference standards and challenge shared-reference bias
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Calibration artefacts, measurement assurance samples, proficiency testing and method-comparison practice.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Validate against references/check samples spanning the real operating range and difficult conditions.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A measurement system can perform well on ideal standards while failing on routine matrices, edge cases or degraded specimens.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False validation from easy, narrow, contaminated or common-mode reference samples.
- **MECHANISM:** Select reference items by intended-use strata and failure mechanisms; use independent methods/sources where shared bias is plausible.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Calibration transfer, evaluator validation, threshold setting and cross-site comparison.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a single known artefact or exact digital identity check, one authoritative reference may be enough.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable assigned values with uncertainty, representative coverage and documented provenance.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Reference uncertainty and commutability/representativeness are known; reference items are not leaked into optimisation.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Sampled references cover decision-relevant variability and do not share all defects with the candidate measurement.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Finds context-specific bias and reduces benchmark overfitting.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Calibration laboratory, evaluator owner and downstream decision maker.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Broad reference programmes can be costly and still miss unknown unknowns.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Narrow references can certify a system that fails precisely where decisions are hardest.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Using only pristine standards; treating consensus as truth; reusing public benchmarks until memorised; excluding failures from the reference pool.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Using only pristine standards; treating consensus as truth; reusing public benchmarks until memorised; excluding failures from the reference pool. Contrary evidence/limit: Representativeness cannot be proven exhaustively; rare or emerging failure modes may be absent.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from physical reference artefacts to commutable samples, proficiency tests, blind holdouts and multi-method triangulation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Reference evidence is valid only for the covered range/context and must be protected from optimisation.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A named standard material or benchmark is not sufficient if it is nonrepresentative.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S060, S105, S055
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S038, S039
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S104
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Representativeness cannot be proven exhaustively; rare or emerging failure modes may be absent.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How can reference sets be refreshed without destroying longitudinal comparability or leaking the target?

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Do reference samples span actual boundary and failure cases?
- Could candidate and reference share the same systematic defect?
- Has optimisation exposure contaminated the reference set?

### P012 — Preserve data provenance, preprocessing, version and lineage

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P012`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Preserve data provenance, preprocessing, version and lineage
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Laboratory records/traceability, reproducible analysis, adaptive-data concerns and modern data governance.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MODERN_DOMAIN_TRANSLATION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Record where observations came from, how they were selected/transformed, and which evaluator/code/threshold version produced each result.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Untracked filtering, deduplication, relabelling or evaluator changes can manufacture an apparent effect.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Irreproducible evidence, hidden leakage, duplicate evidence and inability to separate data change from process change.
- **MECHANISM:** Use immutable identifiers, transformation logs, selection/exclusion reasons, versioned code/configuration and lineage-aware aggregation.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Any multi-stage analysis, reused benchmark, merged source, adaptive evaluation or longitudinal comparison.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** A small manually inspectable deterministic dataset may need only a concise signed record, not a full data platform.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable identifiers, access controls, timestamp/version semantics and retention of raw or authoritative source where lawful.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Preprocessing is deterministic or its randomness/configuration is recorded; lineage captures duplication and dependence.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Recorded provenance is complete enough to reconstruct decision-relevant transformations.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Enables replication, dependence detection, rollback and attribution of evaluator/data changes.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Analyst, reviewer, regulator, future maintainer and experiment consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Heavy lineage systems can become compliance theatre and create privacy/security cost.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Missing lineage allows cherry-picking, test contamination and false multiplicity.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Overwriting raw data; undocumented manual fixes; copied observations counted as independent; changing labels without bridge.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Overwriting raw data; undocumented manual fixes; copied observations counted as independent; changing labels without bridge. Contrary evidence/limit: Provenance proves what happened, not that the recorded process was unbiased or valid.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from laboratory notebooks/calibration certificates to executable pipelines, dataset/evaluator cards and contamination controls.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Preserve the minimum complete lineage needed to reconstruct the evidence and its dependence structure.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A particular tool, schema or immutable ledger is optional; reconstructability and identity are the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S056, S038, S110
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S039, S040, S097
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S105
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Provenance proves what happened, not that the recorded process was unbiased or valid.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What lineage granularity is sufficient to detect common-source dependence without imposing prohibitive cost?
