- Can every reported result be tied to exact data, preprocessing, evaluator and threshold versions?
- Are duplicated or derived observations being counted as independent evidence?
- Can excluded measurements be audited with reasons?

### P013 — Model missingness, censoring, exclusions and selection mechanisms

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P013`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Model missingness, censoring, exclusions and selection mechanisms
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Sampling theory, Rubin missing-data framework, reliability censoring and selective-reporting criticism.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `STATISTICAL_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Treat absence/exclusion as part of the data-generating and observation process rather than silently deleting it.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Failures may be missing, censored or excluded non-randomly, biasing rates, lifetimes and comparisons.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Survivorship bias, complete-case bias, favourable subgroup selection and underreported failure tails.
- **MECHANISM:** Record reasons/timing, define censoring, analyse sensitivity under plausible mechanisms, and use design/model methods only when assumptions are defensible.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Dropout, time-limited tests, incomplete logs, destructive failures, filtered benchmark items or unobserved outcomes.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** If missingness is demonstrably negligible and unrelated to outcome/exposure, transparent complete-case treatment may be adequate.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Provenance of attempted units, exposure time/opportunity, selection rule and reason codes.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** The system distinguishes “not observed,” “not applicable,” “failed before measurement,” and deliberate exclusion.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Chosen MAR/MCAR/censoring/selection assumptions approximate reality; sensitivity bounds cover uncertainty.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents falsely optimistic rates and clarifies which population the result describes.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Reliability analyst, experimenter, quality gate and consumer of aggregate metrics.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-modelled imputation can inject unjustified information and narrow uncertainty.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring informative missingness can reverse conclusions or erase catastrophic cases.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Calling failures “invalid”; censoring at different risk states; post-hoc subgroup restriction; imputation model trained on outcome leakage.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Calling failures “invalid”; censoring at different risk states; post-hoc subgroup restriction; imputation model trained on outcome leakage. Contrary evidence/limit: Missing-not-at-random mechanisms are generally not identified from observed data alone; sensitivity/partial identification may be the honest endpoint.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from simple complete-case analysis to explicit missingness mechanisms, survival methods, inverse weighting/bounds and sensitivity analysis.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Make the observation/selection process auditable and report conclusions conditional on or robust to plausible missingness mechanisms.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Automatic imputation or a missing-data checklist is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S020, S071, S077
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S040, S042
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S087
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Missing-not-at-random mechanisms are generally not identified from observed data alone; sensitivity/partial identification may be the honest endpoint.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Which missingness mechanisms are distinguishable with available auxiliary/process data?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Estimate effect/performance despite incomplete outcomes without hiding selection.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Originally eligible experimental or observational unit, including censored units.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Missingness can induce dependence/selection; clusters remain clusters.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomisation protects assignment but not post-assignment missingness.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Stratify/design follow-up around predictors of missingness.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Replication counts eligible independent units, not only completed observations.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Treatment/context can interact with dropout/censoring.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Pre-specify exclusion and censoring rules; document deviations.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Recovers or bounds decision-relevant estimates and exposes uncertainty.
- **COST:** Follow-up, instrumentation and larger sample requirements.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Complex imputation can become decorative if assumptions are unknowable.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Transparent mechanism model plus sensitivity/bounds and all-unit accounting.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Which attempted units or outcomes are absent, censored or excluded?
- Could exclusion probability depend on failure severity or candidate identity?
- Would plausible missing-not-at-random scenarios change the decision?

### P014 — Account for measurement intervention, destruction and contamination

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P014`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Account for measurement intervention, destruction and contamination
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `MEASUREMENT_AND_DATA_INTEGRITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Destructive inspection/acceptance sampling, test-effect/carryover design, measurement assurance and sensor intrusiveness.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DOMAIN_SPECIFIC`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Recognise that observing can consume, stress, train, prime, cache or otherwise alter the unit/system and later evidence.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Repeated measurements may not be exchangeable because the first test changes the candidate, operator or environment.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Pseudo-replication, induced failures, test-set learning and claiming non-destructive evidence from altered units.
- **MECHANISM:** Classify measurement as destructive/intrusive, randomise order, use fresh units or washout/holdouts, and model carryover when unavoidable.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Destructive material tests, accelerated stress, human/agent learning, repeated benchmark exposure, cache/warm-up effects.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When a verified measurement is genuinely non-invasive and order-invariant, ordinary repeated-measures methods may suffice.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined experimental unit, exposure history, test order, fresh-unit availability and a mechanism for intervention effects.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Provenance records prior exposures and distinguishes unit reuse from independent replication.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Carryover/washout model is plausible or fresh independent units are available.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents repeated testing from manufacturing the very behaviour it purports to measure.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experiment designer, reliability/test engineer and benchmark owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Fresh-unit requirements can be expensive, unethical or impossible; overly strict rules can block learning.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring intervention can produce optimistic adaptation or pessimistic stress damage.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Calling warmed/cached retries independent; destructive test sample not representative; training on failed benchmark items; inadequate washout.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Calling warmed/cached retries independent; destructive test sample not representative; training on failed benchmark items; inadequate washout. Contrary evidence/limit: In adaptive systems, measurement and improvement may be inseparable by design; the goal becomes explicit online-learning evaluation rather than pretending independence.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from destructive sampling and crossover design to explicit benchmark exposure and adaptive-system contamination controls.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use fresh or counterbalanced units where material; otherwise model and report intervention history and limit claims.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A blanket ban on retesting is not the property; exposure-aware evidence is.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S068, S048, S038
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S039, S040
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S072
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** In adaptive systems, measurement and improvement may be inseparable by design; the goal becomes explicit online-learning evaluation rather than pretending independence.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** When is reuse acceptable because the deployment itself includes learning from feedback?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Determine performance/effect without confounding it with prior measurement exposure.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Fresh unit or explicitly exposure-indexed repeated unit.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Repeated observations on altered units are dependent.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise test order/exposure; use controls or fresh units.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block by batch, order, prior exposure or stress level.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent replication requires unexposed units or a correct carryover model.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Exposure×candidate/context interactions may be the main effect.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Predefine retest/washout and stopping rules.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Separates intrinsic behaviour from measurement-induced adaptation/damage.
- **COST:** Fresh samples, destructive loss, washout time.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Full fresh-unit designs may be prohibitive.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Exposure-aware design with fresh-unit cheap path only where decision consequences justify it.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Does running the measurement change the unit, evaluator, cache, operator or future candidate?
- Are retries fresh independent units or exposed repetitions?
- Is test exposure part of the intended deployment, and if so is it evaluated as such?

### P015 — Discriminate routine/common variation from evidence of a changed process

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P015`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Discriminate routine/common variation from evidence of a changed process
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shewhart’s Bell Labs statistical control and later common/special-cause translation.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Use time-ordered evidence to distinguish an approximately stable pattern from departures worth investigation.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Treating every fluctuation as actionable or averaging away a real process change.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Tampering, alert fatigue, blame, missed assignable mechanisms and invalid prediction from unstable data.
- **MECHANISM:** Establish a relevant baseline, plot/model observations in time order, use calibrated signals, and investigate mechanisms rather than equate alarms with causes.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Repeated process output where a stable-enough reference regime and actionable changes are plausible.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a deterministic failure with an authoritative cause, repair it directly; for one-off heterogeneous work with no repeatable unit, causal case analysis may dominate charting.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined process/stream, chronological data, adequate measurement, comparable opportunities and a consequence-linked response plan.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement error is small enough and evaluator drift is separated from process drift.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The baseline is sufficiently homogeneous/stable for the chosen chart/model; dependence and mixtures are handled.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Reduces noise-chasing while creating earlier evidence of meaningful change.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Process owner and investigator—not a mechanism-free automated punishment system.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** A signal may be a false alarm or harmless change; investigation/adjustment can destabilise the process.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Weak signals or poor measurement can miss slow, small or rare harmful changes.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Control limits treated as specs; alarms treated as causes; retrospective cherry-picking; rebaselining away failures; worker blame.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The two-cause vocabulary can oversimplify continuous, interacting and drifting sources; “special” is statistical, not moral or personal.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from Shewhart charts to CUSUM/EWMA, time-series/residual, change-point and context-specific monitoring with explicit costs.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use the lightest calibrated discriminator that separates expected variation from actionable change, then require causal investigation before intervention.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** The property is discrimination and response discipline, not putting every metric on a three-sigma chart.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S001, S002, S003
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S030, S031, S032
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S061, S066, S106
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** In highly adaptive or nonstationary systems there may be no enduring common-cause baseline; change detection must be reframed around local regimes or forecasts.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What level of local stability is sufficient for useful monitoring in continuously changing systems?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Decide when a process departure warrants investigation or intervention.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Approximately stable baseline plus occasional changes, or an explicitly modelled dynamic process.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Time-ordered comparable measurements with controlled evaluator variation.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** A signal with known/estimated operating characteristics relative to a target change.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Wasted investigation, alert fatigue and tampering.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Undetected degradation or delayed containment.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** High if the baseline, population or operating policy changes.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Immediate adjustment to noise can increase variation and obscure causes.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct mechanism evidence or a simple visual/time-order check for large changes.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Calibrated monitoring plus investigation, error costs and rebaseline governance.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Does the target system distinguish expected variation from evidence of a changed process?
- Is a chart signal being mistaken for a proven causal mechanism?
- Could the apparent change instead be evaluator or population drift?

### P016 — Choose rational subgroups and model dependence rather than count correlated observations as sample size

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P016`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Choose rational subgroups and model dependence rather than count correlated observations as sample size
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shewhart subgroup logic, time-series SPC and pseudoreplication criticism.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Arrange within/between-group comparisons so the statistic reflects the variation source relevant to the decision.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Subgroups can mix different regimes or repeated readings can masquerade as independent evidence.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Wrong control limits, attenuated/expanded uncertainty, false replication and concealed between-batch/time effects.
- **MECHANISM:** Define the process unit and correlation structure; subgroup items exposed to common short-term conditions when that answers the decision, or use cluster/time-series models.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Batch, lot, repeated-run, time-series, nested, clustered or same-seed/same-environment observations.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When observations are demonstrably independent and identically produced at the decision scale, ordinary independent analysis is adequate.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Process map, timestamps/batch/seed/operator identifiers, enough clusters/subgroups and a defensible within/between variation interpretation.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement repeats are labelled separately from production units; common-source dependence is observable.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Within-subgroup homogeneity and between-subgroup opportunity for relevant change, or a valid dependence model.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Corrects effective sample size and makes signals interpretable at the intervention scale.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Monitoring designer, experimenter and evidence consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Overly narrow subgroups can hide important within-window heterogeneity; complex models can reduce transparency.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring dependence yields false precision and repeated green checks that are informationally redundant.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Convenience subgroups; averaging across shifts; treating multiple outputs from one run as independent; one cluster per condition.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** “Rational subgroup” is not a magic recipe; it encodes a causal/process judgement and may fail in low-volume/high-mix work.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from subgroup construction rules to hierarchical/time-series/cluster-robust modelling and explicit effective sample size.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Match the unit of evidence and correlation model to the unit of action; disclose residual dependence.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Fixed subgroup sizes or an X-bar/R worksheet are implementation choices, not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S002, S023, S026
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S030, S031, S067
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S032, S107
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Dependence can be difficult to estimate with few clusters or changing correlation; conservative bounds may be preferable to elaborate fitted models.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should evidence be discounted when shared prompts, seeds, fixtures or upstream data create partially known dependence?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Set limits/uncertainty at the correct process and intervention unit.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Nested/clustered/time-correlated observations with within- and between-group variation.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Identifiers for cluster, order, batch, seed, operator and repeated measurement.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Between/within decomposition or dependence-adjusted statistic.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** False alarms or apparent significance from underestimated variance.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Hidden shifts if inappropriate averaging dilutes between-group change.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Correlation structure itself can drift.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Adjusting individual readings when the actionable cause is batch/system-level.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Use cluster summaries or conservative effective sample size when modelling is not worth the cost.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Rational grouping or explicit hierarchical/time-series analysis tied to action.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What is the independent experimental/process unit?
- Are repeated same-seed, same-batch or same-evaluator observations being counted as independent?
- Do subgroup boundaries correspond to plausible variation mechanisms?

### P017 — Keep statistical control limits separate from engineering specifications and tolerances

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P017`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Keep statistical control limits separate from engineering specifications and tolerances
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shewhart control charts, engineering tolerancing and metrological conformity assessment.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Control limits describe expected process behaviour; specifications define required/allowed performance.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A process can be stable yet unacceptable, or unstable while all observed outputs remain inside specification.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False assurance, inappropriate alarms and using customer limits as if statistically calibrated.
- **MECHANISM:** Maintain separate provenance and semantics for natural-process limits, specification/tolerance limits and uncertainty/guard bands.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Any monitored characteristic with both process history and an external requirement.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For an exact deterministic requirement checked once, a control chart may be unnecessary; use the specification directly with measurement uncertainty.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Authoritative specification, stable-enough process baseline, adequate measurement and explicit conformance decision rule.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Uncertainty near specifications is quantified; specification source/version is fixed.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Control-limit estimation reflects the intended baseline; specs are independent of recent sample behaviour.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents stable bad processes from being celebrated and ordinary variation from being called nonconformance.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Process owner, design authority, customer/regulator and conformance decision maker.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Maintaining multiple limit systems can confuse operators if semantics are not made visible.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Conflation can miss capability problems or create unnecessary intervention.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Setting control limits equal to specs; widening limits to keep data “in control”; treating any in-spec point as evidence of stability.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Three-sigma conventions are not tolerances, and neither system identifies root cause.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved toward explicit uncertainty-aware conformity rules and capability conditional on stability.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use control limits for change detection, specifications for requirements, and guard bands/decision rules for uncertain conformance.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** One dashboard may display all limits, but their meanings must not be merged.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S001, S053, S062
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S103
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S100, S107
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Specifications themselves may be poorly justified or gamed; statistical separation does not validate the requirement.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should dynamic/adaptive specifications be governed without turning every threshold movement into process redefinition?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Separate detection of process change from conformance to an external requirement.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Process distribution over time plus independent specification boundaries.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable measurement scale and uncertainty near boundaries.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Control signal for change; guard-banded result for conformance.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Investigating common variation or rejecting conforming output.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Failing to improve a stable but incapable process.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Specs or process regimes may change and require separately versioned baselines.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Widening either limit after failure can erase evidence.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct deterministic spec check where monitoring adds no decision value.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Semantically separate control, specification and uncertainty decision rules.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Are requirements/specifications being confused with statistical control limits?
- Could the process be stable but incapable, or unstable but currently in specification?
- Is measurement uncertainty included in conformance decisions?

### P018 — Establish process stability before interpreting capability or long-run prediction

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P018`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Establish process stability before interpreting capability or long-run prediction
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shewhart statistical control, process characterisation and modern capability standards.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Assess whether the process exhibits sufficiently consistent behaviour before summarising its capability against specifications.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A single capability index can average multiple regimes, trends or special causes into a misleading number.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Predicting future conformance from an unstable historical mixture.
- **MECHANISM:** Use time order, change/mixture checks and process knowledge; if unstable, model regimes or improve/contain before issuing a capability claim.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Capability, tolerance yield, long-run defect rate or predictive performance claims.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a deterministic design proof or 100% authoritative inspection, a statistical capability index may be irrelevant.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Adequate measurement, chronological data, stable specification and enough observations across normal operating conditions.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Evaluator/measurement process is itself stable and capable enough to observe process change.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Future conditions reasonably resemble the characterised regime or are explicitly modelled.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents false long-run assurance and directs improvement toward instability first.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Process/design owner, supplier/customer and regulator.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Demanding global stationarity can block useful local/conditional capability estimates in adaptive or seasonal processes.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Skipping stability can make a high Cpk pure arithmetic over incompatible data.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Shuffled data; pooled shifts/products; one favourable window; rebaselined outliers; no evaluator-stability check.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Finite data cannot prove permanent stability; “stable enough for this prediction horizon” is the mature claim.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from a binary in/out-of-control prerequisite to local, conditional and time-dependent capability models.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** State the regime and prediction horizon; no unconditional capability claim from unresolved drift/mixture.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A mandatory chart is not necessary if equivalent stability evidence exists; the prerequisite is evidential.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S002, S062, S109
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S064
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S107
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Some systems are intentionally nonstationary; capability may need scenario/conditional performance rather than a single process distribution.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What constitutes adequate local stability for adaptive systems with planned policy changes?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Decide whether historical output supports future capability prediction.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** A stable or explicitly regime-indexed process distribution.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Chronological, comparable measurements and stable evaluator.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** No material unresolved shifts/mixtures for the stated horizon, or a validated dynamic model.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Delaying a useful conditional claim through excessive stationarity demands.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** False long-run conformance assurance from unstable pooled data.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Central risk; local stability may expire.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Rebaselining can convert instability into apparent capability.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Use conditional descriptive yield without predictive claim.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Capability explicitly conditional on regime, horizon and measurement fitness.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Was stability assessed in time order before capability was reported?
- Does the capability number pool distinct products, regimes, populations or evaluator versions?
- What prediction horizon and operating conditions does the claim cover?

### P019 — Use capability indices only conditionally on stability, distribution and measurement fitness

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P019`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use capability indices only conditionally on stability, distribution and measurement fitness
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Process capability analysis, normal-theory indices and ISO 22514 evolution.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Compare process location/spread with specification width using indices such as Cp/Cpk under stated conditions.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A convenient scalar can hide skew, tails, drift, measurement error, centring and subgroup structure.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Sigma-score theatre, unsupported defect-rate conversion and supplier ranking on invalid indices.
- **MECHANISM:** Verify prerequisites; report distribution-free/empirical or model-based alternatives and uncertainty; keep indices subordinate to raw/time-ordered evidence.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** High-volume repeatable processes where a stable distribution and specification-based decision exist.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Use direct yield/tolerance evidence, deterministic limits, or scenario-specific performance when assumptions fail or sample is tiny.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** P018 stability, P002 measurement fitness, representative independent data, stable specification and an adequate distribution/tail model.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement variation is small or incorporated; rounding/censoring do not dominate.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Selected index/distribution describes the regime and tail behaviour; sample size supports intended precision.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Compactly communicates margin and can prioritise centring/variation reduction when valid.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Process engineer, supplier/customer and quality planner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Index targets encourage gaming, overinvestment and false equivalence among different risk profiles.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Avoiding indices entirely can lose a useful summary when prerequisites genuinely hold.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Cpk on unstable data; normal DPMO conversion under skew/dependence; ignoring confidence intervals; choosing the most favourable index.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Cpk on unstable data; normal DPMO conversion under skew/dependence; ignoring confidence intervals; choosing the most favourable index. Contrary evidence/limit: Tail defect-rate claims can be dominated by model error far beyond observed data; index precision is not tail certainty.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** NIST and ISO narrow capability to stable/in-control or time-dependent models; modern practice reports conditions and uncertainty.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** A conditional summary—not evidence of control, causality or universal “sigma level.”
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** The index and threshold are optional tools; margin/yield evidence under valid assumptions is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S062, S063, S064
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S032, S084
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S107, S109
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Tail defect-rate claims can be dominated by model error far beyond observed data; index precision is not tail certainty.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** When should capability be expressed as scenario-specific prediction intervals rather than a scalar index?

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Summarise whether a stable process can meet specifications.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Stable/regime-specific distribution with known specification limits.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Adequate resolution, low enough measurement error and representative samples.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Estimated margin/yield with uncertainty under an adequate model.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Gaming a scalar target or rejecting acceptable conditional performance.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** False assurance in tails or under drift.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Invalidates static indices unless modelled.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Changing limits/specs or excluding tails to improve index.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct empirical yield/margin or deterministic tolerance analysis.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Conditional index with raw plots, uncertainty and assumption checks.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Are capability prerequisites—stability, measurement fitness and distribution—demonstrated?
- Is a sigma/DPMO conversion extrapolating far beyond observed data?
- Would direct yield or deterministic margin communicate the decision more honestly?

### P020 — Prevent tampering and overadjustment to routine variation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P020`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Prevent tampering and overadjustment to routine variation
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shewhart economic control and Deming’s later funnel/tampering translation.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Do not alter a stable process in response to fluctuations lacking evidence of an actionable change.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Feedback to noise adds variance, oscillation, bias and instability.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Operator blame, parameter chasing, endless threshold tuning and regression-to-the-mean misread as improvement.
- **MECHANISM:** Define when adjustment is authorised, use calibrated signals/mechanism evidence, and evaluate interventions against a stable baseline or experiment.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Feedback-controlled processes, manual tuning, metric dashboards, repeated reviews and near-threshold variation.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Correct an authoritative deterministic defect immediately; no statistical wait is needed when mechanism/effect is known.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement fitness, action rule, process response knowledge, and ability to distinguish intervention from observation noise.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** The metric is stable enough that an adjustment response is not triggered by evaluator noise.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Unadjusted process has a meaningful baseline and feedback dynamics are understood enough to avoid instability.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Reduces self-inflicted variation, wasted work and false learning.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Operator, process owner and change authority.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Rules too conservative can delay response to small but costly drift.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Rules too permissive create chronic oscillation and hide systemic causes.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Changing settings after every bad point; rewarding short regression-to-mean; retuning evaluator after candidate failure; local optimisation.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** “Do not tamper” must not become “never improve”; designed experiments deliberately change a process under controlled learning.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved into explicit feedback design, intervention analysis and separation of monitoring from experimentation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** No reactive adjustment without a discriminator or planned experiment whose expected value exceeds intervention risk.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A fixed run rule is not the property; disciplined response to evidence is.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S002, S003, S089
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S066
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S014
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** In rapidly drifting environments, delayed adaptation can be worse than overadjustment; adaptive control must model that trade-off.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should tampering risk be balanced against concept drift when the optimal policy is itself nonstationary?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Decide whether to adjust a process after an observation.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Stable process plus feedback response to interventions, possibly with drift.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Reliable time-ordered metric and known action latency.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Calibrated signal or mechanism evidence beyond routine variation.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Adjustment-induced variance, oscillation and wasted investigation.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Delayed response to real deterioration.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Changes optimal sensitivity and action cadence.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Core risk: feedback to noise destabilises the process.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct correction for a known deterministic defect.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Predefined response rules plus post-intervention evaluation.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Does the target system change the candidate/process because of noise rather than a discriminating signal?
- Could regression to the mean explain the apparent improvement after intervention?
- Is the intervention a controlled experiment or untracked parameter chasing?

### P021 — Design monitoring economically around false-alarm, missed-shift and delay costs

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P021`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Design monitoring economically around false-alarm, missed-shift and delay costs
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shewhart economic control, Duncan economic chart design, CUSUM/EWMA operating characteristics and decision analysis.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Choose sampling frequency, statistic and threshold according to consequential shifts and error/delay costs.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** More sensitive rules can flood operators; conservative rules can miss costly small shifts.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Run-rule ritual, alert fatigue, hidden detection delay and sampling costs exceeding prevention value.
- **MECHANISM:** Define target changes, in-control/out-of-control models or simulations, ARL/delay/precision and consequence-weighted action costs.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Ongoing monitoring with repeated opportunities, finite response capacity and quantifiable consequences.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For rare high-consequence events with a deterministic safety interlock, use the interlock; for low-cost obvious defects, direct inspection may dominate statistical detection.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Actionable response plan, baseline, measurement capability, realistic shift scenarios and cost/risk owner.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Sampling/measurement burden and false-alarm mechanisms are known; alerts can be investigated.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Operating-characteristic calculations approximate real dependence/nonstationarity and cost model includes tail consequences.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Concentrates measurement where it changes decisions and reduces both delay and alert fatigue.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Monitoring designer, operator and risk/economic decision owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Monetised cost models may undervalue safety, trust or irreversible loss; overfitting thresholds to history can fail under shift.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring trade-offs makes “more rules” look unconditionally safer.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Stacking Western Electric rules without capacity; tuning on known incidents; no alert disposition; sampling quota detached from value.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Economic design is powerful but assumption-sensitive and must incorporate non-economic constraints where relevant.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from fixed three-sigma practice to target-shift optimisation, simulations, Bayesian/decision rules and service-capacity-aware alerting.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Select the smallest monitoring design that detects decision-relevant change at acceptable false-alarm/delay cost.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Three sigma, eight run rules or a dashboard cadence are not universal properties.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S014, S013, S065
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S066
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S061, S107
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Cost and shift distributions are often uncertain; robust/minimax or scenario designs may be more honest than a single optimum.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should monitoring be tuned when missed rare events have poorly quantifiable catastrophic loss?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Choose monitoring cadence/statistic/threshold.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Baseline and specified change scenarios with repeated sampling.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable measurement and estimated sampling/response costs.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Operating-characteristic or simulated detection performance.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Alert load, investigation cost and tampering.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Damage accumulated before detection.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Can invalidate expected ARL/delay and cost optimum.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Rules tuned after incidents can overfit and trigger churn.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Manual periodic review or deterministic interlock for obvious/high-consequence states.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Scenario-robust, capacity-aware monitoring with explicit error costs.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What shift is the monitor designed to detect, with what delay and false-alarm burden?
- Can the organisation investigate the alerts generated by added run rules?
- Does another measurement have positive expected decision value?

### P022 — Adapt monitoring to autocorrelation, seasonality, drift and nonstationarity

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P022`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Adapt monitoring to autocorrelation, seasonality, drift and nonstationarity
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Time-series SPC, residual charts, dynamic models, change-point methods and updated time-dependent capability standards.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Replace naive iid chart assumptions with a model or discriminator appropriate to temporal structure and evolving regimes.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Serial dependence changes false-alarm/detection properties; trends and seasonality can look like special causes or mask them.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False alarms, missed shifts, invalid limits and rebaselining every natural cycle.
- **MECHANISM:** Model/stratify predictable structure, monitor residuals/forecast errors or use robust change-point methods; validate under simulation/backtests and maintain drift governance.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Metrics with temporal dependence, seasonality, learning curves, policy changes or concept drift.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** A large deterministic discontinuity can be handled directly; sparse heterogeneous events may need case-based analysis rather than fitted time series.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Enough history/regimes, stable measurement versions, time metadata, intervention records and a prediction/action horizon.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Evaluator/context changes are versioned; missingness and changing exposure denominators are handled.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Dynamic model/residuals capture enough structure; change-point method’s calibration approximates deployment.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Retains monitoring value while reducing spurious alarms in dynamic systems.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Process owner, forecasting/monitoring team and policy controller.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Complex residual models can whiten away meaningful shifts, overfit history or become unauditable.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Naive static limits can become permanently alarmed or falsely calm.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Fitting seasonality after every alarm; residuals with hidden model failure; changing mixtures; no intervention annotation.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** No universal evolved chart exists; classical methods are narrowed, not simply replaced.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from stationary Shewhart limits to local/regime-aware monitoring, forecast residuals, CUSUM/EWMA and online change detection.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use a transparent dynamic baseline with validated detection properties and explicit rebaseline/change-control rules.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** “Use an AI anomaly detector” is not the property; calibrated discrimination and interpretable response remain required.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S026, S030, S067
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S064
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S013, S099
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Under adversarial or abrupt concept change, historical calibration may fail exactly when needed; guarantees are local/conditional.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How can drift detectors distinguish benign adaptation, population change, evaluator change and harmful process degradation?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Detect harmful change in a temporally structured process.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Autocorrelated/seasonal/regime-switching process with interventions.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable timestamped measurement and denominator/context metadata.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Forecast residual, change-point score or regime-conditioned statistic with calibrated delay/error.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Persistent spurious alerts and overfitting.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Slow or model-conforming degradation missed.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Defining feature; calibration expires under unmodelled shifts.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Rebaselining or model refit may erase incidents.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Stratify by obvious season/regime or use a deterministic event rule.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Validated local/dynamic monitor plus independent model-drift checks.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Are iid/stationarity assumptions plausible for the monitored sequence?
- Has seasonality or policy change been modelled rather than called a defect?
- Could residual modelling be hiding a materially changed mechanism?

### P023 — Stratify mixtures, high-mix/low-volume and changing populations before aggregation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P023`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Stratify mixtures, high-mix/low-volume and changing populations before aggregation
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Rational subgrouping, stratification, short-run SPC and mixture-distribution criticism.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ENGINEERING_GENERALISATION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Do not pool unlike products, contexts, users or opportunity structures unless the aggregate answers the decision.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Composition change can create or hide trends even when every component is unchanged.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Simpson-type reversals, impossible common limits, false capability and aggregate metric gaming.
- **MECHANISM:** Define comparable strata or standardise/model mix; use short-run/standardised charts only with justified transformations and preserve component views.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** High-mix low-volume production, heterogeneous workloads, changing severity/population or sparse per-type data.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** If all units share the same decision distribution and mixture is stable/irrelevant, aggregate monitoring may be the cheapest valid path.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Product/context identifiers, enough data or engineering standards for normalisation, and a clear aggregate decision.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement is invariant or separately calibrated across strata.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Within-stratum models or standardisation preserve relevant tail/interaction behaviour; no hidden confounding remains.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents composition effects from being attributed to process performance.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Portfolio/process owner and consumers of aggregate dashboards.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-stratification produces tiny samples, multiplicity and paralysis; arbitrary normalisation can hide absolute harm.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Under-stratification misses local defects and unfairly rewards favourable mix.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Dropping hard categories; changing denominator; z-scoring incomparable tails; one global control limit; post-hoc subgroup hunting.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Short-run charts help but are domain-specific and assumption-sensitive; hierarchical pooling may be preferable.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from physical product family grouping to hierarchical/partial-pooling and mix-adjusted monitoring.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Show both relevant strata and an explicitly weighted aggregate; freeze/justify weighting and monitor composition.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A mandatory chart per SKU or one universal standardised score are both ceremonial extremes.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S002, S067, S107
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S099
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S039
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** With very sparse strata, estimates depend strongly on hierarchical priors/model sharing; no purely data-driven partition is definitive.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should partial pooling be governed when local safety tails matter more than overall efficiency?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Detect change without confusing it with mixture/composition change.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Mixture of heterogeneous strata with possibly changing weights.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stratum identity, invariant measurement and denominators.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Within-stratum or mix-standardised change plus composition monitor.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Multiple alerts and false subgroup discoveries.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Local degradation hidden by favourable mix.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Mixture weights and strata can evolve.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Changing weights/exclusions can manufacture improvement.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Monitor the dominant/high-risk strata and a transparent aggregate.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Hierarchical or stratified monitoring with fixed/justified weights.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Could a changed product/user/case mix explain the aggregate trend?
- Are hard strata being excluded or down-weighted after failures?
- Does standardisation preserve the absolute engineering requirement?

### P024 — Use context-adapted multivariate, rare-event, short-run or change-point monitoring only when its added discrimination pays

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P024`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use context-adapted multivariate, rare-event, short-run or change-point monitoring only when its added discrimination pays
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `VARIATION_STABILITY_AND_CONTROL`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** CUSUM/EWMA, multivariate SPC, attribute/rare-event charts, short-run methods and modern change detection.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Choose a monitoring statistic matched to event type, dimension, baseline size and target change rather than force a classical chart.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Univariate normal charts can miss joint shifts, mis-handle sparse events, or be unusable with one-off/high-mix output.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Tool mismatch, multiplicity across dashboards, tail overconfidence and opaque anomaly scores.
- **MECHANISM:** Specify the change/event to detect, compare candidate methods by simulated/empirical operating characteristics, and keep a causal/actionable interpretation.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Multivariate correlated measurements, rare defects, variable exposure, small shifts, low volume or abrupt change points.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Use a direct rule or simple stratified count when the event is obvious and data too sparse to estimate a complex model.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable measurement, exposure denominators, enough baseline or engineering prior, response plan and computational/interpretive capacity.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Input variables retain meaning and covariance/event models are adequate enough for threshold calibration.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Selected detector’s null/change model or robust calibration approximates use; multiplicity across monitored features is controlled.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Improves sensitivity where classical charts are structurally mismatched.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Monitoring team and incident/process owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Complex detectors create opaque false alarms, overfit baselines and encourage dashboard proliferation.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Simple charts can miss joint/slow/rare changes with serious consequences.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Hotelling score without diagnosis; Poisson chart under overdispersion; zero-event reassurance; model retrained after every alarm.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Hotelling score without diagnosis; Poisson chart under overdispersion; zero-event reassurance; model retrained after every alarm. Contrary evidence/limit: Rare-event detector performance is often weakly estimable; simulation depends on an assumed tail/model and can create false certainty.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Extensions retain the underlying discrimination property but remain domain/model-specific.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Adopt only after showing incremental decision value over a cheap baseline, with interpretable follow-up and recalibration rules.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Having a multivariate/anomaly model is not a general property; validated added discrimination is.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S013, S061, S107, S108
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S030, S031, S073
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S099
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Rare-event detector performance is often weakly estimable; simulation depends on an assumed tail/model and can create false certainty.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What evidence threshold is sufficient to deploy a complex detector when incidents are too rare for direct validation?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Select a detector for multivariate, sparse, small-shift or low-volume change.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Context-specific joint/event/regime model, often with limited baseline data.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable features, denominators, covariance/event definitions and protected calibration data.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Detector score calibrated to target change and error costs.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Opaque alert burden, multiple-testing inflation and overfit.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Joint/rare harmful shift missed by simple marginal rules.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Model/calibration particularly fragile under shift.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Retraining after incidents can absorb failures.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct count/rule or expert review when data cannot support a complex detector.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Incremental-value-tested specialised monitor with interpretable diagnosis.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What specific change does the specialised detector find that a cheaper rule misses?
- Are rare-event or covariance assumptions supported?
- Can an alert be translated into an actionable investigation rather than an opaque score?

### P025 — Design sampling to represent the decision population and selection process

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P025`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Design sampling to represent the decision population and selection process
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `SAMPLING_AND_INFORMATION_QUANTITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Probability sampling, industrial inspection, survey/experimental design and modern dataset-shift analysis.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `STATISTICAL_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Define the target population/opportunity frame and select units so inclusion supports the intended inference.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A large convenient sample can be systematically unrepresentative, duplicated or selected after outcomes are visible.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Selection bias, survivorship bias, spectrum restriction and false precision from nonprobability coverage.
- **MECHANISM:** Specify frame, inclusion probabilities or explicit selection model; stratify high-risk/rare groups; document exclusions and compare sample to target.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Any inference beyond the exactly observed units, especially acceptance, prevalence, reliability or benchmark claims.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For exhaustive authoritative enumeration of the entire relevant population, statistical sampling is unnecessary; verify completeness/identity instead.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined target population, unit/opportunity, frame coverage, provenance and a plan for nonresponse/missingness.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement meaning is invariant enough across sampled and target contexts; duplicates and clusters are identified.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Probability or selection-model assumptions hold sufficiently; unobserved selection is bounded or disclosed.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Makes uncertainty and transfer claims correspond to the population that drives the decision.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experimenter, quality/reliability analyst, customer/regulator and benchmark consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Complex representative sampling may cost more than direct inspection or targeted risk sampling; strict probability designs may undersample rare hazards.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Convenience samples can miss failure modes no amount of replication recovers.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Public/easy cases only; post-failure exclusion; duplicated source records; favourable time window; target population left implicit.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Probability sampling does not repair invalid measurement, and nonprobability samples can still support bounded local decisions if claims are restricted.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved toward risk-stratified, cluster-aware, importance-weighted and shift-monitored sampling while preserving explicit target definition.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use the cheapest design that either represents the target or honestly limits the claim; retain all attempted-unit provenance.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A sample-size quota without a target/frame is ceremony.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S045, S020, S068
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S039, S099
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S087
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Open-ended deployment populations cannot be fully represented in advance; monitoring and scenario coverage remain necessary.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should rare catastrophic strata be oversampled while preserving interpretable overall estimates?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Estimate or compare performance for a defined target population.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independently sampled unit or explicitly clustered unit from the frame.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Sampling clusters/duplicates and repeated units must be modelled.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Random selection or transparent risk/stratified design; treatment randomisation is a separate issue.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Stratify by expected heterogeneity, rarity or decision loss.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Replication means new sampled units, not repeated measurement of the same unit.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Sample composition can interact with candidate/treatment performance.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Pre-specify frame, inclusion/exclusion and replacement rules.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Provides population-relevant uncertainty and exposes coverage gaps.
- **COST:** Frame construction, oversampling and follow-up.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Overengineered probability sampling may waste effort for local deterministic decisions.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Explicit target/frame with representative or claim-limited sampling.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What exact population and opportunity frame does the sample represent?
- Could selection, survival or duplication explain the result?
- Would exhaustive deterministic checking be cheaper or more authoritative?

### P026 — Use acceptance sampling only for bounded lot-disposition decisions where inspection cost or destruction justifies it

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P026`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use acceptance sampling only for bounded lot-disposition decisions where inspection cost or destruction justifies it
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `SAMPLING_AND_INFORMATION_QUANTITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Dodge–Romig Bell Labs sampling inspection, wartime/industrial standards and current ISO 2859.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `DIRECT_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Accept or reject a lot using a prespecified sampling plan with producer/consumer operating risks.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** 100% inspection may be destructive, too slow, too expensive or itself error-prone.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Arbitrary lot release, hidden risk transfer and pretending a sample proves every item conforms.
- **MECHANISM:** Choose plan by lot structure, AQL/LQ or OC requirements, inspection error and cost; preserve switching/traceability and disposition semantics.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Incoming/outgoing lots with meaningful lot identity, relatively stable supply and costly/destructive inspection.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Use 100% deterministic inspection when cheap/reliable; use process control/improvement when the goal is future quality rather than one-lot disposition.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined lot, random sample, valid attribute/variable measurement, prespecified plan and clear producer/consumer risk.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Inspection error is small or included; sample selection cannot be manipulated.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Within-lot heterogeneity and production history are compatible with the plan; standard’s switching rules/context apply.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Controls transaction risk at lower inspection cost and limits destructive sampling.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Purchaser/supplier, release authority and regulator.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** A sampling plan can accept bad items/lots by design and may institutionalise tolerable defects or adversarial lot formation.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Overly small/lenient plans expose consumers to unacceptable lots.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** AQL interpreted as a guarantee; nonrandom sample; lot splitting; plan chosen after results; inspection error ignored.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: AQL interpreted as a guarantee; nonrandom sample; lot splitting; plan chosen after results; inspection error ignored. Contrary evidence/limit: Changing lot composition, adversarial suppliers and severe item heterogeneity can invalidate nominal OC risks.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern standards retain the tool but explicitly limit it to acceptance decisions, not process estimation or prevention.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** A domain-specific decision procedure with transparent OC/risk—not a universal quality property.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** ISO table use or a named MIL-STD plan is an implementation; bounded risk-based lot disposition is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `DOMAIN_SPECIFIC`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S008, S068, S069
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S070, S027
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S061
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Changing lot composition, adversarial suppliers and severe item heterogeneity can invalidate nominal OC risks.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** When do modern automated inspections make classical sampling economically obsolete?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | LOW |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Accept/reject a finite lot under inspection cost and error risk.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Finite lot with random sampled items and specified defect model/OC curve.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Reliable item classification and auditable random sample.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Acceptance number/plan with stated producer and consumer risks.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Rejecting good lot, delay and destructive cost.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Accepting bad lot or catastrophic defective item.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Lot heterogeneity/formation changes nominal risks.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Switching or relabelling lots after failure can game the plan.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** 100% reliable automated inspection or direct supplier process evidence.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Prespecified OC-based plan restricted to lot disposition.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Is the decision one-lot disposition, rather than process improvement or a universal quality claim?
- Are sample selection, lot identity and inspection error controlled?
- Would 100% authoritative inspection now be cheaper or safer?

### P027 — Do not use acceptance sampling as a substitute for process understanding, prevention or improvement

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P027`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Do not use acceptance sampling as a substitute for process understanding, prevention or improvement
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `SAMPLING_AND_INFORMATION_QUANTITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Limits recognised in acceptance-sampling handbooks and the shift from end inspection toward process control/prevention.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `QUALITY_ANCESTRY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Separate transaction screening from evidence about why a process generates defects and how future output will behave.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Passing lots can coexist with a poor, drifting or gamed process; rejected lots do not identify the cause.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Inspection dependence, hidden scrap/rework, no learning and claims that accepted samples establish process capability.
- **MECHANISM:** Pair lot disposition with supplier/process feedback, time-ordered defect analysis, corrective action or designed improvement where economically justified.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Repeated supply/production where future quality matters, not merely one isolated transaction.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a one-off purchase with no leverage or future process, bounded acceptance sampling may be all that is rational.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Lot and process identities linked, defect provenance retained, and process owner able to act.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement system distinguishes defects and records failure modes rather than only pass/fail totals.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Lot outcomes contain some information about process, but causal diagnosis requires additional evidence.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Converts inspection cost into learning and prevents sampling plans from becoming a quality ceiling.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Supplier/process owner, procurement and improvement team.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Demanding improvement evidence can be uneconomic for commodity/one-off purchases.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Pure screening leaves recurrent systemic defects untouched.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Discarding rejected-lot data; treating AQL as target; supplier adjusts lot formation; no root-cause evidence.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Discarding rejected-lot data; treating AQL as target; supplier adjusts lot formation; no root-cause evidence. Contrary evidence/limit: For highly regulated or destructive products, acceptance evidence may remain necessary even with excellent process control.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** The evolved form retains acceptance sampling only as one control layer, subordinated to prevention and feedback.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use sampling for the decision it can make; do not infer control, capability or cause from acceptance alone.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Acceptance forms/tables are not process-improvement properties.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S068, S070, S090
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S031, S084
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S061
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** For highly regulated or destructive products, acceptance evidence may remain necessary even with excellent process control.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What combination of process evidence and lot inspection minimises total risk under supplier/adversarial uncertainty?
