- Are rejected and sampled defects fed back into process learning?
- Is future process improvement economically relevant to this decision?

### P028 — Size samples around effect/precision, dependence and decision loss—not a universal count

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P028`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Size samples around effect/precision, dependence and decision loss—not a universal count
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `SAMPLING_AND_INFORMATION_QUANTITY`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Neyman–Pearson power, estimation precision, sampling theory, cluster design and reliability bounds.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `STATISTICAL_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Choose information volume from the smallest meaningful effect or required precision, error costs, variance, design and stopping plan.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Too few independent units miss meaningful effects; many dependent or biased units create only false precision.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Underpowered/equivocal experiments, inflated cost, post-hoc stopping and “large n” used to excuse bad design.
- **MECHANISM:** Specify decision margin, alpha/beta or interval width/utility, estimate design effects, use simulation/sensitivity, and distinguish units from repeated measurements.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Sampling/experiment/reliability decisions where uncertainty influences action.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** No sample-size calculation is needed when a deterministic proof/check resolves the decision or when a pilot’s only goal is feasibility discovery.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined estimand/decision, plausible variance/effect range, independent-unit structure, multiplicity/stopping plan and feasible measurement.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement error and missingness are included in effective information; pilot estimates are not treated as exact.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Planning model approximates design; effect threshold is engineering-justified; clusters/dependence known enough.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Avoids both information-starved decisions and wasteful quota measurement.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experiment owner, risk owner and resource allocator.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Rigid power targets can encourage guessed effect sizes, oversized studies for trivial effects, or neglect of catastrophic rare modes.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Small effective n yields unstable estimates and inconclusive negatives.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Counting repeated readings as n; planning on observed pilot effect; stopping when significant; ignoring multiplicity/attrition.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Counting repeated readings as n; planning on observed pilot effect; stopping when significant; ignoring multiplicity/attrition. Contrary evidence/limit: Rare catastrophic outcomes may require physical/structural evidence, conservative bounds or stress models because feasible samples cannot directly validate target rates.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from fixed-horizon power calculations toward precision, simulation, sequential/decision-theoretic and assurance approaches.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Plan for the independent information needed to discriminate the decision, update only under a valid sequential/adaptive protocol.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** “30 samples,” “10 runs,” or “80% power” without a decision model is ceremony.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S007, S046, S075
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S023, S042
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S073
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Rare catastrophic outcomes may require physical/structural evidence, conservative bounds or stress models because feasible samples cannot directly validate target rates.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should sample planning combine epistemic uncertainty in variance/effect with asymmetric catastrophic loss?

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Obtain enough independent information to choose among actions or bound risk.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent experimental/sample unit at the decision scale.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Clusters, repeated measures and common seeds reduce effective n.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Assignment/sampling design determines variance and bias; randomisation where causal.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Use stratification/blocking when it reduces nuisance variance without hiding heterogeneity.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** New independent units; technical repeats mainly estimate measurement error.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Interactions/heterogeneity increase required coverage and can invalidate one average effect.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Fixed horizon or formally planned sequential/adaptive rule.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Decision discrimination or interval precision per unit cost.
- **COST:** Units, measurement, delay and opportunity cost.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Formulaic power can become a quota and reward trivial-effect detection.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Simulation/sensitivity-based planning with explicit effect, dependence, loss and stopping.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What independent sample size—not raw observation count—supports the decision?
- What smallest engineering-relevant effect or precision target was used?
- Would a sequential or deterministic path reach the decision more cheaply?

### P029 — Frame experiments around an explicit engineering decision, estimand and rival alternatives

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P029`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Frame experiments around an explicit engineering decision, estimand and rival alternatives
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Fisherian experiment purpose, Neyman–Pearson specified alternatives, causal estimands and statistical engineering.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** State what contrast/effect/population and action the experiment is intended to discriminate before selecting a test.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A technically valid analysis can answer an irrelevant null, average over decisive heterogeneity, or lack an action consequence.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Significance-test ritual, moving hypotheses, post-hoc target selection and experiments that generate numbers but no decision.
- **MECHANISM:** Define decision, estimand, experimental unit, rival explanations, smallest consequential effect, validity scope and stopping/analysis plan.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Any nontrivial comparison or intervention whose result may change design, release, allocation or policy.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For an exact authoritative check or exploratory feasibility probe, a formal estimand/test may be unnecessary; label the evidence accordingly.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stakeholder/engineering loss, target population, manipulable treatment/design alternatives and measurable outcome.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Outcome is valid and sensitive enough; unit/assignment/exposure can be identified.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The chosen estimand maps to the decision and potential outcomes/comparisons are meaningful.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents expensive but non-decisive experimentation and makes negative/ambiguous outcomes interpretable.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Design authority, product/process owner and risk/resource decision maker.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Prematurely narrow estimands can miss discovery or emergent failure modes.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Vague goals permit post-hoc success definitions and false causal stories.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Null of exactly zero; endpoint switched after results; no target population; average effect hides catastrophic subgroup.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** No universal formalism resolves normative loss or construct definition; exploratory and confirmatory phases may differ.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from hypothesis testing toward explicit estimands, decision margins, rival models and staged exploration/confirmation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** No experiment begins as “run a test”; it begins with the decision it can change and the cheapest evidence that discriminates it.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A preregistration form or test name is optional; frozen decision/estimand boundaries are the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S005, S007, S018
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S033, S042
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S044, S080
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Some engineering discovery is necessarily open-ended; the mature boundary is transparent separation of exploration from decision-confirming evidence.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should multi-objective engineering decisions state estimands without hiding value trade-offs in a scalar?

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Choose among stated engineering alternatives using a defined effect/estimand.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Unit receiving the intervention/design condition.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** The unit—not each output—is the independent replication basis unless a justified hierarchy is modelled.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise or justify a controlled quasi-experimental assignment.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block nuisance variation relevant to precision/validity.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** New independently assigned units across decision-relevant conditions.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Specify interactions that could reverse the average decision.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Fixed or planned sequential rule tied to the estimand.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Expected reduction in uncertainty between rival actions.
- **COST:** Experiment units, delay, risk and opportunity.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Over-formal framing can suppress exploratory learning.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Staged exploratory/confirmatory design with explicit estimand, decision margin and scope.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What exact decision and rival alternatives will the experiment discriminate?
- Is the estimand aligned with the unit/population that will receive the action?
- Would a cheaper authoritative observation settle the question?

### P030 — Use randomisation to protect causal comparisons against assignment bias and support valid error assessment

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P030`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use randomisation to protect causal comparisons against assignment bias and support valid error assessment
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Fisher’s field experiments, randomised experiments and potential-outcomes causal inference.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Allocate experimental units by a known chance mechanism so treatment is not systematically confounded with observed/unobserved pre-treatment factors.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Convenient or outcome-informed assignment can make association look causal.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Selection bias, time/order confounding and invalid reference distributions.
- **MECHANISM:** Randomise at the correct unit, conceal/record allocation, account for restrictions/clusters, and analyse according to the actual assignment.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** When interventions are feasible and the causal effect matters.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Randomisation is unnecessary for deterministic mechanism verification and may be infeasible/unethical; strong natural/quasi-experimental or mechanistic evidence can be used with narrower claims.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined experimental unit, feasible comparable conditions, stable measurement, compliance/exposure records and enough independent assignments.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Outcome ascertainment is blinded or otherwise protected from allocation bias where possible.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** No unmodelled interference/carryover; randomisation is implemented, not merely planned; attrition does not undo comparability.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Supports causal discrimination and honest uncertainty with fewer modelling assumptions about assignment.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experimenter, design/process owner and causal decision consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Randomisation can be costly, expose units to inferior conditions, or average over important heterogeneity; chance imbalance remains possible.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Without it, hidden assignment bias may dominate even large samples.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Randomising observations instead of units; deterministic seed reused; allocation predictable; post-randomisation exclusions; interference ignored.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Randomisation does not solve invalid measurement, low power, noncompliance, spillover, external validity or poor estimands.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved into cluster, stratified, covariate-adaptive and online randomisation with transparent assignment logs.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Randomise when it materially reduces causal ambiguity; preserve assignment integrity and analyse the design actually run.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** “A/B test” branding or a random seed alone is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S005, S006, S018
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S047, S048
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S044, S037
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Randomised effects may not transport beyond sampled units/context, and operational noncompliance can change the estimand.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** When is mechanism-plus-observational evidence decision-sufficient despite infeasible randomisation?

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Estimate the causal effect of an intervention/design alternative.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Unit assigned to treatment/control.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Units are independent or clustering/interference is designed and analysed.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Known recorded random allocation; concealment/blinding where relevant.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Restricted/stratified randomisation for major nuisance factors.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent assigned units; repeated outcomes are within-unit data.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Treatment effects may vary by block/context; pre-specify important modifiers.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Fixed or design-valid sequential boundaries.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Removes assignment confounding in expectation and supports randomisation inference.
- **COST:** Exposure to alternatives, sample/time and operational disruption.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Randomising trivial questions can waste resources or violate safety constraints.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Correct-unit randomisation plus measurement, compliance, interference and transport safeguards.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Was allocation randomised at the actual intervention unit?
- Could order, selection, attrition or exposure violate the assignment comparison?
- What causal ambiguity remains despite randomisation?

### P031 — Block or stratify known nuisance variation without erasing interactions

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P031`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Block or stratify known nuisance variation without erasing interactions
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Fisher’s local control/blocking, factorial design and modern stratified/randomised experiments.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Group or balance experimental units on nuisance factors before randomisation/analysis to reduce unexplained variance and confounding.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Batch, time, operator, hardware, site or baseline severity can obscure or mimic treatment effects.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Low precision, imbalance, order effects and effects confounded with known nuisance structure.
- **MECHANISM:** Select a few consequential pre-treatment factors, block/stratify randomisation and estimate block/treatment interactions when relevant.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Heterogeneous units or expensive experiments where nuisance variation is large relative to target effect.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Do not block on post-treatment variables or add complexity when units are already homogeneous and the effect is obvious.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Known pre-treatment covariates, enough units per block or matched-pair structure, and stable measurement.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Blocking variables are measured before intervention without candidate-induced bias.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Within-block comparisons remain meaningful; block definitions are not chosen after outcomes.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Increases precision and protects comparisons, often reducing required runs.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experimenter and engineering decision owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Too many/tiny blocks reduce degrees of freedom, break randomisation or hide generalisation; bad matching can amplify bias.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** No blocking may leave a cheap major source of variation unaddressed.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Blocking treatment with time unintentionally; post-hoc subgrouping; using seed as “replication” but not block; forgetting block interactions.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Blocking treatment with time unintentionally; post-hoc subgrouping; using seed as “replication” but not block; forgetting block interactions. Contrary evidence/limit: When nuisance variables are numerous/high-dimensional, model-based adjustment may overfit; design-stage balance remains finite.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern practice combines blocking with regression/hierarchical adjustment but retains design-stage protection.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Block what is known and costly, randomise what remains, and report context interactions rather than only an adjusted average.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A Latin square or mandatory stratification table is optional; nuisance-control efficiency is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S005, S006, S043
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S047, S048
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S044, S049
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** When nuisance variables are numerous/high-dimensional, model-based adjustment may overfit; design-stage balance remains finite.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Which factors merit blocking versus deliberate exploration as potential interactions?

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Estimate an effect with reduced nuisance variance and confounding.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Unit within a pre-treatment block/stratum.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Independence across units/blocks or explicit cluster model.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise within blocks or use justified matched assignment.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Core mechanism: predefine blocks on consequential nuisance factors.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent units within/among blocks; technical repeats do not replace units.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Treatment×block interactions may limit generalisation.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Predefine block construction and stopping.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Higher precision per run and protection from known imbalance.
- **COST:** Design complexity and need for enough units per block.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Overblocking/empty cells and post-hoc stratification.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Sparse, pre-treatment blocking plus interaction-aware analysis.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Were known batch, time, operator, site or severity effects blocked/stratified?
- Was any block defined after outcomes or affected by treatment?
- Could the treatment effect reverse across blocks?

### P032 — Require independent replication at the experimental-unit level; treat technical repeats as measurement information

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P032`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Require independent replication at the experimental-unit level; treat technical repeats as measurement information
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Fisherian replication, hierarchical experimental design and Hurlbert’s pseudoreplication critique.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Repeat treatments on genuinely independent units so variability relevant to generalisation is observed.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Many outputs, seeds, subsamples or repeated readings from one unit can look like a large experiment while providing one treatment assignment.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Underestimated uncertainty, false significance, fragile “6/6” claims and inability to distinguish unit effects from treatment effects.
- **MECHANISM:** Define unit/cluster, allocate multiple independent units per condition, model nesting/repeated measures and label technical replication explicitly.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Any claim intended to generalise beyond one specimen, run, model instance, batch, site or environment.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For deterministic proof or exact exhaustive checks, replication is unnecessary; for measurement precision, technical repeats remain useful but answer a different question.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Multiple independent units, provenance showing common sources, and a stable treatment/evaluator protocol.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Repeated measurement error is separated from between-unit variation; evaluator exposures do not couple units.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Units are exchangeable/representative enough for intended scope; clusters and interference are modelled.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Produces honest uncertainty and tests robustness across naturally varying units.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experimenter, reviewer and engineering decision authority.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Independent replication can be expensive or impossible for unique systems; demanding it mechanically may block mechanistic evidence.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Pseudo-replication creates arbitrarily narrow intervals without additional independent information.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Same seed/environment; multiple metrics from one run; split observations from one batch; repeated test after adaptation; duplicated data lineage.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Replication count is not enough: independence, representativeness and measurement validity remain prerequisites.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved into multilevel models, cluster-robust inference, multisite replication and explicit technical/biological/system-level replication labels.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Count independent decision-relevant units, disclose dependence, and stop treating correlated reruns as corroboration.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A fixed “number of replications” is ceremony; independence at the right level is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S005, S006, S023
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S032, S038
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S044, S039
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** For singular large engineered systems, evidence may need component replication, simulation validation and mechanistic argument rather than classical independent system copies.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What constitutes independent replication for stochastic software/agent systems sharing training data, code and evaluator?

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Generalise a treatment/design effect beyond one realised unit.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent specimen/run/site/model instance/batch receiving a condition.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Technical repeats and outputs nested within units are correlated.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise conditions across independent units when causal.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block by known shared source while retaining multiple units per condition.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** New units with independent exposure and natural variation.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Treatment×unit/context interactions reveal transfer limits.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Set independent-unit target or valid sequential rule before outcome review.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Estimates between-unit variability and robustness.
- **COST:** Independent units may dominate total cost.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Mechanical replication quotas or fake seed replication.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Hierarchical design that labels technical, unit, batch and site replication separately.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What is the actual independently assigned or sampled unit?
- Are multiple outputs, seeds or reviews from one underlying unit being counted as separate experiments?
- Does shared data, evaluator or environment create common-mode dependence?

### P033 — Use factorial structure to discover interactions and avoid one-factor-at-a-time ambiguity

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P033`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use factorial structure to discover interactions and avoid one-factor-at-a-time ambiguity
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Fisherian factorial designs and industrial DOE.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Vary multiple factors jointly so main effects and interactions can be estimated efficiently.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** One-factor-at-a-time trials miss synergies/antagonisms and can recommend settings that fail in combination.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Confounded design choices, local conclusions and excessive runs for weak information.
- **MECHANISM:** Use full or suitable fractional factorial designs, randomisation/blocking and hierarchy/sparsity only as stated assumptions; confirm consequential interactions.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Multiple controllable factors with plausible interactions and experiments costly enough that structured coverage matters.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For one known dominant factor, deterministic mechanism or a tiny safe search space that can be exhaustively tested, formal factorial DOE may be excessive.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable factor levels, adequate measurement, feasible combinations, enough units, and a response meaningful over the design region.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Outcome/evaluator remains comparable across combinations; invalid/impossible combinations identified before randomisation.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Model hierarchy/sparsity or sufficient full design; no uncontrolled time/batch confounding.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Finds interactions with fewer runs than sequential one-factor searches and improves design robustness.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Design/process engineer and optimiser.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Factorial grids can explode, include unrealistic combinations, and overfit interaction terms with limited replication.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring interactions can produce brittle settings and false “best factor” claims.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** OFAT; no randomisation; main effects interpreted despite strong interactions; multiple responses cherry-picked; no confirmation.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Factorial design is not synonymous with all experimentation; interactions outside tested levels/context remain unknown.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved into screening, optimal/computer designs, sequential augmentation and hierarchical modelling.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use factorial structure when interaction ambiguity is decision-relevant; confirm in the intended operating region.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A full 2^k table is optional; efficient interaction-identifying contrast structure is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S006, S043, S049
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S010, S050
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S044
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** High-dimensional systems may violate effect sparsity/hierarchy and contain discontinuities; adaptive or mechanistic designs may outperform classical factorials.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should factor screening incorporate safety constraints and rare catastrophic interactions?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LOW |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Estimate main effects and interactions among controllable factors.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent run/unit at a specified factor combination.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Runs independent or blocked; repeated readings nested.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise run order subject to safety/logistics.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block batch/day/machine or use split-plot structure when factor changes differ in cost.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent runs across combinations; confirmatory runs at chosen settings.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Core target; hierarchy/sparsity assumptions must be explicit.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Preplan analysis and reserve confirmation; sequential augmentation allowed by rule.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** High information per run about combinations and robustness.
- **COST:** Number of combinations, setup changes and unsafe regions.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Overbuilt factorials and spurious high-order interactions.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Smallest design that identifies decision-relevant interactions plus confirmation.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Could interactions reverse the conclusion drawn from isolated factor changes?
- Is the design able to separate main effects from important interactions?
- Would exhaustive/deterministic testing be cheaper than a formal DOE here?

### P034 — Use fractional factorial/screening designs only with explicit alias and sparsity assumptions

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P034`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use fractional factorial/screening designs only with explicit alias and sparsity assumptions
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Plackett–Burman, fractional factorial resolution theory and industrial screening.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Screen many factors with fewer runs by accepting known confounding/alias structure.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A low-resolution design can mistake interactions for main effects or declare inactive factors under false sparsity.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Confident but aliased conclusions, discarded causal factors and optimisation on a contaminated screening model.
- **MECHANISM:** Choose resolution by plausible interactions, inspect alias matrix, randomise/block, use foldover/augmentation or independent confirmation.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Early screening with many candidate factors, limited runs and a credible effect-sparsity/hierarchy hypothesis.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Use full factorial, mechanistic screening or direct elimination when interactions are dense/critical or factors are few.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Known factor ranges, stable measurement, feasible design matrix, predeclared alias tolerance and resources for follow-up.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement error small enough that sparse effects are not buried; factor settings are reproducible.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Few active effects, high-order interactions negligible or follow-up can resolve ambiguity.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Reduces experimental burden while identifying promising factors for later study.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Screening experiment owner and design team.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** False economy if follow-up is omitted; alias assumptions can be chosen after results to tell a preferred story.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Avoiding screening may make broad factor exploration infeasible.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Resolution III main effects treated as clean; no foldover; factors dropped before interaction check; no confirmation.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Resolution III main effects treated as clean; no foldover; factors dropped before interaction check; no confirmation. Contrary evidence/limit: When effect sparsity fails or factors are adaptive/continuous, screening rankings can be unstable and model-dependent.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern practice uses sequential augmentation, Bayesian/sparsity models and optimal designs but cannot eliminate aliasing without information.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Treat screening results as provisional, report aliases, and resolve any ambiguity that could change the decision.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A Plackett–Burman array or “DOE software result” is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S010, S043, S044
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S049, S050
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S102
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** When effect sparsity fails or factors are adaptive/continuous, screening rankings can be unstable and model-dependent.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should alias risk be priced when a missed interaction has severe safety consequences?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Screen many factors to select a smaller follow-up set.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent run/unit at a design row.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Runs independent/blocked; no hidden batch alias.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise rows subject to restrictions.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Account for hard-to-change factors with split-plot/blocks.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Usually limited; follow-up/foldover supplies discrimination.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Aliasing with interactions is the central risk.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Precommit alias interpretation and follow-up trigger.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Broad factor information per run under sparsity.
- **COST:** Follow-up burden and risk of missed factors.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Treating provisional aliases as causal truths.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Resolution-aware screening plus sequential de-aliasing and confirmation.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What effects are aliased, and which assumptions make the reported effect interpretable?
- Was ambiguous screening evidence independently confirmed or augmented?
- Could a plausible interaction contaminate the selected main effect?

### P035 — Use response surfaces and iterative experimentation as local learning, with confirmation and boundary discipline

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P035`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use response surfaces and iterative experimentation as local learning, with confirmation and boundary discipline
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Box–Wilson response-surface methodology, Box/Hunter sequential experimentation and Shewhart/Box iterative science.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Build local empirical models, move toward promising regions, augment the design and confirm the selected operating point.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A one-shot optimum from sparse data can be a model artefact; unstructured trial-and-error wastes runs and hides stopping bias.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Overfit optima, extrapolation, hill-climbing into unsafe regions and confirmation on reused data.
- **MECHANISM:** Start with screening/local design, check curvature/residuals, proceed sequentially under a stated rule, respect constraints, and reserve independent confirmation/robustness tests.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Continuous controllable factors, expensive runs and an objective surface smooth enough locally.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For discrete deterministic search spaces that are cheap to enumerate or when a mechanistic model fixes the optimum, RSM is unnecessary.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable measurement, controllable factors, safe region, adequate local design, model diagnostics and fresh confirmation capacity.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Outcome definition/threshold remains stable across stages; process drift is monitored.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Local low-order approximation is adequate within each step; sequential data reuse is reflected in uncertainty.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Finds useful settings efficiently while learning curvature/interactions.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Design/process engineer and optimisation decision owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Sequential flexibility can overfit and obscure error rates; local optima may be fragile or nontransportable.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Rigid one-shot designs can miss the region where information is valuable.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Reporting fitted optimum without confirmation; extrapolating beyond design; stopping after favourable stage; no noise-factor test.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Reporting fitted optimum without confirmation; extrapolating beyond design; stopping after favourable stage; no noise-factor test. Contrary evidence/limit: Adaptive optimisers can exploit evaluator noise and drift; repeated use of the same validation surface erodes confirmation value.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern practice hybridises RSM with Bayesian optimisation/adaptive design, but still needs protected validation and decision costs.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Iterate models as provisional maps; require confirmation in the intended context and robustness around the selected point.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** RSM contour plots or a fixed phase sequence are tools; sequential information-efficient learning is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S009, S021, S049
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S044, S050
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S080
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Adaptive optimisers can exploit evaluator noise and drift; repeated use of the same validation surface erodes confirmation value.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What degree of fresh confirmation is needed after highly adaptive optimisation?

| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Locate/compare an operating region or optimum under continuous factors.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent run/unit at selected settings.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Sequential runs may be dependent through adaptation; observation noise independent conditional on design or modelled.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise/localise run order where drift permits; adaptive selection is recorded.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block nuisance factors and include centre/replicate points.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent runs and protected confirmation at final settings.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Curvature and interactions are core; unknown high-order behaviour outside region.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Stage/decision rule plus independent confirmation, not “stop when best.”
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** High local information gain and efficient movement toward useful region.
- **COST:** Runs, setup changes, risk near boundaries and confirmation.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Adaptive overfit, local optimum and ceremonial contour fitting.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Constraint-aware sequential local modelling with model checks, robustness and fresh confirmation.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Was the reported optimum independently confirmed in the intended operating region?
- Did sequential adaptation preserve a stopping/validation boundary?
- Could the optimiser be exploiting evaluator noise or an unsafe extrapolation?

### P036 — Report effect magnitude, uncertainty and engineering significance; use equivalence/noninferiority when “no important difference” is the decision

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P036`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Report effect magnitude, uncertainty and engineering significance; use equivalence/noninferiority when “no important difference” is the decision
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Neyman–Pearson power, estimation/tolerance practice, modern p-value criticism and equivalence testing.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `STATISTICAL_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Interpret estimated magnitude and uncertainty against a predeclared engineering consequence/margin, not only a null-test threshold.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Tiny effects become “significant” with large n, while meaningful effects are dismissed after inconclusive small studies; non-significance is called equivalence.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Statistical/practical-significance confusion, false “no difference,” and optimisation of trivial changes.
- **MECHANISM:** Define smallest effect of interest or acceptance margin; report interval/distribution and decision consequences; use equivalence/noninferiority design where appropriate.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Comparisons where action depends on magnitude, not merely direction, and negative results may support interchangeability.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For deterministic threshold compliance or an overwhelming effect far from uncertainty, a p-value may add nothing.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Valid measurement, meaningful scale/margin, representative units, adequate information and multiplicity/stopping control.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Metric resolution and uncertainty are small enough relative to the engineering margin.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Model/interval coverage is adequate; equivalence margin is fixed independently of observed results.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Aligns inference with engineering consequence and distinguishes absence of detection from evidence of negligible effect.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Design/process owner, customer, regulator and risk decision maker.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Arbitrary “practical” margins can be chosen to obtain a desired conclusion; focus on average effect can hide tails/subgroups.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Threshold-only testing can miss consequential uncertainty or reward minuscule gains.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** p<.05 presented without units; CI crossing zero called “same”; post-hoc equivalence margin; relative change hiding absolute harm.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: p<.05 presented without units; CI crossing zero called “same”; post-hoc equivalence margin; relative change hiding absolute harm. Contrary evidence/limit: Engineering-significance margins can be multi-dimensional and context-dependent; scalar margins may conceal trade-offs or rare harms.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** ASA-era guidance preserved inferential tools but rejected single-threshold interpretation; equivalence methods sharpen negative claims.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Show effect, uncertainty, smallest consequential magnitude, and decision; reserve “equivalent” for designs that test a justified margin.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A particular effect-size statistic or ban on p-values is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S007, S029, S041
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S033, S034, S035, S042
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S046
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Engineering-significance margins can be multi-dimensional and context-dependent; scalar margins may conceal trade-offs or rare harms.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should practical-significance criteria aggregate average benefit and catastrophic tail risk?

| --- | --- |

| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Decide whether an effect is large enough, or small enough, to matter.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent unit at the action scale.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Design-appropriate independence/cluster structure.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise/control if causal; otherwise limit claim.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block major nuisance factors and examine consequential strata.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Enough independent units for interval precision around the decision margin.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Subgroup/tail interactions may defeat average equivalence.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Fixed or valid sequential rule using the same margin.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Directly discriminates engineering-relevant alternatives.
- **COST:** Sample and measurement cost.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Overformal effect metrics or arbitrary margins can game decisions.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Magnitude-plus-uncertainty decision, with equivalence/noninferiority only when prejustified.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What effect magnitude would actually change the engineering decision?
- Is a negative result merely inconclusive, or was equivalence against a justified margin tested?
- Could a statistically significant change be operationally trivial?

### P037 — Control multiplicity, analyst degrees of freedom and selective inference across the actual family of opportunities

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P037`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Control multiplicity, analyst degrees of freedom and selective inference across the actual family of opportunities
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Multiple-comparison theory, FDR, preregistration/transparent reporting and empirical research on undisclosed flexibility.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `STATISTICAL_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Account for the number and adaptivity of outcomes, subgroups, models, thresholds and looks that could have generated a favourable claim.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Trying enough analyses almost guarantees an apparently strong result even when no stable effect exists.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False discoveries, cherry-picking, hidden file drawers and post-hoc subgroup/evaluator selection.
- **MECHANISM:** Define confirmatory families, preserve all analyses, use FWER/FDR/selective methods as appropriate, split/external validation, and label exploration.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Many metrics, variants, subgroups, prompts, models, thresholds or parallel experiments.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When one authoritative predeclared deterministic discriminator exists, multiplicity machinery is unnecessary; report any exploratory alternatives separately.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Complete analysis provenance, stable outcome definitions, independent/protected validation data and a meaningful error criterion.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Evaluator/threshold versions and excluded outputs are retained; duplicates/dependence are known.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Chosen correction matches dependence and decision loss; unreported researcher flexibility is bounded.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents false winners and preserves the evidential meaning of a reported result.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experiment owner, reviewer, portfolio decision maker and public evidence consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Overly broad correction can destroy power or discourage useful exploration; formal adjustment cannot repair biased hypotheses or invalid measurement.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** No control allows a flexible workflow to manufacture confidence.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Correcting only final p-values while selecting endpoints/models; post-hoc “primary” metric; repeated benchmark variants; favourable subgroup only.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Correcting only final p-values while selecting endpoints/models; post-hoc “primary” metric; repeated benchmark variants; favourable subgroup only. Contrary evidence/limit: The true adaptive analysis family may be impossible to enumerate; fresh independent replication/holdout protection may be more credible than retrospective correction.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from fixed families to FDR, hierarchical testing, selective inference, reusable holdouts and explicit exploration/confirmation separation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Protect the claim-generating opportunity set, not merely the final table; use fresh confirmation when adaptivity is complex.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A preregistration badge, Bonferroni-by-default or “one metric” slogan is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S028, S038, S040
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S033, S034, S035, S042
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S039
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** The true adaptive analysis family may be impossible to enumerate; fresh independent replication/holdout protection may be more credible than retrospective correction.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should multiplicity be controlled when agents dynamically generate hypotheses and evaluators during the campaign?

| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |

| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Select or confirm effects while controlling false claims across many opportunities.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent unit within each experiment; the claim family spans analyses/experiments.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Dependence among tests affects error control; shared data is not independent replication.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomisation protects each causal contrast but not unreported multiplicity.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Use hierarchical families/strata where decision structure justifies them.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent confirmatory units/data after exploration.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Post-hoc interaction/subgroup searches are major multiplicity sources.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Predefine looks or use valid sequential/multiple-testing procedure.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Protects credibility of selected winners.
- **COST:** Power cost and need for fresh holdout/replication.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Blanket corrections can be wasteful and obscure exploratory learning.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Transparent family definition, suitable error criterion, and protected confirmation.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- How many outcomes, subgroups, models, thresholds and looks could have produced the selected claim?
- Were unfavourable measurements or analyses retained?
- Is fresh independent confirmation needed because the adaptive family cannot be reconstructed?

### P038 — Plan sequential evidence and stopping so repeated looks do not manufacture confidence

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P038`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Plan sequential evidence and stopping so repeated looks do not manufacture confidence
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Wald sequential analysis, alpha-spending/group-sequential methods, confidence sequences and always-valid online inference.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `STATISTICAL_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Permit data-dependent continuation/stopping only under a rule whose error/coverage or decision risk remains valid over time.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Peeking and sampling until favourable inflates false positives; fixed quotas can waste measurements after a decision is already discriminated.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Optional-stopping abuse, confirmation loops, repeated review inflation and needless evidence collection.
- **MECHANISM:** Choose fixed horizon, group-sequential/alpha-spending, SPRT, confidence sequence, Bayesian decision rule or explicit noninferential learning cadence; preserve stopping metadata.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Repeated monitoring/experiments where results are reviewed before collection ends or cost accumulates over time.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Stop immediately when an authoritative deterministic discriminator settles the decision; no statistical stopping machinery is needed.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined outcome/estimand, valid time/dependence model, precommitted boundaries or anytime-valid method, complete history and action loss.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement/evaluator is stable over the sequence; repeated observations add identifiable information.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Procedure’s martingale/likelihood/independence conditions hold or robust alternatives are used; adaptive outcome switching is excluded.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Cuts expected cost while preserving error/coverage and prevents repeated looks from becoming false replication.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Experiment/monitoring owner and resource/risk decision maker.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Overly conservative spending can delay useful decisions; complex anytime-valid methods may be misunderstood or applied outside assumptions.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Unplanned peeking yields overstated evidence; quota stopping wastes resources and can expose units needlessly.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Restarting tests after failure; treating every review as independent; stopping at first green; continuing only negative arms; changing metric midstream.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Restarting tests after failure; treating every review as independent; stopping at first green; continuing only negative arms; changing metric midstream. Contrary evidence/limit: No universal stopping rule exists across discovery, safety, optimisation and governance; information value and consequence determine the boundary.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from model-specific SPRT to group-sequential, alpha spending, confidence sequences and decision-theoretic adaptive designs.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Another observation is justified only when it is expected to change the decision and the stopping/error framework remains valid.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A universal number of rounds or a confidence-sequence library is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S011, S012, S036, S037
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S040, S042
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S035
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** No universal stopping rule exists across discovery, safety, optimisation and governance; information value and consequence determine the boundary.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should sequential validity be preserved when both candidate and evaluator adapt between looks?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Stop/continue an experiment while preserving evidential validity and minimising expected cost.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Sequentially observed independent/conditionally modelled units.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Time dependence/adaptive assignment must meet the chosen procedure.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomisation continues under the protocol; adaptive allocation accounted for.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Stratification can be maintained; information fractions may guide looks.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** New units/information increments, not repeated reinterpretation of the same data.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Adaptive subgroup/outcome switching is outside ordinary boundaries.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Fixed horizon or explicit sequential boundary/decision rule.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Expected decision-value gain per next observation.
- **COST:** Delay, samples, exposure and analysis burden.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Using advanced sequential tools ceremonially where a simple fixed design suffices.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Method matched to dependence and decision, with full look/stopping provenance.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Was the stopping/continuation rule defined before outcomes were reviewed?
- Does each additional test add independent or conditionally valid information?
- Is collection continuing because the decision remains ambiguous or merely because a quota exists?

### P039 — Bound causal claims by external validity, interference, carryover and order effects

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P039`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Bound causal claims by external validity, interference, carryover and order effects
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Causal inference, cross-over design, cluster/interference methods and transportability concerns.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Separate internal comparison validity from applicability to new contexts and from one unit affecting another or later periods.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A clean experiment can estimate the wrong deployment effect when units interfere, learn, carry over, or differ from the target population.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Overgeneralisation, contaminated controls, order confounding and deployment failure despite a significant trial.
- **MECHANISM:** Define exposure mapping/population, design clusters/washouts/counterbalancing, measure effect modifiers, replicate across contexts and restrict claims.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Networked/shared-resource systems, sequential treatments, adaptive users, multi-site deployment or any generalisation claim.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For an exact local deterministic mechanism with no scope extension, external-validity statistics may be unnecessary; state the local claim.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Target population/context, unit/exposure graph, period/order history, representative sampling and stable measurement across contexts.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Outcome remains comparable; contamination/exposure is observable enough to model.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** No hidden interference/carryover beyond the specified structure; sampled contexts cover intended use or extrapolation is bounded.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents local experimental success from being treated as universal engineering truth.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Deployment owner, system architect, regulator and experiment consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Maximal heterogeneity studies can be prohibitively costly and reduce precision; overcautious scope may underuse valid mechanisms.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring transfer/interference can reverse sign or erase benefit at scale.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Users share treatments; same system state across arms; inadequate washout; one site; aggregate effect hides vulnerable context.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Randomisation is preserved but narrowed: it supports the assigned comparison, not automatic transport.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved into cluster/network experiments, crossover diagnostics, multisite replication and explicit transport/sensitivity analysis.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** State the local causal estimand and separately justify transport; design around interference/order when materially plausible.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A “randomised” label is not the property if exposure or deployment scope differs.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S018, S047, S048
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S039, S099
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S037
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** General transport under unmeasured effect modification is not identified from one experiment; mechanistic and multi-context evidence remain necessary.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What minimum context diversity is needed before an engineering effect can be treated as robust rather than local?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Estimate a causal effect under deployment-relevant exposure and context.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Unit or cluster under a defined exposure mapping/period sequence.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Interference/carryover explicitly absent or modelled.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise clusters/sequences or use appropriate controls.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Stratify sites/contexts and counterbalance order.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent clusters/period sequences and multisite confirmation.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Treatment×context and spillover interactions are central.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Stopping rule respects clusters/periods and delayed outcomes.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Distinguishes direct, spillover, carryover and transported effects.
- **COST:** Clusters/sites, washout, longer follow-up.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Overly broad designs may be infeasible; local claims may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Scope-bounded causal estimate plus interference/carryover and transport evidence.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Can units influence one another or retain effects across periods?
- Does the experimental population/context match deployment?
- What evidence supports transporting the local effect beyond the tested conditions?

### P040 — Use robustness and sensitivity analysis to expose dependence on model, confounding and nuisance assumptions

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P040`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use robustness and sensitivity analysis to expose dependence on model, confounding and nuisance assumptions
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Robust statistics, model criticism, causal sensitivity and industrial robustness practice.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Recompute or bound conclusions under plausible alternative distributions, nuisance values, confounding, preprocessing and analysis choices.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A conclusion may exist only under one fragile model or arbitrary analytic choice.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Model-driven false certainty, hidden leverage/outliers, unmeasured confounding and specification-search overfit.
- **MECHANISM:** Specify plausible perturbation set, inspect residuals/influence, use robust/nonparametric/model-averaged/bounded analyses and report decision stability.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Small samples, heavy tails, observational comparisons, extrapolation, complex models or high-consequence decisions.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When a deterministic mechanism/proof settles the decision, statistical sensitivity may be redundant; verify mechanism assumptions instead.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Domain-informed alternative models/ranges, adequate measurement/provenance and a fixed decision criterion.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement error and evaluator variants are included among perturbations where material.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Perturbation set spans credible alternatives rather than only convenient ones; robust method’s loss/neighbourhood matches consequence.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Shows whether action is stable or identifies which assumption needs new evidence.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Analyst, reviewer, risk owner and design authority.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Performing dozens of unprincipled alternatives can become another multiplicity exercise or obscure a clear primary analysis.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** No sensitivity analysis leaves fragile assumptions invisible.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Only normality test; deleting “outliers” post hoc; robust estimator treated as assumption-free; sensitivity range chosen to preserve result.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Only normality test; deleting “outliers” post hoc; robust estimator treated as assumption-free; sensitivity range chosen to preserve result. Contrary evidence/limit: Sensitivity conclusions are only as broad as the considered perturbations; unknown unknowns and structural model error remain.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from single-model checks to decision-focused robustness, influence analysis, partial identification and stress scenarios.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Challenge the assumptions capable of changing the decision; do not confuse robust-to-one-model feature with universal validity.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A mandatory normality test or a menu of every model is ceremony.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S016, S017, S021
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S042, S099
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S039
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Sensitivity conclusions are only as broad as the considered perturbations; unknown unknowns and structural model error remain.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should plausible perturbation sets be governed so they are neither result-preserving nor impossibly broad?
