| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Determine whether an experimental conclusion is stable to plausible violations/heterogeneity.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Same independent units, reanalysed or augmented under alternative assumptions.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Dependence alternatives explicitly considered.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomisation protects assignment but sensitivity addresses noncompliance/missingness/model choices.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Assess block/context heterogeneity and alternative stratifications.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Fresh confirmation may be required when sensitivity reveals overfit.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Unmodelled interactions are a key robustness target.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Sensitivity plan ideally prespecified; exploratory analyses fully reported.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Identifies which assumption/new experiment has highest value.
- **COST:** Analysis and possible follow-up runs.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Sensitivity theatre via many irrelevant models.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Decision-focused perturbation/bounds with independent follow-up for fragile drivers.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Which model or nuisance assumption could reverse the decision?
- Was the perturbation range justified independently of the desired result?
- Does a robust conclusion survive evaluator, sampling and preprocessing alternatives—not only distribution choice?

### P041 — Choose experiments and measurements by expected information or decision value relative to cost

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P041`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Choose experiments and measurements by expected information or decision value relative to cost
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shewhart/Duncan economic control, decision analysis/value of information and sequential industrial experimentation.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Collect evidence when its expected effect on the next decision is worth its monetary, time, risk and opportunity cost.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Measurement quotas, full DOE or repeated review can consume more than the uncertainty they resolve; cheap tests may be omitted despite high value.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Evidence theatre, analysis paralysis, overmeasurement and underinvestment in decisive discriminators.
- **MECHANISM:** List current alternatives/uncertainty, possible outcomes and changed actions; estimate information gain/EVSI or use qualitative dominance; stop when no plausible result changes action enough.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Costly/slow/destructive measurement, adaptive experimentation, competing tests or staged engineering decisions.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Take the direct authoritative fact immediately when cost is negligible; do not build a probabilistic model merely to justify an obvious check.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Explicit decision/action set, consequence ranges, credible outcome model and measurement/experiment cost.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement can actually discriminate the uncertain states and does not repeat shared bias.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Utility/probability model or qualitative ranking is sufficiently credible; option value and irreversible harm are considered.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Maximises learning per resource and supplies a principled stopping/cheap-path boundary.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Resource allocator, design/test owner and risk decision maker.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Numerical EVSI can create false precision, undervalue discovery or encode contested utilities; qualitative use may be safer.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring information value either starves key evidence or funds endless low-value replication.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** “More data” default; full factorial despite deterministic discriminator; no action change after result; cost omitted; catastrophic risk monetised casually.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: “More data” default; full factorial despite deterministic discriminator; no action change after result; cost omitted; catastrophic risk monetised casually. Contrary evidence/limit: Deep uncertainty and irreversible tail harms can defeat expected-value summaries; robust/safety constraints may override average information value.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from economic chart/sample design toward general evidence portfolios, adaptive design and qualitative decision-value gates.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Ask whether the next observation is independently informative enough to change action; choose the cheapest valid discriminator.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A formal Bayesian model is optional; evidence proportionality is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S014, S098, S009
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S021, S080
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S032, S072
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Deep uncertainty and irreversible tail harms can defeat expected-value summaries; robust/safety constraints may override average information value.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should option/discovery value and catastrophic constraints be combined with expected information value?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Select the next experiment or decide to stop based on decision value.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Depends on candidate experiment; independent information unit must be explicit.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Expected information must discount correlation/shared bias.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Prefer designs that discriminate alternatives; randomise when causal.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block/stratify if it cheaply improves information.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Value comes from new independent information, not repeated looks.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Experiments should target uncertain interactions capable of changing action.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Stop when expected net decision value is nonpositive or boundary reached.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Core criterion: expected reduction in decision loss/uncertainty.
- **COST:** Measurement, delay, risk, setup and opportunity.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** False numerical precision or bureaucracy around EVSI.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Qualitative/quantitative value-of-information gate with safety/robust constraints.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Which possible result of the next measurement would change the next decision?
- Is the evidence independent enough to reduce the dominant uncertainty?
- Does a cheaper test, sample or deterministic check dominate the proposed study?

### P042 — Give precedence to an authoritative deterministic discriminator when it settles the decision

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P042`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Give precedence to an authoritative deterministic discriminator when it settles the decision
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `EXPERIMENTATION_CAUSAL_DISCRIMINATION_AND_STOPPING`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Not a single historical statistical school; a convergent anti-overapplication boundary implicit in measurement science, engineering proof and evidence economy.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `CONVERGENT_PROPERTY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** When a valid exact check, identity, proof, specification comparison or mechanism directly decides the case, use it before stochastic inference.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Statistical machinery can obscure a decisive fact, introduce model assumptions and waste samples.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Decorative statistics, false uncertainty, repeated green tests replacing source authority, and averaging over a deterministic defect.
- **MECHANISM:** Verify authority, identity, applicability and measurement; execute the direct check; use statistics only for residual uncertainty, frequency, transfer or noisy observation.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Exact configuration/identity, complete enumeration, formal proof, direct physical interlock or unambiguous requirement violation.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Do not invoke this property when the “deterministic” rule is an unvalidated proxy, incomplete oracle, stale reference or only one sample from a stochastic population.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Authoritative source, exact scope/identity, reliable implementation and no hidden measurement ambiguity.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** The discriminator’s input and output are verifiable and its resolution covers the decision.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The rule truly entails the decision under current conditions; completeness/authority have been established.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Eliminates needless uncertainty and preserves statistics for genuinely stochastic/unknown questions.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Engineering decision owner and evidence reviewer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Overclaiming determinism can suppress uncertainty, rare contexts or model error; an exact test may only check a proxy.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring a direct discriminator can prolong unsafe ambiguity and create opportunities for gaming.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Running significance tests on exhaustive results; rerunning a deterministic failure until green; using source count over authoritative identity; stale oracle.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Running significance tests on exhaustive results; rerunning a deterministic failure until green; using source count over authoritative identity; stale oracle. Contrary evidence/limit: Whether a discriminator is complete and authoritative is itself an evidence question; probabilistic residual risk may remain.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern evidence-efficient practice treats statistical methods as conditional tools, not obligatory proof decorations.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** First ask whether the decision is already logically or physically discriminated; if yes, stop statistical collection and document the decisive evidence.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** The absence of statistical analysis is not a defect when uncertainty is not the decision problem.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S098, S079, S110
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S051, S054, S080
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S068
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Whether a discriminator is complete and authoritative is itself an evidence question; probabilistic residual risk may remain.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How can systems prevent “deterministic” gates from becoming stale or proxy-captured while retaining their cheap decisiveness?

| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | MODERATE |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Determine whether experimentation is needed at all.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Not applicable when the direct discriminator is complete; otherwise define residual unit.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** No replication requirement for a logically decisive exact fact.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Not applicable unless residual causal uncertainty remains.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Not applicable unless context scope is uncertain.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Replication addresses implementation/transfer reliability, not the already settled exact fact.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Potential context exceptions must be tested if they limit entailment.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Stop immediately once authoritative discrimination is verified.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Maximum information at minimum cost when valid.
- **COST:** Verification of authority/identity and maintenance.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Misclassifying a probabilistic proxy as deterministic.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Deterministic precedence with explicit residual-uncertainty check.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Is there an authoritative deterministic check that already settles the decision?
- Has the check’s identity, completeness and applicability been verified?
- What residual uncertainty, if any, actually requires statistical evidence?

### P043 — Design robustness to nuisance factors and context variation, not only nominal mean performance

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P043`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Design robustness to nuisance factors and context variation, not only nominal mean performance
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Industrial parameter/robust design, Taguchi influence, classical factorial/RSM and robust statistics.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `RELIABILITY_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Choose designs/settings whose performance remains acceptable as uncontrollable noise factors vary.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A nominal optimum can be fragile to temperature, operator, load, population, supplier or environment.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Local optimisation, high variance, field failure and “best average” designs with poor context tolerance.
- **MECHANISM:** Identify realistic noise factors, cross them with control factors or stress them, model mean/variance/tails separately, and confirm in representative contexts.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Products/processes deployed across variable environments or with costly field failures.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a fixed, tightly controlled environment or a deterministic design margin proven against all relevant variation, elaborate robust DOE may be unnecessary.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Valid noise-factor ranges/distributions, controllable factors, measurement fitness, feasible stress combinations and consequence criteria.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement remains valid across noise conditions and can resolve dispersion/tail changes.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Tested noise factors represent deployment; interactions/model form are adequate; accelerated stresses do not introduce irrelevant mechanisms.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Reduces sensitivity, warranty/failure cost and transfer surprises, often without requiring tighter process control.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Designer, reliability/quality engineer and deployment owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Over-broad noise arrays can be expensive; robustness to selected factors can hide vulnerability to omitted mechanisms.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Nominal-only optimisation misses field variability and tail risk.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Using Taguchi S/N ratio automatically; unrealistic noise ranges; no confirmation; averaging away context-specific failures.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Using Taguchi S/N ratio automatically; unrealistic noise ranges; no confirmation; averaging away context-specific failures. Contrary evidence/limit: No finite noise-factor set guarantees open-world robustness; field monitoring and conservative design margins remain necessary.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Later statistical criticism retained parameter/noise-factor design but narrowed or replaced universal S/N analyses with direct modelling and standard DOE.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Test and optimise decision-relevant performance across plausible nuisance contexts, with transparent loss and confirmation.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Taguchi orthogonal arrays, S/N ratios and loss slogans are optional/historically contingent; robustness is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S025, S049, S095
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S096, S102, S016
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S094
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** No finite noise-factor set guarantees open-world robustness; field monitoring and conservative design margins remain necessary.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should robust design cover rare combinations and adversarial contexts without combinatorial explosion?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Select settings/designs that remain acceptable across nuisance contexts.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent run/unit at control×noise condition.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Runs independent/blocked; repeated observations nested.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise feasible order; deliberate noise-factor assignment.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block hard-to-change factors or use split-plot designs.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent confirmation across representative noise contexts.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Control×noise interactions are central.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Predefine robustness loss/criteria and confirmation.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Information on sensitivity and variance reduction per run.
- **COST:** Expanded design/stress cost.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Ceremonial S/N optimisation or unrealistic stress matrix.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Direct mean/variance/tail modelling over plausible noise contexts plus confirmation.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Were nuisance/noise factors representative of deployment and crossed with controllable factors?
- Is robustness claimed from average performance or from dispersion/tail/context evidence?
- Would a direct engineering margin prove robustness more cheaply?

### P044 — Model reliability over time with explicit failure definitions, exposure, censoring and degradation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P044`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Model reliability over time with explicit failure definitions, exposure, censoring and degradation
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Life-testing, survival analysis, reliability engineering and degradation modelling.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `RELIABILITY_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Estimate time-to-failure or degradation processes using all exposure and censoring information under a defined mission/failure criterion.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Snapshot pass rates do not describe reliability duration; censored survivors and varying duty cycles can bias estimates.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Confusing current performance with lifetime reliability, dropping early failures, and ignoring degradation paths.
- **MECHANISM:** Define failure/missions, record time/exposure and interventions, use survival/degradation models with uncertainty, stratify failure modes and validate fit.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Components/systems where ageing, wear, cumulative use or time-dependent failure changes the decision.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a purely timeless deterministic property or rapidly replaceable low-consequence component, full life modelling may not pay.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Representative units/duty cycles, failure-mode taxonomy, censoring records, stable measurement, sufficient time/failures or degradation surrogate.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Failure detection threshold is stable and degradation measure is linked to actual failure.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Independent/appropriately clustered lifetimes, censoring mechanism acceptable, distribution/regression model adequate over prediction horizon.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Supports maintenance, warranty, design margin and release decisions with time-indexed risk.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Reliability engineer, maintainer, design authority and risk owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Complex lifetime models can extrapolate far beyond evidence and give false precision; failure definitions may be gamed.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring time can miss wear-out, early-life or cumulative hazards.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Removing early failures; right-censored units treated as nonfailures forever; mixed modes pooled; uptime denominator changed; threshold drift.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Removing early failures; right-censored units treated as nonfailures forever; mixed modes pooled; uptime denominator changed; threshold drift. Contrary evidence/limit: Complex software/agent failure opportunities may not map cleanly to lifetime distributions; reliability methods transfer only where exposure/failure processes are coherent.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from failure-count summaries to censored survival, recurrent/repairable systems, covariates, degradation and predictive uncertainty.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Make reliability conditional on mission, time/exposure, failure definition and context; report bounds and model sensitivity.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A Weibull plot or MTBF number is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `DOMAIN_SPECIFIC`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S071, S077, S104
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S078, S072
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S075
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Complex software/agent failure opportunities may not map cleanly to lifetime distributions; reliability methods transfer only where exposure/failure processes are coherent.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should reliability be defined for continually updated systems whose configuration and workload change during exposure?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Predict/monitor failure risk over time and decide maintenance/release.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Time-to-event or degradation process with censoring, covariates and possible competing modes.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable failure detection, exposure records and representative duty cycles.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Hazard/survival/degradation estimate with uncertainty and mode diagnostics.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Premature maintenance/rejection.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Unexpected field failure or underestimated wear-out.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Configuration/context drift can make historical lifetimes obsolete.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Changing failure threshold or dropping early failures improves apparent reliability.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Deterministic wear limit or conservative life bound when available.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Mission-specific censored/degradation model with sensitivity and ongoing field update.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Is the reliability claim tied to a mission, time/exposure and stable failure definition?
- Were censored, early and competing-mode observations retained?
- Does the model represent deployment duty cycles and configuration changes?

### P045 — Use accelerated testing only with validated stress-to-use mechanisms and explicit extrapolation uncertainty

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P045`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Use accelerated testing only with validated stress-to-use mechanisms and explicit extrapolation uncertainty
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Accelerated life testing, physics-of-failure models and reliability statistics.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `RELIABILITY_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Apply elevated stress to obtain failures sooner, then translate to use conditions through an acceleration/life model.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Use-stress tests may produce too few failures, but high stress can create different failure modes or nonlinear extrapolation.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Overstated lifetime, irrelevant stress failures, parameter uncertainty hidden by large acceleration factors.
- **MECHANISM:** Select physically justified stresses, include multiple levels/use-level anchors, diagnose failure modes, fit and challenge acceleration models, report prediction intervals/sensitivity.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Long-lived products where timely direct life testing is infeasible and a credible acceleration mechanism exists.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Do not accelerate when stress changes the mechanism or when conservative deterministic margin/field data already decide the question.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Mechanistic knowledge, representative specimens, stable failure definition, stress control, enough failures/levels and use-condition data/anchors.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stress/temperature/load measurement is calibrated; failure detection remains comparable across levels.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Same relevant failure mechanisms and adequate acceleration/life distributions across tested-to-use range.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Obtains earlier reliability evidence and supports design comparison without waiting full life.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Reliability/design owner and release/risk authority.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Wrong models can produce orders-of-magnitude false confidence; accelerated stress may damage irrelevant modes.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Avoiding all acceleration can make evidence unavailable before deployment.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** One stress level; no use anchor; pooled failure modes; extrapolation without interval; treating high-stress pass as field proof.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: One stress level; no use anchor; pooled failure modes; extrapolation without interval; treating high-stress pass as field proof. Contrary evidence/limit: Extreme acceleration and sparse failures make model uncertainty dominant; there may be no defensible high-reliability extrapolation.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Mature ALT integrates physics, multiple stresses, model diagnostics and conservative uncertainty rather than a generic acceleration factor.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Acceleration is an assumption-heavy bridge whose physical continuity must be evidenced, not a shortcut to certainty.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A standard Arrhenius/Weibull fit is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S072, S076, S077
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S078, S104
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S071
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Extreme acceleration and sparse failures make model uncertainty dominant; there may be no defensible high-reliability extrapolation.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How much use-condition anchoring is enough to falsify an acceleration model before release?

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | LOW |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Estimate use-condition life from elevated-stress tests.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent specimen/system at a stress level.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Lifetimes independent/clustered; censoring and common batch effects modelled.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise specimens/run order where feasible; stress deliberately assigned.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block batch/manufacturing lot and include use-level anchors.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent specimens across multiple stress levels and confirmation.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Stress×failure-mode/material interactions can invalidate extrapolation.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Predefine censoring/test termination and model-selection/validation rule.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Large time acceleration if model is valid.
- **COST:** Specimens, chambers, destructive failures and model risk.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Routine ALT without physics or anchors creates false certainty.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Physics-informed multi-level ALT with failure-mode checks, sensitivity and conservative prediction.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What physical evidence shows the accelerated and use conditions share failure mechanisms?
- How far beyond observed stress/time is the reliability claim extrapolated?
- Would a conservative design bound or degradation measure be more credible?

### P046 — Treat reliability growth as intervention-conditioned evidence, not an automatically improving trend

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P046`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Treat reliability growth as intervention-conditioned evidence, not an automatically improving trend
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Reliability growth testing, Crow–AMSAA/Duane-type models and test-analyse-fix programmes.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `RELIABILITY_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Evaluate whether documented design/process fixes change failure intensity over exposure while accounting for configuration and environment.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A downward failure trend can arise from easier tests, survivor populations, changed definitions or selective fixes rather than durable growth.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Trend worship, double-counting fixes, uncontrolled configuration changes and claiming causality from calendar order.
- **MECHANISM:** Version configurations, log every intervention/exposure/failure mode, use growth models as summaries, and validate fixes on fresh representative tests/field data.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Development programmes with repeated failures and deliberate corrective modifications.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When no modifications occur, use ordinary reliability estimation; when one deterministic defect/fix is proven, validate that mechanism directly.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Configuration control, comparable exposure/test environments, stable failure definitions, independent validation and enough events.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement/evaluator thresholds are stable across versions or bridged.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Model form approximates the repair/improvement process; changes other than intended fixes are documented.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Turns failures into structured learning and supports readiness forecasts.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Reliability programme manager, design authority and acquisition/release decision maker.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Growth targets can incentivise relabelling failures or easier tests; fitted curves can extrapolate beyond fix effectiveness.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** No growth tracking loses cumulative learning and repeated-mode evidence.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Test difficulty decreases; failures reclassified; exposure resets; fixes not independently verified; one programme curve pooled across modes.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Test difficulty decreases; failures reclassified; exposure resets; fixes not independently verified; one programme curve pooled across modes. Contrary evidence/limit: Growth models are particularly vulnerable when few failures, many simultaneous changes or rapidly changing test environments exist.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Consensus guidance narrows claims and stresses configuration/test-environment assumptions and independent verification.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Attribute change to documented fixes only when comparable exposure and fresh validation support it; otherwise call it an observed trend.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A Crow–AMSAA plot or target slope is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `DOMAIN_SPECIFIC`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S078, S071, S077
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S040, S097
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S104
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Growth models are particularly vulnerable when few failures, many simultaneous changes or rapidly changing test environments exist.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How can causal contribution be allocated among bundled fixes without an infeasible factorial programme?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | LOW |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Decide whether reliability is improving after interventions and whether readiness targets are credible.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Failure process over exposure with discrete design/process interventions and configuration changes.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable failure definitions, exposure accounting and versioned configurations.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Mode-specific post-fix reduction confirmed under comparable/fresh tests.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Credit to ineffective fixes and premature release.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Failure to recognise genuine learning or recurring modes.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Test environment and configuration changes are major nonstationarity.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Relabelling or easier testing can manufacture growth.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct proof/validation of a specific deterministic fix.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Intervention-conditioned trend plus fresh validation and configuration control.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Are reliability improvements tied to documented fixes and comparable exposure?
- Did failure definitions, thresholds, test difficulty or configuration change?
- Was the claimed fix validated on fresh representative units?

### P047 — Translate zero observed failures into an upper risk bound, never “perfect reliability”

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P047`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Translate zero observed failures into an upper risk bound, never “perfect reliability”
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Binomial/Poisson reliability demonstration and the rule of three.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `RELIABILITY_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Use one-sided confidence/credible bounds on failure probability/rate when no events are observed.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Zero events in finite exposure is compatible with nonzero—sometimes material—failure rates.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Infinite-reliability claims, misleading “100% pass,” and underpowered high-reliability demonstrations.
- **MECHANISM:** Define independent opportunities/exposure and target confidence; calculate bound, include heterogeneity/censoring, and compare with required risk.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Reliability/safety claims based on zero failures or no detected rare defects.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When a formal proof/physical impossibility establishes zero risk within scope, statistical zero-event bounds are secondary; verify proof scope.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Clear failure opportunity, independent/representative trials, stable detection threshold, complete event capture and fixed stopping.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Failures would be detected with sufficient sensitivity; no excluded/censored near-failures.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Bernoulli/Poisson/exposure model and homogeneity or conservative stratification are adequate.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Converts “none seen” into a transparent finite statement and reveals sample burden for high reliability.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Safety/reliability/release authority and customer/regulator.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Bounds can be overly reassuring if opportunities are correlated/easy or detection misses failures; conservative bounds may require huge samples.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring zero-event uncertainty can understate tail risk by orders of magnitude.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Counting repeated same-condition trials as independent; optional stopping after a clean run; changing failure definition; hiding near misses.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Counting repeated same-condition trials as independent; optional stopping after a clean run; changing failure definition; hiding near misses. Contrary evidence/limit: Extremely low target rates are rarely demonstrable by direct testing alone; model/mechanism/operational evidence must be combined without pretending equivalence.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern practice uses exact/Bayesian/Poisson/exposure bounds and combines test with mechanistic/field evidence for very high reliability.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Report the strongest upper bound supported by independent representative exposure and state what remains unobserved.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** “0 defects” and a green dashboard are not properties.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S073, S075, S071
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S045, S078
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S068
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Extremely low target rates are rarely demonstrable by direct testing alone; model/mechanism/operational evidence must be combined without pretending equivalence.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should heterogeneous stress, common-mode failures and changing exposure be incorporated into zero-event bounds?

**EVIDENCE_STRENGTH**

| Partition | Judgement |
| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Demonstrate that failure probability/rate is below an engineering bound.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent unit/opportunity or exposure interval.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** Independence/common-mode assumptions explicitly tested or conservatively adjusted.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Random sampling of units/conditions where possible.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Stratify high-risk contexts and common-mode clusters.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent opportunities; repeats on one unit may not count equally.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Context interactions can concentrate rare failures.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Fixed exposure/sample or valid sequential reliability-demonstration plan.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Tightens upper risk bound.
- **COST:** Large sample/exposure burden, especially for rare targets.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Massive low-risk testing can crowd out mechanism/stress evidence.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Bound-based demonstration integrated with mechanistic and stress evidence.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- What upper failure-rate bound—not pass percentage—follows from the independent exposure?
- Are trials representative and sufficiently sensitive to detect failure?
- Could correlation, optional stopping or changed definitions make zero failures uninformative?

### P048 — Treat rare-event and tail claims as distinct, data-hungry and model-sensitive

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P048`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Treat rare-event and tail claims as distinct, data-hungry and model-sensitive
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Extreme-value/tolerance/reliability theory, rare-event sampling and safety/reliability practice.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `RELIABILITY_OR_ROBUSTNESS_LINEAGE`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `RELIABILITY_NATIVE`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Analyse high quantiles, exceedance probabilities and catastrophic modes separately from means and ordinary variance.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Good averages and even stable central behaviour can coexist with unacceptable tails; finite data contain little direct tail information.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Rare-event overconfidence, normal extrapolation, benchmark averages hiding catastrophic cases and absence-of-event fallacy.
- **MECHANISM:** Define tail event/consequence, preserve maxima/near misses, use tolerance/extreme/reliability models with diagnostics and conservative bounds, stress/importance sample, and combine mechanism evidence.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** High-consequence low-frequency failures, tight population-content guarantees or asymmetric loss.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For bounded deterministic systems with proven hard limits, direct worst-case analysis can dominate statistical tail estimation.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable event definition/detection, representative exposure including stress contexts, enough tail observations or justified physical model, and no selective deletion.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Resolution captures near-tail behaviour and censoring/thresholds are stable.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Tail family/extrapolation and dependence are adequate; stress sampling is correctly reweighted; unobserved modes acknowledged.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents mean-focused release and quantifies what high reliability cannot be claimed from limited evidence.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Safety/reliability/design authority and risk owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Tail models can be highly unstable and stress tests can create irrelevant modes; extreme conservatism can make decisions impossible.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring tails can hide catastrophic but decision-dominating outcomes.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Normal six-sigma extrapolation; dropping outliers; zero events called zero risk; tail metric tuned after incident; pooled modes.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Normal six-sigma extrapolation; dropping outliers; zero events called zero risk; tail metric tuned after incident; pooled modes. Contrary evidence/limit: Unknown failure modes and dependence can dominate beyond-sample tails; precise numerical estimates may be less honest than conservative bounds/scenarios.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved toward explicit tolerance/exceedance bounds, extreme-value methods, stress testing and mechanism-informed conservative evidence.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Separate central performance from tail claims; calibrate claim strength to observed/validated tail information and consequence.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A “six sigma” label or one worst-case anecdote is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S074, S075, S071
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S062, S078, S099
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S104
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Unknown failure modes and dependence can dominate beyond-sample tails; precise numerical estimates may be less honest than conservative bounds/scenarios.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What combination of stress, mechanism and operational data justifies a claim at a failure rate never directly observed?

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | LOW |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Detect/limit rare catastrophic events or high quantiles.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Heavy-tail/extreme/event process with possible dependence and heterogeneous exposure.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Sensitive event capture, exposure denominator and preserved extremes/near misses.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Conservative exceedance/tolerance bound or stress-validated mechanism.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** False alarms/overdesign from unstable tails.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Catastrophic missed mode or grossly understated rate.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Tail law and exposure can shift strongly.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Dropping “outliers” or changing event definitions erases risk.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Deterministic worst-case bound/interlock if authoritative.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Multi-source conservative tail case with explicit evidence limit.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Is a mean or central interval being used to imply tail safety?
- How much direct or validated stress/mechanism evidence exists for the claimed tail?
- Were outliers, near misses and censored failures preserved?

### P049 — Test distribution shift and stress contexts; do not equate held-out performance with deployment validity

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P049`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Test distribution shift and stress contexts; do not equate held-out performance with deployment validity
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Measurement invariance, robust/reliability testing, dataset-shift science and refreshed benchmark evidence.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `MODERN_DOMAIN_TRANSLATION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `DOMAIN_TRANSLATION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Evaluate whether performance/calibration persists under plausible population, environment, workload, adversarial or temporal shifts.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A model/process can succeed on a fixed holdout while failing after deployment composition, context or incentives change.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Benchmark saturation, test-set overfitting, stale controls and false generalisation from one distribution.
- **MECHANISM:** Map shift scenarios, reserve protected/refresh samples, stress test mechanism-relevant changes, monitor calibration and restrict claims to covered regions.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Adaptive software/ML/agent systems, long-lived processes, changing customers/suppliers, or field environments beyond lab conditions.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a closed deterministic domain fully enumerated by an authoritative test, distribution-shift analysis may be unnecessary; verify closure.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Context metadata, representative or deliberately stressed samples, stable evaluator/construct, fresh data source and shift-response plan.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Labels/references remain valid under shift or are independently adjudicated.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Stress scenarios are plausible and not merely convenient; deployment monitoring can detect uncovered shifts.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Finds brittle improvements before deployment and makes holdout evidence scope honest.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Deployment owner, reliability/quality engineer and benchmark/evaluator owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Stress suites can overfit too, invent unrealistic cases or create endless scenario bureaucracy.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Single-distribution evaluation can produce sharp but stale confidence.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Repeated public benchmark tuning; no fresh collection; adversarial examples counted as prevalence; aggregate shift hides subgroup collapse.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Repeated public benchmark tuning; no fresh collection; adversarial examples counted as prevalence; aggregate shift hides subgroup collapse. Contrary evidence/limit: Open-world shifts are unbounded; no finite suite proves universal robustness, and fresh sets may still share collection artefacts.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern practice combines protected holdouts, fresh test sets, shift taxonomy, stress scenarios and ongoing field monitoring.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** A held-out score is local evidence; deployment claims require context coverage, shift sensitivity and evaluator integrity.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A benchmark leaderboard or one stress suite is not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S099, S039, S038
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S027, S097
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S037
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Open-world shifts are unbounded; no finite suite proves universal robustness, and fresh sets may still share collection artefacts.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should shift suites be refreshed independently without making longitudinal progress incomparable?

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | VERY_HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Detect performance/calibration degradation under changing deployment distributions.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Context-indexed data distribution with possible covariate, prior, concept and adversarial shifts.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Fresh/protected samples, stable labels/evaluator and context metadata.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Shift-sensitive calibration/performance deviation tied to action.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** Chasing unrealistic stress cases or alarm fatigue.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Deployment failure outside the original holdout.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Defining risk; historical guarantees are local.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Refreshing/tuning on failures can absorb evidence.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Closed-domain exhaustive check or targeted mechanism test.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Protected fresh evaluation plus scenario stress and field drift monitoring.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Does held-out evidence cover deployment populations, contexts and incentives?
- Could benchmark/test-set reuse or contamination explain the score?
- What shift would invalidate the current threshold or evaluator calibration?

### P050 — Keep mean performance, variability, tail behaviour, reliability over time and model robustness as separate claims

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P050`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Keep mean performance, variability, tail behaviour, reliability over time and model robustness as separate claims
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAILS`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Quality variation, robust design, tolerance/reliability theory and modern multi-metric evaluation.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `QUALITY_ENGINEERING_GENERALISATION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Decompose performance dimensions instead of compressing them into one average or sigma score.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** An improvement in mean can worsen variability, tails, durability or context sensitivity; a composite can hide the trade.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False “overall improvement,” weighted-metric gaming and releases that optimise average at the expense of catastrophic modes.
- **MECHANISM:** Report decision-relevant central effect, dispersion, quantiles/tails, time/exposure reliability and assumption/context sensitivity separately; use explicit multi-criteria decisions.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Systems with heterogeneous outcomes, repeated operation, rare harms or multiple stakeholders.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a single exact binary requirement with no temporal/context dimension, one authoritative result may be sufficient.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Valid metrics for each dimension, declared weighting/priority, representative units/contexts and enough tail/time evidence for any claim.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement error is separated by dimension and thresholds remain stable.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Dimensions are not falsely treated as independent; composite utility, if used, is explicit and decision-owned.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents one favourable summary from erasing reliability or tail regressions.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Design/release authority, risk owner and public evidence consumer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Too many dimensions create multiplicity and indecision; dashboards can become ritual without priorities.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** One-score optimisation can hide exactly the failure mode that matters most.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Mean-only benchmark; variance ignored; uptime without severity; composite weights changed; tail based on one anecdote.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Mean-only benchmark; variance ignored; uptime without severity; composite weights changed; tail based on one anecdote. Contrary evidence/limit: Multi-criteria trade-offs are partly normative and may resist statistical aggregation; unresolved conflicts must remain visible.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from mean/variance quality metrics toward explicit reliability, robustness, tails and context-separated evidence.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Make one claim per dimension and combine them only through a transparent consequence model.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A mandatory giant dashboard is not the property; non-substitution among materially different risk dimensions is.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S062, S071, S074
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S039, S084
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S043, S099
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Multi-criteria trade-offs are partly normative and may resist statistical aggregation; unresolved conflicts must remain visible.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What minimal dimension set prevents harmful substitution without producing unmanageable reporting?

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Are mean, variability, tail, time reliability and context robustness being claimed separately?
- Could a favourable average conceal a serious tail or durability regression?
- Who owns any composite weighting, and was it frozen before results?

### P051 — Treat optimised metrics as vulnerable proxies and monitor for gaming/surrogation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P051`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Treat optimised metrics as vulnerable proxies and monitor for gaming/surrogation
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `PROXY_EVALUATOR_AND_SYSTEM_GOVERNANCE`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Goodhart/Campbell/Strathern indicator criticism, quality-target experience and modern benchmark overfitting.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `CONVERGENT_PROPERTY`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Assume a metric’s relation to the underlying goal can weaken when rewards, release or status depend on it.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Optimisers learn to improve the measurement surface rather than the real property.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Relabelled defects, denominator shifts, benchmark overfitting, threshold gaming and local metric improvement with system harm.
- **MECHANISM:** Keep construct/outcome checks independent, use multiple non-substitutable indicators where justified, audit exclusions/denominators, refresh protected evidence and inspect mechanisms.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** High-stakes target metrics, leaderboards, incentives, automated optimisation or repeated candidate exposure.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a direct authoritative physical/logical property that cannot be cheaply manipulated, proxy safeguards may be minimal; maintain authority/integrity checks.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined underlying goal, provenance, stable denominators, independent outcome/reference evidence and the ability to observe optimisation response.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Metric and construct are distinguishable; evaluator changes and candidate exposures are versioned.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** A plausible behavioural/optimisation mechanism links targeting to metric degradation; independent checks are not co-optimised.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Prevents numerical improvement from substituting for real engineering improvement.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Metric owner, incentive/release authority and system outcome owner.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Anti-gaming controls can become adversarial bureaucracy, suppress useful optimisation or proliferate unowned metrics.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Ignoring gaming allows precise, reproducible and meaningless success.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Dropping hard cases; changing denominator; teaching to test; benchmark leakage; proxy chosen for availability; outcome not independently checked.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Dropping hard cases; changing denominator; teaching to test; benchmark leakage; proxy chosen for availability; outcome not independently checked. Contrary evidence/limit: Goodhart/Campbell claims are not universal formal theorems; some metrics remain useful under targeting if causal alignment and integrity controls are strong.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** The social-science laws are treated as convergent warning mechanisms, strengthened in digital domains by adaptive-data/benchmark evidence.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use metrics as defeasible sensors: preserve an independent construct/outcome path and investigate divergence under optimisation.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** “Use multiple KPIs” is not itself the property; independent construct evidence and anti-substitution boundaries are.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S019, S022, S097
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S038, S039, S040
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S085, S087
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Goodhart/Campbell claims are not universal formal theorems; some metrics remain useful under targeting if causal alignment and integrity controls are strong.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How can independent outcome checks remain independent when the whole organisation can observe and optimise them?

| --- | --- |
| HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| FORMAL_OR_THEORETICAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| EMPIRICAL_CAUSAL_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| EMPIRICAL_ASSOCIATIONAL_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| DOMAIN_CASE_STRENGTH | VERY_HIGH |
| REPLICATION_STRENGTH | MODERATE |
| TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH | HIGH |
| ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY | HIGH |
| CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | MODERATE |

**VARIATION_CONTROL_PROFILE**

- **DECISION_PROBLEM:** Detect divergence between a governed metric and the underlying system outcome.
- **ASSUMED_DATA_GENERATING_PROCESS:** Metric and latent goal co-evolve under optimisation/incentives.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Independent outcome/reference measures, provenance and stable definitions.
- **SIGNAL_OR_DISCRIMINATOR:** Metric–outcome divergence, exclusion/denominator anomalies or mechanism failure.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_COST:** False accusations of gaming and metric churn.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_COST:** Sustained optimisation of a corrupted proxy.
- **NONSTATIONARITY_RISK:** Very high because optimisation changes behaviour/data.
- **INTERVENTION_RISK:** Changing the metric after each exploitation can create evaluator instability.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Direct outcome/mechanism check when cheap.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Protected independent checks, versioning and controlled metric retirement.

**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **APPLICABILITY:** NOT_PRIMARY_FOR_THIS_PROPERTY

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Can the target metric be improved without improving the underlying engineering property?
- Have denominators, labels, exclusions or case mix changed under incentive?
- What independent outcome or mechanism evidence would expose proxy capture?

### P052 — Require independent, prospective justification before changing an evaluator, fixture, expected answer or threshold after failure

- **PROPERTY_ID:** `P052`
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Require independent, prospective justification before changing an evaluator, fixture, expected answer or threshold after failure
- **PROPERTY_FAMILY:** `PROXY_EVALUATOR_AND_SYSTEM_GOVERNANCE`
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Measurement assurance/calibration change control, conformity decision rules, adaptive-data safeguards and proxy-gaming criticism.
- **LINEAGE_CLASS:** `REACTION_OR_CORRECTION`
- **INFORMATIONAL_RELATION:** `HYBRID_RESOLUTION`
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Treat post-failure evaluator change as a new measurement intervention needing evidence independent of the candidate outcome.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** An organisation can make failures disappear by editing the instrument rather than fixing the object.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Evaluator drift, threshold drift, moving goalposts and reward hacking disguised as recalibration.
- **MECHANISM:** Classify suspected defect (candidate/measurement/construct/sampling), reproduce on references, use blinded bridge studies, obtain independent authority, version change and re-evaluate affected claims.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Any proposed evaluator/threshold/fixture/answer change triggered by an unfavourable result.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Correct a provable transcription/specification error immediately, preserving evidence and authority; no large statistical study is needed.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Frozen prior version, external reference/construct evidence, bridge sample, change rationale and authority separate from candidate optimisation.
- **MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITIONS:** Reference cases include failures/boundaries and are protected from the candidate change.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** The bridge can distinguish measurement defect from candidate defect; updated rule better represents the underlying construct.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Allows legitimate recalibration while blocking outcome-contingent mutation.
- **DECISION_OR_CONSUMER:** Measurement/evaluator owner, release authority and independent reviewer.
- **FALSE_POSITIVE_OR_OVERREACTION_RISK:** Excessive governance can freeze known-bad evaluators or delay urgent safety corrections.
- **FALSE_NEGATIVE_OR_MISSED_SIGNAL_RISK:** Weak governance permits arbitrary pass-making and destroys longitudinal evidence.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Changing expected output after seeing candidate; threshold chosen to include current score; deleting failed case; no old/new bridge; circular validation.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** The property is bounded by its stated assumptions and known failures: Changing expected output after seeing candidate; threshold chosen to include current score; deleting failed case; no old/new bridge; circular validation. Contrary evidence/limit: Independent reference truth may be unavailable for evolving constructs; legitimate change can remain unresolved rather than forced.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Evolved from calibration/check-standard discipline to versioned benchmark/evaluator governance and reusable-holdout independence.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Change only on independent evidence that the measurement/construct—not merely the candidate—is defective or context has legitimately changed.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A fixed change-request form is optional; independence, bridge and retrospective impact assessment are the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** `STRONGLY_RETAINED`
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** S015, S053, S060
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** S038, S039, S097
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** S100, S103
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE:** Independent reference truth may be unavailable for evolving constructs; legitimate change can remain unresolved rather than forced.
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Who can supply independent construct authority when the evaluator and target system co-evolve?
