**EXPERIMENT_DISCRIMINATION_PROFILE**

- **HYPOTHESIS_OR_DECISION:** Design for robustness across noise factors; reject universal recipe.
- **EXPERIMENTAL_UNIT:** Independent run/unit at control×noise settings.
- **INDEPENDENCE_ASSUMPTION:** As in factorial/robust DOE.
- **RANDOMISATION_OR_CONTROL:** Randomise/assign factors appropriately.
- **BLOCKING_OR_STRATIFICATION:** Block hard-to-change factors.
- **REPLICATION_MEANING:** Independent confirmation across noise contexts.
- **INTERACTION_RISK:** Interaction/alias structure must be explicit.
- **STOPPING_RULE:** Predefine actual loss/criterion and confirm.
- **EXPECTED_INFORMATION_GAIN:** Potential robustness information if design/analysis appropriate.
- **COST:** Runs and setup complexity.
- **FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED:** Recipe-following discards information and hides aliases.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Standard DOE/direct modelling, with Taguchi tools only when conditions justify them.

**QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT**

- Is the S/N criterion mathematically aligned with the actual engineering loss and adjustability?
- What interactions are aliased by the chosen array?
- Would direct modelling of mean, variance and tails retain more information?


# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_MEASUREMENT_SYSTEM_MODEL

### Decision-linked measurement gate

| Gate | Required establishment | Properties |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0 — Decision and consumer | State the action, consumer, consequence asymmetry, horizon and smallest difference that could alter action. | P001, P006, P036, P041 |
| M1 — Measurand/construct | Define the property, unit of analysis, operating conditions, admissible interpretation and non-equivalent states. | P001, P009 |
| M2 — Reference and authority | Identify standards, reference methods, bridge samples, domain authority or triangulation; distinguish traceability from fitness. | P003, P004, P011 |
| M3 — Observation/sampling process | Define population, frame, selection, missingness, censoring, destructive measurement and measurement-induced contamination. | P011, P013, P014, P025 |
| M4 — Error-source decomposition | Separate bias, repeatability, reproducibility, operator/instrument/environment/evaluator effects and interactions. | P002, P004, P005, P008, P053 |
| M5 — Resolution and uncertainty | Demonstrate resolution/sensitivity/specificity and uncertainty relative to the decision margin, not a universal percentage. | P006, P007 |
| M6 — Decision rule | Fix threshold, guard band, loss or classification rule; distinguish conformance, statistical control and practical importance. | P006, P017, P036 |
| M7 — Provenance and version | Bind results to data, preprocessing, exclusions, evaluator, reference, threshold and software versions. | P012, P052, P054 |
| M8 — Stability, bridge and drift | Use check standards, repeated anchors, proficiency/bridge studies and change records to detect evaluator/reference drift. | P010, P052 |
| M9 — Use gate | Block metric governance when decision-relevant states cannot be discriminated; use a cheaper authoritative deterministic discriminator when it already settles the decision. | P002, P041, P042, P057 |

The mature rule is **not** “perform a gauge R&R before every decision.” It is: establish enough measurement/evaluator capability to distinguish the decision-relevant states before using the output to govern a candidate or process. The needed design can be a calibration chain, crossed/nested variance study, rater agreement design, reference/bridge challenge set, uncertainty budget, proficiency comparison, sensitivity/specificity analysis or direct authoritative check. The amount of evidence is consequence- and margin-dependent. [S015, S024, S027, S032, S051–S060, S100, S103, S105]

### Diagnostic classification for an observed discrepancy

| Class | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| CANDIDATE_DEFECT | The measurement contract is stable and independently fit; the candidate/process differs in the decision-relevant property. |
| MEASUREMENT_DEFECT | Instrument, fixture, operator, environment, algorithm or repeatability/reproducibility failure explains the observation. |
| CONSTRUCT_DEFECT | The operationalised metric does not preserve the intended property or decision-relevant distinctions. |
| SAMPLING_DEFECT | Frame, allocation, missingness, censoring, contamination or selection makes the observed sample unrepresentative. |
| EVALUATOR_DRIFT | The evaluator’s mapping changes across time/version/context without an authorised construct change. |
| THRESHOLD_DRIFT | The decision boundary changes, especially after seeing results, without independent consequence/uncertainty justification. |
| LEGITIMATE_RECALIBRATION | Independent reference/bridge evidence shows the old measurement mapping is defective or stale; retrospective impact is assessed. |
| REWARD_HACKING_OR_PROXY_CAPTURE | Optimisation improves the score while degrading, bypassing or relabelling the intended property. |
| UNRESOLVED | Available evidence cannot identify which surface changed; the action must preserve the ambiguity rather than assign blame. |

### Required distinctions

- `precise != accurate`: averaging narrows random error but does not remove common bias.
- `repeatable != reproducible`: same-condition consistency does not establish cross-operator/site/version stability.
- `reproducible != valid`: many systems can reproduce the same construct error.
- `stable evaluator != valid property definition`: drift control cannot repair a wrong measurand.
- `metric available != metric decision-relevant`: availability is not construct authority or information value.
- `measurement changed != process changed`: evaluator/instrument/reference variation must be separated.
- `candidate changed != evaluator changed`: a bridge or invariant reference is needed when both can move.

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_VARIATION_AND_CONTROL_MODEL

| Stage | Mature requirement |
| --- | --- |
| V0 — Decision stream | Define unit, order, time base, opportunity/exposure, response owner and cost of false alarm, missed shift and delay. |
| V1 — Measurement gate | Apply the measurement-system model before interpreting observed dispersion or chart signals. |
| V2 — Regime and baseline | Specify the local process/regime and whether prediction from a baseline is defensible; avoid mixed populations. |
| V3 — Dependence and subgrouping | Model rational subgrouping, clustering, autocorrelation, seasonality and changing sample size/opportunity. |
| V4 — Distinct limits | Keep control/detection limits separate from specifications/tolerances and from capability/performance claims. |
| V5 — Detector design | Choose Shewhart, CUSUM/EWMA, residual, rare-event, multivariate or change-point logic for the shift and costs of interest. |
| V6 — Signal interpretation | Treat a signal as evidence that the assumed regime changed—not as proof of mechanism, guilt or moral attribution. |
| V7 — Intervention discipline | Investigate mechanism, avoid tampering with common-cause noise, and evaluate interventions against a preserved counterfactual/baseline. |
| V8 — Rebaseline/retirement | Version and justify rebaselining, threshold changes and stale-control retirement; do not erase unfavourable history. |

The surviving SPC property is calibrated discrimination between variation compatible with a local predictive regime and evidence that the regime changed enough to justify investigation or action. Classical chart formulas remain useful under their models; autocorrelation, nonstationarity, low-volume/high-mix streams, changing mixtures, rare events and adaptive feedback require specialised or local models. A signal is not a causal mechanism, and absence of a signal is not proof of absence. [S001–S004, S013–S014, S026, S030–S032, S061–S067, S106–S109]

### Variation-control distinctions

- `within specification != statistically stable`
- `statistically stable != capable`
- `control limits != specifications or tolerances`
- `control-chart signal != proven causal mechanism`
- `no signal != no meaningful defect`
- `special cause != morally or personally attributable cause`
- `common cause != harmless`
- `more run rules != better control when false-alarm and response costs are ignored`

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_EXPERIMENT_DESIGN_MODEL

| Stage | Mature requirement |
| --- | --- |
| E0 — Decision and estimand | State the design alternative, causal contrast or prediction target, consequence threshold and consumer. |
| E1 — Experimental unit | Identify the independently assignable unit and common-source structure; observations within a unit are not new replication. |
| E2 — Measurement fitness | Show the outcome/evaluator can resolve the smallest engineering-relevant effect and remains version-stable. |
| E3 — Assignment/control | Randomise when feasible; otherwise state identification assumptions and use credible controls or mechanistic evidence. |
| E4 — Blocking/stratification | Control known nuisance variation without post-hoc subgroup fishing; preserve randomisation within design constraints. |
| E5 — Factor structure | Use factorial/fractional/RSM structures when interactions or efficient multi-factor learning matter; document aliasing. |
| E6 — Replication | Count independent units/sources, not repeated reads, outputs, folds or same-seed reruns; model clusters/repeated measures. |
| E7 — Multiplicity and stopping | Predefine or use valid adaptive/sequential boundaries; protect confirmation from exploratory flexibility. |
| E8 — Transfer and interference | Assess context, spillover, carryover, attrition, missingness and distribution shift before generalisation. |
| E9 — Decision/confirmation | Report effect, uncertainty, engineering margin, assumptions and sensitivity; confirm optima or high-consequence claims independently. |
| E10 — Proportionality | Prefer the smallest design that separates live alternatives; omit formal DOE when an authoritative deterministic discriminator or exhaustive cheap search suffices. |

Formal DOE is most valuable when several factors, nuisance gradients, interactions, expensive runs or causal ambiguity make informal testing inefficient or uninterpretable. Randomisation protects assignment-based comparisons within its scope; blocking reduces nuisance variation; independent replication supports generalisation/error estimation; factorial structures reveal interactions; fractional designs trade runs for explicit aliasing; and response surfaces support local sequential improvement. None cures an invalid outcome, wrong unit, interference, selection, drift or post-hoc endpoint mutation. [S005–S010, S018, S023, S043–S050]

### Experimental-discrimination distinctions

- `observed association != causal effect`
- `replication count != independent replication`
- `repeated same-seed runs != independent evidence`
- `multiple outputs != multiple experiments`
- `statistical significance != engineering significance`
- `held-out evaluation != causal identification`
- `negative result != equivalence`
- `no detected interaction != no interaction`

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_SEQUENTIAL_EVIDENCE_AND_STOPPING_MODEL

| Element | Mature rule |
| --- | --- |
| Fixed-horizon path | Freeze units, endpoint, analysis and sample size; do not repeatedly inspect and stop under a fixed-horizon calibration. |
| Planned sequential path | Choose SPRT, group-sequential/alpha-spending, confidence-sequence/always-valid or explicit Bayesian decision boundaries appropriate to the model and loss. |
| Information-increment test | Another observation is worth buying only when it has a credible chance of changing the posterior/interval/boundary or the next engineering action after dependence and shared bias are considered. |
| Dependence gate | Repeated measurements sharing unit, seed, batch, evaluator, dataset, reference or selection process contribute less—or no—independent information. |
| Stop for discrimination | Stop when the decision alternatives are separated to the required consequence/uncertainty standard, including equivalence or futility where specified. |
| Stop for negative value | Stop when marginal information value is below cost or when a deterministic authority has already settled the claim. |
| Invalid stop | Quota completion, favourable first crossing, repeated peeking under fixed-horizon rules, endpoint switching, or evaluator mutation after failure does not constitute evidential stopping. |
| Reopen condition | New regime, material drift, changed construct, new failure mechanism or changed consequence can reopen evidence—but requires a new versioned decision contract. |

There is no universal stopping rule for all engineering work. Validity depends on what is adapted, what remains protected, the dependence structure, and the decision loss. Wald-style sequential tests, group-sequential methods, confidence sequences and always-valid inference are planned alternatives to fixed-horizon analysis—not licences to sample until favourable. Regression to the mean, winner’s curse, confirmation loops and repeated benchmark/evaluator reuse must be treated as selection/dependence mechanisms. [S011–S012, S028, S036–S042, S098]

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_ROBUSTNESS_RELIABILITY_AND_TAIL_MODEL

| Claim dimension | Required separation | Properties |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MEAN_PERFORMANCE | Expected/central response under a defined population and use condition. | P035–P036, P050 |
| VARIABILITY | Dispersion across units, runs and nuisance factors; may change independently of the mean. | P004–P007, P043, P050 |
| TAIL_BEHAVIOUR | Quantiles, tolerance content, extremes and catastrophic-event uncertainty; not inferred from the mean alone. | P048, P050 |
| RELIABILITY_OVER_TIME | Survival/failure/degradation across exposure with censoring and competing mechanisms. | P044–P047, P050 |
| ROBUSTNESS_TO_CONTEXT | Performance under specified nuisance, site, user, environment and distribution shifts. | P009, P039, P043, P049–P050 |
| ROBUSTNESS_TO_MODEL_ASSUMPTION | Decision stability under plausible alternative distributions, dependence, transformations and extrapolation models. | P040, P045, P048–P050, P055 |

Reliability evidence requires exposure/time, censoring and failure-mechanism assumptions; zero observed failures yields a bound, not perfection. Accelerated testing is defensible only when stress preserves or correctly models the use-condition failure mechanism. Robust design survives as deliberate insensitivity to nuisance conditions, while universal Taguchi signal-to-noise/array recipes are superseded by designs and models matched to the actual loss, mean, variance, interactions and tails. [S016, S025, S071–S078, S094–S096, S102, S104]

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_PROXY_AND_EVALUATOR_MODEL

| Stage | Governance requirement |
| --- | --- |
| Q0 — Frozen construct contract | Define intended property, scoring rule, threshold, excluded cases and consequence before candidate exposure. |
| Q1 — Separation | Use stable references/bridges to estimate candidate/process variation separately from evaluator/threshold variation. |
| Q2 — Mutation burden | A failing instrument may be changed only on independent evidence of measurement/construct defect, not because the candidate failed. |
| Q3 — Versioned recalibration | Record old/new evaluator, reasons, reference evidence, bridge performance and retrospective impact on all affected conclusions. |
| Q4 — Gaming probes | Search for score-preserving/property-breaking cases, selective exclusions, relabelled failures, post-hoc subgroups and test-set contamination. |
| Q5 — Independent outcome check | Retain at least one outcome/reference not directly optimised by the governed actor when proxy capture is plausible. |
| Q6 — Threshold governance | Guard thresholds with consequence, uncertainty and comparability evidence; prohibit silent ratcheting or relaxation. |
| Q7 — Inconclusive classification | When candidate and evaluator change together, classify the result unresolved until a bridge or independent discriminator separates them. |

When the measurement surface becomes a target, apparent precision can coexist with construct degradation. Goodhart/Campbell mechanisms, undisclosed flexibility, adaptive holdout reuse, benchmark saturation and dataset shift show different routes by which a score stops carrying its original evidence. Legitimate recalibration therefore requires evidence independent of the candidate’s failure, a preserved bridge to the prior evaluator and an impact analysis; otherwise candidate defect, evaluator defect and construct mutation are not identified. [S019, S022, S038–S040, S097, S099]

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_STRIPPING_LEDGER

| Practice or artefact | Underlying property | What it buys | When it fails | Artefact required? | Alternative implementations | Modern disposition | Properties |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Universal p < 0.05 gate | Effect/uncertainty/error control tied to a decision | One conventional false-positive convention inside a valid design | Magnitude, multiplicity, stopping, model or decision loss are omitted | NO | Intervals/equivalence; explicit loss; randomisation inference; Bayesian/sequential methods | REJECTED_AS_UNIVERSAL; conditional tests retained | P036–P038, P058 |
| Control chart for every metric | Variation/change discrimination | Time-ordered visibility and possible early warning | No stable stream, invalid measurement/dependence, no response owner, alert overload | NO | Simple run plot; case review; forecast residual; deterministic check; specialised detector | USE_ONLY_WITH_POSITIVE_DECISION_VALUE | P015–P024, P059 |
| Western Electric rule stack by default | Higher sensitivity to patterns | Shorter detection delay for some shifts | In-control ARL collapses and investigation capacity is exceeded | NO | Targeted CUSUM/EWMA; scenario-calibrated rules; fewer consequence-linked signals | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | P021, P059 |
| Cpk/sigma score on any data | Stable-process margin/yield summary | Compact capability communication | Unstable, nonnormal, dependent or poorly measured process; tail extrapolation | NO | Direct yield/margin; time-dependent model; prediction/tolerance intervals | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | P018–P019 |
| 3.4 DPMO universal target | Context-specific defect/reliability goal | Memorable aspiration | Opportunity/severity/model/shift gaming | NO | Consequence-derived defect/risk target; direct tail evidence | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | P060 |
| DMAIC/DMADV names and tollgates | Evidence sequencing and project coordination | Common language and handoffs | Documents substitute for evidence; forced linearity/overhead | NO | Any risk-proportionate iterative governance with real evidence gates | OPTIONAL_PROGRAMME_ARTEFACT | P056, P061 |
| Belt hierarchy/certification | Demonstrated statistical/domain competence and role capacity | Training, mentoring, protected resources | Credential substitutes for judgement or creates consultant market | NO | Peer-reviewed competence, statistical function, apprenticeship, domain-led teams | OPTIONAL_PROGRAMME_ARTEFACT | P062 |
| Fixed 10×3×3 GR&R and universal percentage cutoff | Decision-specific measurement discrimination | Convenient variance-decomposition template | Wrong range/operators/design; arbitrary cutoff; invalid construct | NO | Crossed/nested agreement, reference comparisons, simulation and loss-based adequacy | USE_ONLY_WHEN_DESIGN_MATCHES | P002, P005, P008, P053, P064 |
| Mandatory normality test | Decision-sensitive model adequacy | Quick check of one distributional aspect | Low/high-n pathologies; misses dependence/tails; encourages pass/fail thinking | NO | Residual/time plots, predictive checks, robust/nonparametric methods, sensitivity | REJECTED_AS_UNIVERSAL | P040, P055, P064 |
| Acceptance sampling as quality programme | Bounded lot-disposition risk | Lower inspection/destruction cost | No process learning; AQL becomes target; lot gaming | NO | 100% reliable inspection; supplier/process evidence; SPC/prevention | DOMAIN_SPECIFIC_AND_NARROWED | P026–P027 |
| Full factorial/DOE for every change | Causal/interaction discrimination | Efficient structured information when ambiguity matters | Known deterministic answer, tiny search space or low-cost obvious change | NO | Direct check; one-factor mechanism test; small pilot; sequential screening | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | P029–P035, P041–P042 |
| Taguchi S/N ratio and orthogonal array recipe | Robustness to nuisance factors | Focuses design on noise insensitivity | Criterion misaligned, information loss, aliasing, no confirmation | NO | Standard factorial/RSM; direct mean–variance–tail modelling; robust optimisation | SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM | P043, P065 |
| Repeated green-run quota | Independent evidence and valid stopping | Can expose variation when runs truly independent | Shared bias/dependence; stop-at-green; test exposure | NO | Effective sample-size plan; fresh confirmation; confidence sequence; deterministic proof | REJECTED_AS_UNIVERSAL | P028, P032, P038, P063 |
| Single composite quality score | Separate mean/variance/tail/time/context claims | Simplifies communication | Weights hide trade-offs and invite gaming | NO | Dimension-specific claims plus explicit consequence model | USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED | P050–P051 |
| Automatic periodic recalibration/rebaseline | Evaluator validity over time | Regular maintenance cadence | No independent evidence; erases failures/comparability | NO | Check standards, trigger-based drift evidence, blind bridge and retrospective impact review | RETAINED_ONLY_WITH_INDEPENDENCE | P010, P052 |
| Permanent legacy control because “we always measured it” | Positive expected decision value | Continuity and defence-in-depth | No consumer, stale threshold, duplicated evidence, alert fatigue | NO | Retire/consolidate/revalidate under risk authority | RETIRE_OR_REVALIDATE | P057 |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_CRITICISM_LEDGER

| ID | Criticism class | Claim | Evidence strength | Property vs misuse disposition | Evolution | Properties | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C001 | MODEL_ASSUMPTION | Classical chart limits are miscalibrated under autocorrelation/nonstationarity. | Strong formal and domain support. | Critique of naive model use, not all monitoring. | REFINED/HYBRIDISED | P016, P022, P024 | S026, S030–S031, S067 |
| C002 | MEASUREMENT_SYSTEM | Measurement error attenuates shift detection and confounds process/evaluator variation. | Strong formal/measurement support. | Critique of unvalidated measurement, not variation discrimination. | PRESERVED_WITH_PRECONDITION | P002, P032, P053 | S015, S032, S055–S058 |
| C003 | CEREMONIAL_IMPLEMENTATION | Control charts become dashboards, blame or automatic adjustment rather than investigation aids. | Historical/management and implementation support; causal quantification heterogeneous. | Mostly misuse, but enabled by oversimplified two-cause rhetoric. | NARROWED | P015, P020–P021, P059 | S031, S066, S089 |
| C004 | MODEL_ASSUMPTION | Rational subgrouping embeds untested causal/process judgements. | Strong theoretical/practice support. | Property retained only with explicit unit/dependence model. | REFINED | P016, P023 | S002, S023, S026 |
| C005 | DECISION_THRESHOLD | Specifications, control limits and capability are routinely conflated. | Strong authoritative guidance. | Misuse; underlying distinctions strongly retained. | PRESERVED | P017–P019 | S053, S062–S064 |
| C006 | CEREMONIAL_IMPLEMENTATION | Run rules raise false alarms and alert burden. | Formal operating-characteristic support. | Not a rejection of supplementary rules; requires cost calibration. | NARROWED | P021, P059 | S014, S065–S066 |
| C007 | DOMAIN_MISAPPLICATION | Acceptance sampling is treated as process improvement or defect-rate proof. | Strong handbook/standard scope evidence. | Technique is domain-specific rather than defective. | NARROWED | P026–P027 | S008, S068–S070 |
| C008 | SAMPLING_DESIGN | Large convenient samples remain biased and duplicated observations do not expand the population evidence. | Very strong sampling theory/domain evidence. | Critique of sampling/independence. | PRESERVED | P025, P028, P032, P063 | S023, S045–S046 |
| C009 | EXPERIMENT_DESIGN | Randomisation does not ensure valid measurement, compliance, no interference or transport. | Very strong causal-design support. | Limits scope; randomisation retained. | NARROWED | P030, P039 | S018, S047–S048 |
| C010 | EXPERIMENT_DESIGN | Pseudo-replication turns repeated observations into false independent evidence. | Strong formal and cross-domain support. | Direct critique of design. | PRESERVED | P032, P063 | S023, S032 |
| C011 | EXPERIMENT_DESIGN | Fractional designs can alias interactions and main effects. | Very strong formal evidence. | Technique retained conditionally. | NARROWED | P034 | S010, S043–S044 |
| C012 | MODEL_ASSUMPTION | Response-surface optima are local/model-dependent and vulnerable to adaptive overfit. | Strong formal/practice support. | RSM retained with confirmation and boundary discipline. | REFINED | P035, P040 | S009, S021, S044 |
| C013 | DECISION_THRESHOLD | Statistical significance is substituted for engineering consequence. | Strong professional/method support. | Critique of misuse and universal thresholding. | REPLACED_AS_DECISION_RULE | P036, P058 | S029, S033–S035, S041–S042 |
| C014 | SAMPLING_DESIGN | Underpowered negative studies are called evidence of no effect. | Strong theory and methodology support. | Requires precision/equivalence, not simply larger n. | REFINED | P028, P036 | S029, S041–S042, S046 |
| C015 | EXPERIMENT_DESIGN | Multiplicity, optional stopping and analyst flexibility inflate false claims. | Very strong formal and empirical support. | Critique of unprotected adaptive practice. | REFINED/HYBRIDISED | P037–P038, P054 | S028, S036–S040 |
| C016 | MEASUREMENT_SYSTEM | Repeatable or inter-rater-consistent instruments may be invalid. | Strong measurement/method evidence. | Reliability retained as precondition, not validity proof. | NARROWED | P001, P005, P008–P009 | S024, S027, S052, S059 |
| C017 | METRIC_GAMING | High-stakes metrics induce relabelling, denominator change and proxy optimisation. | Strong convergent theory/cases; domain causality varies. | Critique of metric governance, not measurement itself. | HYBRIDISED | P051–P052 | S019, S022, S097 |
| C018 | MEASUREMENT_SYSTEM | Gauge R&R can become a form/cutoff ritual detached from the decision range. | Strong conceptual/measurement support; threshold evidence context-specific. | Measurement-system fitness retained; fixed form rejected. | NARROWED | P002, P053, P064 | S055, S058 |
| C019 | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME | Six Sigma may be rigid, resource-intensive, innovation-inhibiting or certification-driven. | Systematic review plus heterogeneous empirical studies. | Critique of programme architecture, not inherited tools. | STILL_CONTESTED/CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | P056, P060–P062 | S082–S087 |
| C020 | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME | Branded programme success evidence is often observational, selected and context-dependent. | Strong methodological concern; some positive association studies. | Programme causal effect remains weaker than component methods. | STILL_CONTESTED | P056 | S085–S087 |
| C021 | MODEL_ASSUMPTION | Taguchi S/N ratios/analyses are inefficient or valid only under special mean–variance relationships. | Strong peer-reviewed criticism/panel evidence. | Robust-design objective retained; universal recipe superseded. | SUPERSEDED | P043, P065 | S025, S095–S096, S102 |
| C022 | RELIABILITY_OR_TAIL | Zero failures and sparse tails support far weaker reliability claims than commonly asserted. | Very strong formal support. | Reliability methods retained with bounds. | NARROWED | P047–P048 | S073–S075 |
| C023 | RELIABILITY_OR_TAIL | Accelerated tests can change failure mechanisms and make extrapolation fragile. | Strong technical/domain support. | ALT retained only with physics/model validation. | NARROWED | P045 | S072, S076–S078 |
| C024 | RELIABILITY_OR_TAIL | Reliability growth trends are confounded by configuration/test-environment/failure-definition changes. | Strong consensus/domain evidence. | Growth retained as intervention-conditioned evidence. | NARROWED | P046 | S078 |
| C025 | MODEL_ASSUMPTION | Normality tests and model checklists are sample-size-sensitive and miss consequential dependence/tails. | Strong statistical-method support. | Model adequacy retained in purpose-specific form. | REPLACED | P040, P055, P064 | S017, S021, S042 |
| C026 | METRIC_GAMING | Fixed holdouts/benchmarks lose independence through adaptive reuse and contamination. | Strong formal and empirical domain support. | Held-out evaluation retained with protection/refresh. | REFINED | P049, P051, P054 | S038–S040, S099 |
| C027 | DECISION_THRESHOLD | Changing evaluator/threshold after candidate failure can make any candidate pass. | Strong measurement-governance logic and adaptive-data/proxy evidence. | Legitimate recalibration retained only with independent bridge evidence. | PRESERVED_WITH_GOVERNANCE | P010, P052–P053 | S015, S038–S039, S053 |
| C028 | ECONOMIC_COST | Excessive measurement can cost more than the uncertainty it removes. | Formal decision/economic-control support; empirical values local. | Critique of overapplication, not evidence collection. | PRESERVED_AS_PROPORTIONALITY | P041–P042, P057 | S014, S098 |
| C029 | DETERMINISTIC_PRECEDENCE | Statistical machinery can obscure an authoritative deterministic discriminator. | Strong logical/engineering boundary; limited direct empirical literature. | No-general-property result for mandatory statistics. | REJECTED_AS_UNIVERSAL | P042, P063–P064 | S079–S080, S098, S110 |
| C030 | INTERNAL_VALIDITY | Missingness, censoring and selective exclusion can erase failures. | Very strong formal/reliability support. | Observation-process modelling retained as precondition. | PRESERVED | P013, P044, P048 | S020, S071, S077 |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_EVOLUTION_UNDER_CRITICISM

| Earlier form | Criticism | Later/evolved response | Disposition | Residual boundary | Properties | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Static Shewhart chart on iid baseline | Autocorrelation, seasonality, changing mixtures and nonstationarity | Residual/time-series, CUSUM/EWMA, local/regime/change-point and time-dependent capability | HYBRIDISED | No detector is assumption-free; calibration and actionable response remain. | P015–P024 | S013, S026, S030–S031, S064, S067 |
| Control signal as cause | Signals do not identify mechanism and can encourage blame | Signal → bounded investigation → mechanism evidence → controlled intervention | NARROWED | “Special cause” remains statistical evidence, not moral attribution. | P015, P020 | S001–S003, S089 |
| Fixed supplementary run rules | False-alarm/alert-capacity costs | Economic/scenario-specific detector design | NARROWED | Sensitivity must be purchased with explicit false-alarm/delay cost. | P021, P059 | S014, S065–S066 |
| Capability as a scalar on any dataset | Instability, nonnormality, measurement error and time dependence | Stability/measurement prerequisite; distribution/time-dependent models; direct yield/uncertainty | NARROWED | Capability remains conditional and local to a regime/horizon. | P018–P019 | S062–S064 |
| Periodic calibration certificate | Calibration does not ensure routine fitness; instruments/evaluators drift | Ongoing measurement assurance, check standards, proficiency and bridge studies | GENERALIZED | Traceability and fitness are separate, continuing properties. | P002–P011 | S015, S054–S060, S105 |
| Repeatability as trust | Repeatable systems can be biased, nonreproducible or invalid | Bias, reproducibility, agreement, invariance and construct validation separated | GENERALIZED | No consistency statistic alone proves validity. | P001, P004–P009 | S024, S027, S052, S057, S059 |
| Randomisation/replication as complete validity | Interference, attrition, carryover, pseudoreplication and transport | Correct unit, cluster/network/crossover design, missingness and context replication | NARROWED | Randomisation remains strongest assignment protection within its actual scope. | P030–P032, P039 | S018, S020, S023, S047–S048 |
| One-factor-at-a-time experimentation | Interactions and inefficient learning | Factorial/fractional/RSM and sequential augmentation | REPLACED_FOR_INTERACTION_PROBLEMS | Formal DOE is unnecessary when a direct discriminator or exhaustive cheap search exists. | P033–P035, P041–P042 | S006, S009–S010, S043–S050 |
| Fixed-horizon-only inference | Peeking and wasted samples | Planned group-sequential, alpha-spending, SPRT, confidence sequences and always-valid inference | GENERALIZED | Outcome/evaluator adaptivity still must respect the protocol. | P038 | S011–S012, S036–S037 |
| Significance-only decisions | Magnitude, practical importance and non-significant≠equivalent | Effect/interval/margin/equivalence plus consequence-aware thresholds | REPLACED_AS_GENERAL_DECISION_RULE | P-values remain conditional diagnostics/tools. | P036, P058 | S029, S033–S035, S041–S042 |
| Unadjusted multiple analysis | False discoveries and undisclosed flexibility | FWER/FDR/hierarchical/selective methods, transparency and protected confirmation | REFINED | Fresh confirmation may be stronger than reconstructing an unknowable adaptive family. | P037, P054 | S028, S038–S040 |
| Taguchi S/N/array recipe | Criterion inefficiency and alias/mean–variance mismatch | Direct robust-factor design with standard DOE, explicit loss and separate mean/variance/tails | SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM | Noise-factor/parameter-design insight survives. | P043, P065 | S025, S095–S096, S102 |
| Zero failures as perfection | Finite exposure only supports an upper bound | Exact/one-sided bounds plus mechanistic, stress and field evidence | REJECTED | Extremely high reliability is rarely established by testing alone. | P047–P048 | S073–S075 |
| ALT as time compression | Stress can change failure mechanism | Physics-informed multi-stress models, use anchors, failure-mode checks and sensitivity | NARROWED | Extrapolation remains assumption-heavy. | P045 | S072, S076–S078 |
| Fixed public holdout as permanent truth | Adaptive reuse/contamination and distribution shift | Protected/reusable holdouts, fresh collections, shift/stress monitoring | HYBRIDISED | Fresh sets trade independence against comparability/collection shift. | P049, P054 | S038–S039, S099 |
| Single target metric | Goodhart/Campbell effects and hidden system trade-offs | Independent construct/outcome checks, non-substitutable dimensions and versioned governance | HYBRIDISED | Metric portfolios can themselves become bureaucracy/gaming surfaces. | P050–P052 | S019, S022, S097 |
| Six Sigma tool menu/brand | Old-tool repackaging, bureaucracy and weak causal attribution | Separate component methods from optional organisational architecture; evidence-proportional statistical engineering | NARROWED/STILL_CONTESTED | Programme effects remain context-dependent and less certain than component mechanisms. | P056, P060–P062 | S082–S087 |
| Permanent control accumulation | Stale thresholds, alert fatigue and negative information value | Lifecycle revalidation, consolidation, retirement and deterministic cheap paths | GENERALIZED | Rare-event defence can justify quiet controls; retirement needs independent risk authority. | P041–P042, P057 | S014, S060, S098 |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_INTERNAL_TENSIONS

| Tension | Side A property | Side B property | Context favouring A | Context favouring B | Known hybrid resolution | Unresolved risk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| More measurement vs measurement cost | P002/P007/P028 | P041/P057 | Near boundary, high consequence, weak measurement discrimination | Wide deterministic margin, low consequence, repeated correlated evidence | Value-of-information gate; dominant-uncertainty analysis; staged measurement | Unknown tail/option value can defeat simple EV calculations. |
| Sensitivity vs false alarms | P021/P024/P048 | P020/P057 | Small costly shifts or rare hazards, adequate response capacity | High alert cost, unstable baseline, low actionability | Scenario-calibrated detector and tiered confirmation | Catastrophic misses and alarm fatigue may resist common utility scale. |
| Fixed thresholds vs adaptive drift | P006/P017/P052 | P009/P010/P022 | Comparability, anti-gaming and regulated requirements | Verified context/construct/evaluator change | Versioned bridge, guard bands and independent recalibration | No external anchor may exist for evolving constructs. |
| Replication vs dependence | P028/P032 | P016/P063 | Independent heterogeneous units are available | Shared seeds, batches, sites, evaluators or repeated units | Hierarchical design/effective sample size plus fresh-source confirmation | Common-mode dependence may be only partly observable. |
| Sequential learning vs optional-stopping abuse | P035/P038/P041 | P037/P054 | High observation cost and valid anytime protocol | Adaptive endpoint/evaluator/model changes outside protocol | Protected outcome/holdout, explicit boundary and stage labels | Co-adaptive candidate/evaluator invalidates simple sequential guarantees. |
| Standardisation vs heterogeneous contexts | P003/P056 | P009/P023/P049 | Cross-site comparability, regulated measurement, repeatable processes | Meaning/calibration/failure modes differ by context | Core invariant definition plus context-specific calibration/strata | Forced invariance can hide local harm; fragmentation can destroy comparability. |
| Process stability vs deliberate experimentation | P018/P020 | P030–P035 | Routine prediction/monitoring and avoiding tampering | Controlled learning where improvement is required | Separate operational baseline from randomised experimental perturbation | Experiment effects may spill into the monitoring baseline. |
| Local metric improvement vs system outcome | P019/P035 | P050/P051/P056 | Metric is causally aligned and independently validated | Multi-objective/tail/downstream trade-offs | System-level outcome checks and non-substitution constraints | Underlying goal may be plural/normative. |
| Model simplicity vs misspecification | P055 | P022/P040/P048 | Transparent model adequate at decision scale | Dependence, tails or nonlinear/extrapolative decision | Start simple; escalate only when diagnostics/sensitivity change action | Several models may agree centrally and diverge in tails. |
| False-positive control vs rare-event sensitivity | P037/P021 | P048 | High investigation/decision cost and many opportunities | Catastrophic miss cost and sparse early warning | Hierarchical/tiered alarms, safety interlocks, conservative bounds | No adequate empirical calibration at extreme rates. |
| Statistical evidence vs deterministic mechanism evidence | P029–P041 | P042 | No exact mechanism/oracle; stochastic population inference needed | Authoritative proof, exhaustive check or direct physical discriminator | Deterministic first, statistics for residual uncertainty/transfer/reliability | Claimed oracle may be incomplete, stale or proxy-captured. |
| Robust general rule vs context tuning | P043/P056 | P023/P049 | Stable broad nuisance distribution and shared loss | Local populations/failure mechanisms materially differ | Hierarchical/partial pooling and context-specific guardrails | Adaptation can erode global comparability and invite gaming. |
| Precision vs validity | P004/P005/P007 | P001/P009/P051 | Measurand is fixed and method validity established | Construct/proxy/transport is uncertain | Validity gate before precision optimisation; independent outcome checks | Construct validity may remain contested without a gold standard. |
| Automation vs independent construct judgement | P006/P054 | P001/P008/P052 | High-volume stable construct and calibrated evaluator | Novel/ambiguous/adversarial cases or evaluator drift | Automated screen plus protected human/reference adjudication | Humans and automated evaluators can share bias/training sources. |
| Capability target vs innovation/discovery | P019/P060 | P035/P056 | Mature repetitive process and stable customer requirement | New design space, uncertain construct or changing market | Separate exploitation control from exploration experiment portfolio | Management programmes can reward one mode and suppress the other. |
| Transparency vs holdout protection | P012/P037 | P054 | Reproducibility, audit and public scrutiny | Adaptive optimisation would exploit disclosed confirmation set | Transparent method/provenance with controlled data access and later release | Secrecy can hide bias; disclosure can destroy independence. |
| Reference stability vs reference representativeness | P010/P011 | P009/P049 | Longitudinal comparability and drift detection | Population/technology changes make old references stale | Bridge old and new reference panels with overlapping anchors | No fixed panel can be both permanent and indefinitely representative. |
| Control retirement vs defence in depth | P057/P041 | P047/P048 | Control has no consumer/value and cheaper discriminator exists | Rare catastrophic failure, legal/safety requirement, weak incident frequency | Risk-authorised retirement with alternate coverage and sunset monitoring | Absence of catches may mean success or uselessness; observationally ambiguous. |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_HYBRIDISATION_PRESSURES

| Pressure | Why hybridisation is needed | Properties | Informational relation | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Measurement science ↔ SPC | Control limits and capability are invalid when evaluator variation/drift dominates. | P002, P010, P015–P019, P053 | MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE + QUALITY_ANCESTRY | S015, S032, S055–S058 |
| DOE ↔ measurement science | Randomisation/replication cannot rescue an outcome unable to resolve the target effect. | P002, P006–P008, P029–P036 | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE + MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE | S005–S007, S015, S043–S058 |
| SPC ↔ time-series/change detection | Classical iid charts evolve under autocorrelation/nonstationarity but retain signal/cost/response discipline. | P015–P024 | QUALITY_ANCESTRY + HYBRID_RESOLUTION | S013, S026, S030–S031, S064, S067 |
| Reliability ↔ DOE/robust design | Stress/noise-factor experiments estimate time/context failure only under physical continuity and censored-data models. | P043–P048 | RELIABILITY_NATIVE + EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_NATIVE | S025, S049–S050, S071–S078 |
| Sequential theory ↔ online experimentation | Always-valid methods translate formal sequential ideas to continuous A/B monitoring. | P038 | SHARED_STATISTICAL_ANCESTRY + DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | S011–S012, S036–S037 |
| Multiplicity ↔ adaptive evaluation | Reusable holdouts/confirmation boundaries respond to analysis and benchmark adaptivity. | P037, P054 | REACTION_OR_CORRECTION + DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | S028, S038–S040 |
| Metrology ↔ conformity decisions | Uncertainty and guard bands connect measurement capability to false accept/reject action. | P006–P007, P017, P052 | MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE + HYBRID_RESOLUTION | S051–S053, S100, S103 |
| Proxy governance ↔ measurement invariance | Optimisation and context shift can mutate what a stable score means. | P009–P011, P049–P052 | CONVERGENT_PROPERTY + MEASUREMENT_SCIENCE_NATIVE | S019, S022, S027, S039, S097, S099 |
| Six Sigma ↔ statistical engineering | Six Sigma contributes optional organisational architecture; broader statistical engineering integrates methods beyond the brand. | P056, P060–P062 | MANAGEMENT_PROGRAMME_TRANSLATION + QUALITY_ENGINEERING_GENERALISATION | S079–S087 |
| Evidence economy ↔ deterministic precedence | Decision analysis supplies the stopping/cheap-path boundary; exact engineering evidence can dominate sampling. | P041–P042, P057 | CONVERGENT_PROPERTY | S014, S079–S080, S098 |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_STRONGEST_SURVIVING_PROPERTIES

| ID | Property | Status | Mature form | Cheap/non-trigger path | Primary sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P001 | Define the decision-relevant measurand or construct before quantification | STRONGLY_RETAINED | No metric governs until its intended property, scope, consumer, and admissible interpretation are fixed enough to be challenged. | When an authoritative deterministic fact directly answers the decision, record that fact rather than inventing a surrogate score. | S052, S055, S056 |
| P002 | Establish measurement-system fitness before metric governance | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Demonstrate enough discrimination for the intended decision, not abstract “good measurement,” and recheck after material evaluator change. | For a deterministic binary check with an authoritative oracle and negligible ambiguity, verify the oracle and use it directly rather than perform full MSA. | S015, S055, S058 |
| P003 | Calibration and metrological traceability where decision-relevant | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Require the level of traceability needed for comparability and decision risk, alongside—not instead of—fitness validation. | For purely ordinal/local decisions with a verified internal reference and no cross-context claim, full national/international traceability may add no value. | S052, S054, S056 |
| P004 | Keep bias/accuracy distinct from precision | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Decision-specific decomposition of systematic and random error, with correction only when validated. | If the decision is invariant to a known constant offset and only ranks within one stable system, bias correction may be unnecessary—but must be explicit. | S015, S052, S057 |
| P005 | Distinguish repeatability, reproducibility and validity | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Claim only the level of stability actually tested, and never infer validity from repeatability alone. | For one-use, one-instrument local control with no transfer claim and a wide margin, a limited stability check may suffice. | S057, S058, S059 |
| P006 | Match resolution, sensitivity, specificity and threshold discrimination to the decision | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Use the cheapest discriminator whose error profile is adequate for the decision and freeze/version its threshold unless independent recalibration evidence exists. | When a deterministic authoritative predicate directly distinguishes states, use it instead of estimating sensitivity/specificity. | S052, S053, S103 |
| P007 | Carry measurement uncertainty into engineering decisions | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Use proportionate uncertainty analysis sufficient to show whether the next action is discriminated. | When the result is far from every relevant boundary and a conservative worst-case bound suffices, a detailed budget may be unnecessary. | S051, S053, S100 |
| P009 | Test measurement invariance and transport across contexts | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Claim equivalence only over tested contexts; otherwise maintain context-specific calibration or explicit uncertainty. | No invariance study is needed for an explicitly local, one-time decision that makes no comparison beyond its validated context. | S027, S057, S099 |
| P010 | Monitor evaluator/instrument drift and require independent recalibration evidence | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Recalibrate when independent evidence shows measurement defect or context change; preserve old/new bridge and re-evaluate prior conclusions where material. | For a one-shot destructive test with contemporaneous references, ongoing drift monitoring may be impossible; preserve calibration evidence and uncertainty instead. | S015, S060, S056 |
| P012 | Preserve data provenance, preprocessing, version and lineage | STRONGLY_RETAINED | Preserve the minimum complete lineage needed to reconstruct the evidence and its dependence structure. | A small manually inspectable deterministic dataset may need only a concise signed record, not a full data platform. | S056, S038, S110 |
| P013 | Model missingness, censoring, exclusions and selection mechanisms | MEASUREMENT_PRECONDITION_PROPERTY | Make the observation/selection process auditable and report conclusions conditional on or robust to plausible missingness mechanisms. | If missingness is demonstrably negligible and unrelated to outcome/exposure, transparent complete-case treatment may be adequate. | S020, S071, S077 |
| P015 | Discriminate routine/common variation from evidence of a changed process | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Use the lightest calibrated discriminator that separates expected variation from actionable change, then require causal investigation before intervention. | For a deterministic failure with an authoritative cause, repair it directly; for one-off heterogeneous work with no repeatable unit, causal case analysis may dominate charting. | S001, S002, S003 |
| P016 | Choose rational subgroups and model dependence rather than count correlated observations as sample size | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Match the unit of evidence and correlation model to the unit of action; disclose residual dependence. | When observations are demonstrably independent and identically produced at the decision scale, ordinary independent analysis is adequate. | S002, S023, S026 |
| P017 | Keep statistical control limits separate from engineering specifications and tolerances | STRONGLY_RETAINED | Use control limits for change detection, specifications for requirements, and guard bands/decision rules for uncertain conformance. | For an exact deterministic requirement checked once, a control chart may be unnecessary; use the specification directly with measurement uncertainty. | S001, S053, S062 |
| P018 | Establish process stability before interpreting capability or long-run prediction | STRONGLY_RETAINED | State the regime and prediction horizon; no unconditional capability claim from unresolved drift/mixture. | For a deterministic design proof or 100% authoritative inspection, a statistical capability index may be irrelevant. | S002, S062, S109 |
| P020 | Prevent tampering and overadjustment to routine variation | STRONGLY_RETAINED | No reactive adjustment without a discriminator or planned experiment whose expected value exceeds intervention risk. | Correct an authoritative deterministic defect immediately; no statistical wait is needed when mechanism/effect is known. | S002, S003, S089 |
| P025 | Design sampling to represent the decision population and selection process | STRONGLY_RETAINED | Use the cheapest design that either represents the target or honestly limits the claim; retain all attempted-unit provenance. | For exhaustive authoritative enumeration of the entire relevant population, statistical sampling is unnecessary; verify completeness/identity instead. | S045, S020, S068 |
| P029 | Frame experiments around an explicit engineering decision, estimand and rival alternatives | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY | No experiment begins as “run a test”; it begins with the decision it can change and the cheapest evidence that discriminates it. | For an exact authoritative check or exploratory feasibility probe, a formal estimand/test may be unnecessary; label the evidence accordingly. | S005, S007, S018 |
| P030 | Use randomisation to protect causal comparisons against assignment bias and support valid error assessment | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY | Randomise when it materially reduces causal ambiguity; preserve assignment integrity and analyse the design actually run. | Randomisation is unnecessary for deterministic mechanism verification and may be infeasible/unethical; strong natural/quasi-experimental or mechanistic evidence can be used with narrower claims. | S005, S006, S018 |
| P031 | Block or stratify known nuisance variation without erasing interactions | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY | Block what is known and costly, randomise what remains, and report context interactions rather than only an adjusted average. | Do not block on post-treatment variables or add complexity when units are already homogeneous and the effect is obvious. | S005, S006, S043 |
| P032 | Require independent replication at the experimental-unit level; treat technical repeats as measurement information | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY | Count independent decision-relevant units, disclose dependence, and stop treating correlated reruns as corroboration. | For deterministic proof or exact exhaustive checks, replication is unnecessary; for measurement precision, technical repeats remain useful but answer a different question. | S005, S006, S023 |
| P033 | Use factorial structure to discover interactions and avoid one-factor-at-a-time ambiguity | EXPERIMENTAL_DESIGN_PROPERTY | Use factorial structure when interaction ambiguity is decision-relevant; confirm in the intended operating region. | For one known dominant factor, deterministic mechanism or a tiny safe search space that can be exhaustively tested, formal factorial DOE may be excessive. | S006, S043, S049 |
| P036 | Report effect magnitude, uncertainty and engineering significance; use equivalence/noninferiority when “no important difference” is the decision | STRONGLY_RETAINED | Show effect, uncertainty, smallest consequential magnitude, and decision; reserve “equivalent” for designs that test a justified margin. | For deterministic threshold compliance or an overwhelming effect far from uncertainty, a p-value may add nothing. | S007, S029, S041 |
| P037 | Control multiplicity, analyst degrees of freedom and selective inference across the actual family of opportunities | STRONGLY_RETAINED | Protect the claim-generating opportunity set, not merely the final table; use fresh confirmation when adaptivity is complex. | When one authoritative predeclared deterministic discriminator exists, multiplicity machinery is unnecessary; report any exploratory alternatives separately. | S028, S038, S040 |
| P038 | Plan sequential evidence and stopping so repeated looks do not manufacture confidence | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Another observation is justified only when it is expected to change the decision and the stopping/error framework remains valid. | Stop immediately when an authoritative deterministic discriminator settles the decision; no statistical stopping machinery is needed. | S011, S012, S036, S037 |
| P040 | Use robustness and sensitivity analysis to expose dependence on model, confounding and nuisance assumptions | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Challenge the assumptions capable of changing the decision; do not confuse robust-to-one-model feature with universal validity. | When a deterministic mechanism/proof settles the decision, statistical sensitivity may be redundant; verify mechanism assumptions instead. | S016, S017, S021 |
| P041 | Choose experiments and measurements by expected information or decision value relative to cost | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Ask whether the next observation is independently informative enough to change action; choose the cheapest valid discriminator. | Take the direct authoritative fact immediately when cost is negligible; do not build a probabilistic model merely to justify an obvious check. | S014, S098, S009 |
| P042 | Give precedence to an authoritative deterministic discriminator when it settles the decision | STRONGLY_RETAINED | First ask whether the decision is already logically or physically discriminated; if yes, stop statistical collection and document the decisive evidence. | Do not invoke this property when the “deterministic” rule is an unvalidated proxy, incomplete oracle, stale reference or only one sample from a stochastic population. | S098, S079, S110 |
| P051 | Treat optimised metrics as vulnerable proxies and monitor for gaming/surrogation | USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED | Use metrics as defeasible sensors: preserve an independent construct/outcome path and investigate divergence under optimisation. | For a direct authoritative physical/logical property that cannot be cheaply manipulated, proxy safeguards may be minimal; maintain authority/integrity checks. | S019, S022, S097 |
| P052 | Require independent, prospective justification before changing an evaluator, fixture, expected answer or threshold after failure | STRONGLY_RETAINED | Change only on independent evidence that the measurement/construct—not merely the candidate—is defective or context has legitimately changed. | Correct a provable transcription/specification error immediately, preserving evidence and authority; no large statistical study is needed. | S015, S053, S060 |
| P053 | Separate candidate/process variation from evaluator/measurement variation before assigning corrective action | STRONGLY_RETAINED | Hold one side fixed while testing the other against independent references; leave unresolved when evidence cannot distinguish. | If a direct inspection proves a deterministic candidate defect or evaluator bug, repair that source immediately and document it. | S058, S032, S024 |
| P055 | Check model adequacy and misspecification against the decision, not through ritual assumption tests | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Use the simplest adequate model and report which guarantees are conditional; switch/robustify when a departure changes the decision. | For deterministic checks or methods robust by design to the relevant deviation, skip irrelevant normality tests and document why. | S017, S021, S110 |
| P056 | Integrate multiple statistical and engineering methods into a system-level evidence strategy | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | A problem-structuring and integration property: right evidence, right order, right scale, with assumptions and consumer decisions explicit. | For a well-structured local question solved by one authoritative method/check, use that method; system-level statistical engineering would be overhead. | S079, S080, S081 |
| P057 | Make measurement and controls economically proportional; retire, revalidate or recalibrate stale controls | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Every recurring control has a current decision, validity evidence and proportional cost; otherwise simplify, replace, revalidate or retire with risk sign-off. | Keep cheap high-value controls and legal/safety requirements even if failures are rare; deterministic checks may remain near-zero cost. | S002, S014, S098 |

These properties survive because they regulate an identifiable evidence failure rather than prescribe a branded artefact. Their trigger remains conditional: even a strongly retained principle can be satisfied by a small direct check rather than a formal statistical project.

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_CONTEXT_SPECIFIC_PROPERTIES

| ID | Property | Status | Trigger | Assumptions | Failure if generalised |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P014 | Account for measurement intervention, destruction and contamination | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Destructive material tests, accelerated stress, human/agent learning, repeated benchmark exposure, cache/warm-up effects. | Carryover/washout model is plausible or fresh independent units are available. | Calling warmed/cached retries independent; destructive test sample not representative; training on failed benchmark items; inadequate washout. |
| P016 | Choose rational subgroups and model dependence rather than count correlated observations as sample size | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Batch, lot, repeated-run, time-series, nested, clustered or same-seed/same-environment observations. | Within-subgroup homogeneity and between-subgroup opportunity for relevant change, or a valid dependence model. | Convenience subgroups; averaging across shifts; treating multiple outputs from one run as independent; one cluster per condition. |
| P019 | Use capability indices only conditionally on stability, distribution and measurement fitness | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | High-volume repeatable processes where a stable distribution and specification-based decision exist. | Selected index/distribution describes the regime and tail behaviour; sample size supports intended precision. | Cpk on unstable data; normal DPMO conversion under skew/dependence; ignoring confidence intervals; choosing the most favourable index. |
| P021 | Design monitoring economically around false-alarm, missed-shift and delay costs | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Ongoing monitoring with repeated opportunities, finite response capacity and quantifiable consequences. | Operating-characteristic calculations approximate real dependence/nonstationarity and cost model includes tail consequences. | Stacking Western Electric rules without capacity; tuning on known incidents; no alert disposition; sampling quota detached from value. |
| P023 | Stratify mixtures, high-mix/low-volume and changing populations before aggregation | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | High-mix low-volume production, heterogeneous workloads, changing severity/population or sparse per-type data. | Within-stratum models or standardisation preserve relevant tail/interaction behaviour; no hidden confounding remains. | Dropping hard categories; changing denominator; z-scoring incomparable tails; one global control limit; post-hoc subgroup hunting. |
| P024 | Use context-adapted multivariate, rare-event, short-run or change-point monitoring only when its added discrimination pays | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Multivariate correlated measurements, rare defects, variable exposure, small shifts, low volume or abrupt change points. | Selected detector’s null/change model or robust calibration approximates use; multiplicity across monitored features is controlled. | Hotelling score without diagnosis; Poisson chart under overdispersion; zero-event reassurance; model retrained after every alarm. |
| P026 | Use acceptance sampling only for bounded lot-disposition decisions where inspection cost or destruction justifies it | DOMAIN_SPECIFIC | Incoming/outgoing lots with meaningful lot identity, relatively stable supply and costly/destructive inspection. | Within-lot heterogeneity and production history are compatible with the plan; standard’s switching rules/context apply. | AQL interpreted as a guarantee; nonrandom sample; lot splitting; plan chosen after results; inspection error ignored. |
| P028 | Size samples around effect/precision, dependence and decision loss—not a universal count | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Sampling/experiment/reliability decisions where uncertainty influences action. | Planning model approximates design; effect threshold is engineering-justified; clusters/dependence known enough. | Counting repeated readings as n; planning on observed pilot effect; stopping when significant; ignoring multiplicity/attrition. |
| P034 | Use fractional factorial/screening designs only with explicit alias and sparsity assumptions | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Early screening with many candidate factors, limited runs and a credible effect-sparsity/hierarchy hypothesis. | Few active effects, high-order interactions negligible or follow-up can resolve ambiguity. | Resolution III main effects treated as clean; no foldover; factors dropped before interaction check; no confirmation. |
| P039 | Bound causal claims by external validity, interference, carryover and order effects | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Networked/shared-resource systems, sequential treatments, adaptive users, multi-site deployment or any generalisation claim. | No hidden interference/carryover beyond the specified structure; sampled contexts cover intended use or extrapolation is bounded. | Users share treatments; same system state across arms; inadequate washout; one site; aggregate effect hides vulnerable context. |
| P040 | Use robustness and sensitivity analysis to expose dependence on model, confounding and nuisance assumptions | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Small samples, heavy tails, observational comparisons, extrapolation, complex models or high-consequence decisions. | Perturbation set spans credible alternatives rather than only convenient ones; robust method’s loss/neighbourhood matches consequence. | Only normality test; deleting “outliers” post hoc; robust estimator treated as assumption-free; sensitivity range chosen to preserve result. |
| P041 | Choose experiments and measurements by expected information or decision value relative to cost | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Costly/slow/destructive measurement, adaptive experimentation, competing tests or staged engineering decisions. | Utility/probability model or qualitative ranking is sufficiently credible; option value and irreversible harm are considered. | “More data” default; full factorial despite deterministic discriminator; no action change after result; cost omitted; catastrophic risk monetised casually. |
| P044 | Model reliability over time with explicit failure definitions, exposure, censoring and degradation | DOMAIN_SPECIFIC | Components/systems where ageing, wear, cumulative use or time-dependent failure changes the decision. | Independent/appropriately clustered lifetimes, censoring mechanism acceptable, distribution/regression model adequate over prediction horizon. | Removing early failures; right-censored units treated as nonfailures forever; mixed modes pooled; uptime denominator changed; threshold drift. |
| P045 | Use accelerated testing only with validated stress-to-use mechanisms and explicit extrapolation uncertainty | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Long-lived products where timely direct life testing is infeasible and a credible acceleration mechanism exists. | Same relevant failure mechanisms and adequate acceleration/life distributions across tested-to-use range. | One stress level; no use anchor; pooled failure modes; extrapolation without interval; treating high-stress pass as field proof. |
| P046 | Treat reliability growth as intervention-conditioned evidence, not an automatically improving trend | DOMAIN_SPECIFIC | Development programmes with repeated failures and deliberate corrective modifications. | Model form approximates the repair/improvement process; changes other than intended fixes are documented. | Test difficulty decreases; failures reclassified; exposure resets; fixes not independently verified; one programme curve pooled across modes. |
| P047 | Translate zero observed failures into an upper risk bound, never “perfect reliability” | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Reliability/safety claims based on zero failures or no detected rare defects. | Bernoulli/Poisson/exposure model and homogeneity or conservative stratification are adequate. | Counting repeated same-condition trials as independent; optional stopping after a clean run; changing failure definition; hiding near misses. |
| P048 | Treat rare-event and tail claims as distinct, data-hungry and model-sensitive | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | High-consequence low-frequency failures, tight population-content guarantees or asymmetric loss. | Tail family/extrapolation and dependence are adequate; stress sampling is correctly reweighted; unobserved modes acknowledged. | Normal six-sigma extrapolation; dropping outliers; zero events called zero risk; tail metric tuned after incident; pooled modes. |
| P051 | Treat optimised metrics as vulnerable proxies and monitor for gaming/surrogation | USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED | High-stakes target metrics, leaderboards, incentives, automated optimisation or repeated candidate exposure. | A plausible behavioural/optimisation mechanism links targeting to metric degradation; independent checks are not co-optimised. | Dropping hard cases; changing denominator; teaching to test; benchmark leakage; proxy chosen for availability; outcome not independently checked. |
| P055 | Check model adequacy and misspecification against the decision, not through ritual assumption tests | ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE | Any inferential/control/reliability method whose operating characteristics depend materially on distribution, independence, link or functional form. | Diagnostics have power for material deviations; alternative models are plausible and not chosen only for desired results. | Mandatory Shapiro–Wilk; accepting model because p>.05; residuals not time-ordered; transformation after seeing desired significance; no out-of-sample check. |
| P057 | Make measurement and controls economically proportional; retire, revalidate or recalibrate stale controls | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Long-lived quality systems, recurring tests, duplicated reviews, low-yield monitoring or changed process/evaluator context. | Past yield predicts some future value or scenario analysis captures uncertainty; retirement does not violate unmodelled catastrophic safeguards. | Quota retained with no consumer; threshold never revalidated; duplicated tests treated as corroboration; control removed because it catches failures. |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_REJECTED_OR_SUPERSEDED_PRACTICES

| ID | Candidate practice | Status | What survives | Why the form is not general | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P058 | Universal p < 0.05 threshold as an engineering decision rule | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Use error thresholds only when tied to a declared family, effect/decision and consequence; otherwise report continuous evidence and uncertainty. | The universal rule is ceremony/rejected; calibrated hypothesis tests remain conditional tools. | S033, S034, S035; critical: S007, S042 |
| P059 | Control-chart-everything and automatic run-rule escalation | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | Monitor only where the stream, signal, cost and response are defensible; otherwise choose a cheaper direct/qualitative method. | The chart/tool is ceremony when detached from a decision; variation discrimination is the property. | S001, S061, S107; critical: S030, S031, S066 |
| P060 | Universal 3.4 DPMO or “six-sigma level” as the definition of quality | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | Set targets from customer/safety/economic consequences and observed/modelled risk, not brand numerology. | 3.4 DPMO and “sigma level” are programme rhetoric/conditional conversion, not a surviving general property. | S082, S062, S063; critical: S084, S031, S048 |
| P061 | DMAIC/DMADV phase names and tollgates as a general statistical property | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | Retain evidence dependencies and governance where useful; strip mandatory branding and tailor path to problem risk/uncertainty. | Phase names/tollgates are artefacts, not general properties. | S082, S083, S091; critical: S084, S087 |
| P062 | Belt hierarchy and certification as a general engineering property | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | Use role/competence systems proportionate to need and assess actual decisions/work products; no required belt taxonomy. | Belt titles/certificates are programme artefacts. | S082, S085, S086; critical: S084, S087 |
| P063 | More samples, more reruns or repeated green tests automatically mean more truth | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Another observation earns weight only through identifiable independent/conditional information and positive decision value. | Raw run count and streaks are not properties. | S023, S028, S032, S038; critical: S040, S042 |
| P064 | Mandatory normality tests, fixed gauge R&R cutoffs and full MSA paperwork for every metric | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | Use the smallest diagnostic/MSA design that can falsify decision adequacy. | The mandatory form/cutoff is ceremony; measurement fitness/model adequacy are retained properties. | S055, S058, S110; critical: S032, S042 |
| P065 | Universal Taguchi signal-to-noise ratios and orthogonal-array recipes as the definition of robust design | SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM | Model the actual mean, variance, tails and noise-factor interactions; use a Taguchi criterion only when demonstrably appropriate. | Universal S/N/array recipe is superseded; robust design remains retained in evolved form. | S094, S095, S096; critical: S025, S102 |

# EVOLVED_STATISTICAL_ENGINEERING_OPEN_QUESTIONS

| ID | Question | Family | Properties | Evidence limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OQ01 | How can evaluator fitness be established for generative or partly subjective systems when no stable gold standard exists? | Measurement/evaluator validity | P001–P011, P052–P054 | UNRESOLVED: triangulation and bridge designs help, but construct authority remains external. |
| OQ02 | What is an adequate independence model for stochastic software/agents sharing training data, code, prompts and evaluators? | Replication/dependence | P016, P032, P063 | UNRESOLVED: hierarchical/common-source models are plausible but empirical dependence is often hidden. |
| OQ03 | How should co-adaptive candidate and evaluator changes preserve sequential validity? | Sequential evidence | P038, P052–P054 | UNRESOLVED: classical anytime-valid guarantees generally assume a fixed target/outcome process or controlled adaptation. |
| OQ04 | What evidence justifies changing expected answers when the construct itself evolves? | Construct/evaluator mutation | P001, P009–P010, P052 | UNRESOLVED: independent authority and old/new bridge are necessary but may not identify one correct construct. |
| OQ05 | How much fresh confirmation is necessary after highly adaptive optimisation? | Adaptive experimentation | P035, P037–P038, P054 | CONTESTED/DOMAIN_SPECIFIC. |
| OQ06 | How can rare catastrophic rates be bounded when direct samples are infeasible and stress models are uncertain? | Reliability/tails | P045, P047–P049 | UNRESOLVED: requires combined mechanistic, stress, operational and conservative evidence without false numerical synthesis. |
| OQ07 | When does a local dynamic baseline provide enough “stability” for useful capability in adaptive systems? | Nonstationarity/capability | P018–P024 | CONTESTED and model-dependent. |
| OQ08 | How should value-of-information models incorporate deep uncertainty, irreversibility and discovery option value? | Evidence economy | P041, P048, P057 | UNRESOLVED: expected utility alone may be inadequate. |
| OQ09 | How can reference sets be refreshed without losing longitudinal comparability or becoming optimisation targets? | Reference governance | P010–P011, P049, P054 | UNRESOLVED: overlapping bridge panels and protected access are partial solutions. |
| OQ10 | What monitoring designs work reliably for low-volume, high-mix and rapidly changing software/knowledge work? | Domain transfer | P022–P024 | EVIDENCE LIMITED: strong general cautions, heterogeneous direct evidence. |
| OQ11 | Which Six Sigma organisational practices have causal benefits independent of leadership, selection and resources? | Programme effects | P056, P061–P062 | UNRESOLVED/MIXED: positive observational studies coexist with weak/heterogeneous reviews. |
| OQ12 | Which Taguchi-specific criteria remain optimal for clearly defined loss and mean–variance relationships? | Robust design | P043, P065 | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT: universal claims rejected, some special cases retained. |
| OQ13 | How should multiple non-substitutable metrics be governed without creating multiplicity and dashboard bureaucracy? | Proxy/system metrics | P037, P050–P051, P057 | UNRESOLVED organisational/statistical design problem. |
| OQ14 | When can mechanistic evidence substitute for independent system-level replication? | Evidence synthesis | P032, P040, P042, P047–P048 | DOMAIN_SPECIFIC and consequence-sensitive. |
| OQ15 | How should stale-control retirement be distinguished from gaming away inconvenient tests? | Control lifecycle | P052, P057 | Requires independent risk authority and alternate-coverage evidence; no universal statistic. |
| OQ16 | How should measurement uncertainty include learned-evaluator retraining and model-selection uncertainty without false precision? | Measurement uncertainty | P007, P010, P052–P055 | UNRESOLVED; conventional budgets often underrepresent structural uncertainty. |
| OQ17 | What is the right unit of evidence when interventions alter shared system state for all subsequent units? | Interference/carryover | P014, P030–P032, P039 | Requires explicit exposure mapping; often domain-specific. |
| OQ18 | Can statistical engineering be empirically distinguished from excellent systems engineering that uses statistics? | Discipline identity | P056 | UNRESOLVED; component mechanisms are much better evidenced than unique discipline-wide effect. |
| OQ19 | How should practical significance combine average benefit, tail harm and distributional fairness? | Decision thresholds | P036, P048, P050 | Partly normative; no universal scalar. |
| OQ20 | What evidence is enough to call a deterministic discriminator complete and authoritative in an evolving system? | Deterministic precedence | P042, P052, P057 | UNRESOLVED governance boundary; identity, scope and staleness must be re-established. |

No property remains undispositioned at freeze. These questions bound transfer, construct authority, dependence visibility, extreme-tail inference and programme causality; they do not reopen the 65-property population.
